- Pricing is costed, not estimated: `src/lib/domain/catalogue.ts` holds the chosen products at supplier cost (AWM Clayton price list: Jinko 475 W panel, Sungrow string inverters for solar-only, Sigenergy SigenStor controller + 8 kWh modules for batteries, Antai rail and tin/tile kits). `src/lib/domain/costing.ts` builds the bill of materials by rule: inverter = smallest with array ≤ 133% of its rating (150% for a hybrid with a battery, `arrayRatio`); rail = (panel width + 0.1 m) × 2 per portrait panel, plus a 10% buffer on flat roofs (to bridge arrays) or 5% otherwise, in 4.8 m lengths; arrays are allowed for as 2, or 3 above 18 panels (+$150 third-array installation); one tin/tile kit per 2 kW (roof "not sure" → tile); flat roofs always get the tin kit plus Clenergy universal Kliplok interfaces (CLNER-I-34, 2 per panel + 2 per array), since most are Kliplok, and ask "How would you like your panels?": laid flat (default; sized with no tilt gain so a little larger) or tilted (adds an Antai 10–15° tilt kit per 2 kW plus $15/panel installation; tilting depends on roof space and looks, both checked on the call, with one short note, not paragraphs); BOS = DC label kit, battery label kit (when there's a battery), 10 MC4 pairs, 4 panel clips per panel in packs of 100, and an AC isolator sized to the inverter (`acIsolatorFor`): NHP 40 A 2-pole single phase / 3-pole three phase while the inverter's output is ≤ 32 A a phase, a 63 A for bigger single-phase inverters (8 kW and up; the 63 A is a stand-in 3-pole until a 63 A 2-pole is on the price list); solar install 30c/W (+$400 double storey); battery install $1,800 per stack with modules beyond six at $1,800 ÷ 6 each; EV charger install $1,000; +$150 only when a three-phase inverter is fitted. Install costs are ex GST. Then +20% margin, +GST. "About your home" asks roof, storeys and phase (phase "not sure" → single), one question per card (`QuestionCard` in the profile page, also the existing-solar questions and the yes/no questions; no card holds two questions). Add-ons without a confirmed price (`price: null` in `ADD_ONS`: until costed, Surge Protection (`surge-protection`, "Protect my appliances from power surges"; it replaced Smart Switchboard; Smart Home Integration was removed: too vague to sell); "Blackout Backup" (`home-backup`, `needsBattery`) is a priced extra: wiring backup circuits to the battery, `COSTING.backupCircuitsInstall` $1,000 ex GST installation (the gateway is already in every battery), offered and priced only with a battery; the battery sheet explains it (`backupNote`) and the circuits are agreed on the call. Every price carries `PRICE_INCLUDES` ("Includes installation, the inverter, electrical work, commissioning and GST"); the system step shows the panel wattage ("7.1 kW · 475 W panels"), the battery maker, and "Panels we use" / "Batteries we use" lists (maker · size · warranty) from `PANEL_RANGE`/`BATTERY_RANGE` in `catalogue.ts` (add up to three panels as they're priced; warranties marked VERIFY until checked on the datasheets); the exact equipment for the home is chosen in the design and shown on the call) are "discuss on my call": no price, not in the total, listed in the basket, HubSpot note and email. Home electrification (reverse-cycle heating & cooling, heat pump hot water) is `comingSoon`: a "Coming soon" badge on the Extras step, "Let me know when it's available" (`price.interested`: not priced, not discussed on the call, listed as "Coming soon" in the basket and email and "Interested (coming soon)" in HubSpot). Only give an add-on a price once its supply and install are confirmed. Heat pumps will need their own rebates (federal STCs, Victorian VEECs, Solar Victoria hot water rebate) before they're sold.
- Supplier price imports (`/admin` "Supplier prices"; `price-import.ts`, tested against the real AWM list in `__fixtures__/awm-price-list.xlsx`): staff upload an Excel (`server/xlsx.ts`, no library) or CSV price list with the supplier's name; rows are matched to catalogue SKUs (`catalogueEntries`, prices ex GST, AWM's "26.0c/w ($123.50)" read as $123.50), the changes shown (≥ 30% in amber) and nothing changes until "Apply". Every upload is kept (`price_uploads`: supplier, file, who, when, changes, applied/discarded by whom and when) and shown as the upload history. Applied prices live in `price_overrides`, served by `/api/prices`, fetched by the flow (`state.prices`) and used by `billOfMaterials` (`CostingInput.costs`; a retailer partner's own cost still comes first). Products not in a file keep their price.
- Rebates (`src/lib/domain/rebates.ts`) are separate customer-facing lines in green, named "STC Solar Incentive", "BSTC Battery Incentive" and "Solar Victoria Rebate" (+ "Solar Victoria Battery Rebate"), shown as a reward on the system step (the rebates the customer is eligible for, no price; VIC new systems without the toggle on see "you may also be eligible") and with the price at checkout (price before rebates, each rebate, price after): federal solar STCs, federal battery STCs, and Solar Victoria (VIC addresses only, behind a customer toggle, with an eligibility link). The Solar Victoria interest-free loan is a second, always-visible toggle (switching it on switches on the rebate it depends on) that lowers the upfront cost, not the price. For expansions of existing solar the card explains that Solar Victoria doesn't apply rather than disappearing. Rules are loaded daily from the CER and Solar Victoria (`/api/rebates`, `src/lib/server/rebate-rates.ts`, parsers tested against saved page text in `src/lib/domain/__fixtures__`), falling back to `VERIFIED_RATES` (hand-checked 27 Sep 2026) per item. STCs use the CER postcode zone table (`zone-ratings.ts`), deeming years for the install year, and the battery factor for the install date, tapered 100% ≤14 kWh / 60% ≤28 / 15% ≤50 kWh. The STC $ price isn't published by the CER: set `STC_PRICE`. Sunshine comes from NASA POWER for the home's coordinates (`/api/sunshine`).
- Live mode (`NEXT_PUBLIC_PREVIEW_MODE=false`): fictional demo installers are never matched (`customerNetwork`), the sample installer portal is off (only signed-in partners get in), My RENUABL is always labelled an example (`PortalPreviewBanner`, for every visitor in preview and live), and no preview details appear in errors. Keep anything demo-only behind `PREVIEW_MODE`.
- Estimates never claim solar covers all of a home's use (`ASSUMPTIONS.maxSolarShare`, 90%).
- Emails are built from `cleanOrder`/`plainText` on the server (no links or addresses from the browser), and the call label is built server-side.
- Checkout is an editable basket: optional items can be removed or added back; the core system is edited on the system step.
- RENUABL Care (`src/lib/domain/care.ts`) is switched off (`CARE_ENABLED = false`) until performance summaries and alerts can be delivered (phase 2/3, via inverter monitoring APIs). Don't show or sell it. When it returns: $19/month or $199/year, opt-in only, never pre-selected, never charged with the deposit, billed after switch-on.
- System options, in order: Essential · Recommended (default) · Maximum. (While Care is off, Maximum does not include free Care.)
- Desktop flow steps are centred columns (`FlowStep` `width`), not left-aligned.
- The example home (`mock/home-energy.ts`, sample jobs, sample addresses) never names a real business or a real street address: its partner is "Your installation partner" and the address is "8 Example Court, Glen Waverley". Revo never names the matched partner while "Finding your installation partner" is still playing (`RevoGuide` waits for `matchedPostcode`). The My RENUABL tab bar marks the active tab with a bolder icon (no fill), and /my/service counts as Support.
- My RENUABL (the post-install home) is shown before purchase as a preview: `PortalTeaser`/`PortalPreview` (`src/components/consumer/portal-preview.tsx`) on the system step and confirmation page, and a banner on `/my` for reserved-but-not-installed customers. Always label its figures as an example home; its live data depends on inverter monitoring integration.
- Post-purchase customers book service visits with their installer at `/my/service` (`src/lib/domain/service.ts`). Never quote a call-out fee in the UI: warranty work is free, other fees are confirmed before the visit is locked in.
- "About your home" and the system step, trimmed (8 Oct 2026, same aim as the reserve step): before a bill, only the upload, "Tell us roughly what you spend" and "Prefer a hand with this?"; after it, the savings reveal, the existing-solar questions (when the bill shows panels) and "Would you like a battery?". Roof, flat mounting, storeys, phase, EV now, EV planned and backup sit in one collapsed "Your home details (optional)" panel whose line says what's assumed (`homeDetailsSummary`: "Tiled roof, single storey, single-phase power.", checked on the call); `isAboutComplete` needs only the bill, the existing-solar answers and the battery answer. The "Don't have your bill handy?" box (`BillLater`) is gone (the spend range and "Prefer a hand?" cover it). The system step has no My RENUABL teaser and no monitoring row, "Why this system, and how we worked it out" is one disclosure, and `DESIGN_ON_CALL` is two sentences. The Extras step is skipped (system → installation partner; `previousHref` skips it; the page still exists): priced extras are "Add to your system" on the reserve step, and the unpriced ones (talk about on the call, coming soon) are chips there, "Interested in anything else? Tap to mention it on your call."
- Reserve step layout (8 Oct 2026, Martin: "the page is very busy"; remove overwhelm by asking for things only when needed): one centred column titled "Your order summary." — one card with home, installation partner and the tentative install date (+ its note, "You can change it on your call"), then the basket, rebates with the Solar Victoria rebate and loan toggles inside the price (no separate Solar Victoria card), totals and "Due today $0"; then "Add to your system"; then "Book your free 15-minute call". "Who should we call?" (name, mobile, email, healthy-home box, consent) appears only once a call time is picked, so the details belong to the call, not the order. The sticky button reads "Book my 15-minute call" (scrolls to the booking) until then, "Confirm my call" after. The date step is "Choose a tentative installation date." (not locked in, confirmed on the call, changeable); the step rail says "Pick a tentative date · Change it any time" and "Your order and call".
- The 15-minute call is booked on the reserve step and is required: "Reserve my date" won't go through without a time (`CallBooking` there; the slot goes with `/api/reserve`, whose server-built label is in the HubSpot note, the order email and its invite). Afterwards the confirmation page offers "Change call time" (`/api/call` with `changed`, "Your call has moved") and "Change install date" (`ChangeInstallDate`, `/api/reschedule`: own reservation only, reference + email must match in the database, updates the job, HubSpot note, `installMovedEmail` + invite, and emails the job's accepted partner `jobMovedEmail`); every booking email carries `CHANGE_BOOKING_NOTE`.
- The customer books the confirmation call themselves: HubSpot Meetings when `NEXT_PUBLIC_HUBSPOT_MEETINGS_URL` is set, otherwise the in-app calendar (Monday–Saturday times, never Sundays or Victorian public holidays, before the install date, `src/lib/domain/booking.ts`).
- Minimise jargon; technical detail lives behind disclosures.
- Partner pitch: `/partners` opens with `PartnerPitch` (copy in `domain/partner-pitch.ts`, tested: no job numbers, earnings or ratings; terms from `OFFER_HOURS` and `PAYOUT_DAYS_AFTER_INSTALL`): headline, an example job offer (labelled "Example"), how it works, what we handle, tools, the money, FAQ, then "Become a partner" starts the form. Keep it in step with the portal and the partner terms.
- Partners (`src/lib/domain/partner.ts`): sign-up at `/partners` (public, dark theme) starts with installer (installation only; RENUABL sells and supplies) or retailer (they conduct the sale, supply, install and connect). It collects name, contact, business name, ABN (ATO checksum), website, business address, base address (Google) and job radius, accreditation number, electrical licence, public liability (at least $10M, current) and a certificate of currency, then install rates (c/W, a close-to-home rate for single-storey homes within N km, extra array, double storey, switchboard upgrade, tilt, battery, three phase, EV) with RENUABL's pricing as grey suggestions (blank = suggestion); retailers also give product costs (by SKU) and a margin. The switchboard upgrade suggestion is $1,200 (confirmed). `/api/partners/apply` validates server-side, stores privately (Vercel Blob, `src/lib/server/storage.ts`), adds a HubSpot contact (lifecycle "other") + note, and only reports success when saved somewhere (live mode). Pricing uses a matched partner's `pricing` (`partnerPricingFor`, `PartnerPricing` in costing); without it, RENUABL's rates.
- Job handover (`src/lib/domain/handover.ts`, `Handover` in the job workspace): photos of the roof before, each array, inverter, switchboard, commissioning and inverter online, plus every panel serial, the inverter serial and each battery module serial; submit only when complete. Stored privately at `jobs/<recordKey>/` (long random key; photos are read through the record, never by path). Writes are allowed for the job's own partner (or the sample portal); the customer's record is `/my/installation?record=<key>` (example record, no photos, when there's none). Without storage it's kept on the device (IndexedDB). Job documents the partner supplies (e.g. the certificate of compliance) are uploaded in the job's Documents section, page by page (`documents` on the record, `/api/jobs/[key]/documents`), and shown on the customer's record. Every document upload uses `CaptureButtons` (`src/components/ui/capture.tsx`): "Take a photo" (camera) + "Upload a file" on phones, one upload button on desktop; photos are shrunk on the device before sending.
- Materials (`src/lib/domain/materials.ts`): each job's products come from `billOfMaterials` (no prices or labour), grouped for ordering, with a Materials tab/section in the job and `/installer/materials` adding up accepted and confirmed jobs for the next 7/14/30 days (copy list, CSV). The roof is read from the job's site notes; phase defaults to single.
- Variations (`src/lib/domain/variations.ts`): the partner prices extra work found on site ex GST (presets use their rates, else `suggestedRates`); the customer sees it with margin + GST and approves or declines once on their installation record (`/api/jobs/[key]/variations/[id]`, allowed live since the key is private). Kept on the job's record with `connection`. Approved variations add to the payout; "Nothing is charged now".
- Grid connection and rebates (`connection.ts`): steps owned by the partner or RENUABL depending on installer/retailer (installer: CES, EWR, meter; RENUABL: pre-approval, STCs, Solar Victoria, confirmed). Partners tick only their own steps; customers see plain-language progress.
- Compliance (`compliance.ts`): public liability (≥ $10M), electrical licence, SAA accreditation with expiry dates; expiring within 30 days warns, lapsed or missing pauses new offers (accepted jobs carry on). Banner across the portal, `/installer/compliance` takes renewals (stored privately for review).
- Payouts (`payouts.ts`): installers are paid their installation lines (no equipment) plus approved variations, 7 days after install (PLACEHOLDER terms); RENUABL issues recipient-created tax invoices (GST only when the partner is registered; issued by Reburthed Pty Ltd trading as RENUABL, ABN 96 662 374 905, `RENUABL_BUSINESS`). Bank details are device-only in preview; never put them in HubSpot or emails.
- SMS (`sms.ts`, Twilio in `server/sms.ts`): short, one link built from `SITE_URL`, never from the request. Demo jobs are never texted (`customerMobileFor` returns null until jobs come from the database).
- Learn with Revo (the customer name for the energy guides, `LEARN_NAME`; `/learn`, `/learn/[slug]`; content in `src/lib/domain/guides.ts`, tested): Revo's plain-language articles for search (sizing from a bill, rebates, batteries, battery size from your exports (Martin's video), eight Healthy home guides (gas cooking and indoor air, bedroom air overnight, water filtration, kitchen tap vs whole-house filters, what's in Melbourne tap water (figures marked VERIFY against Melbourne Water and retailer reports; links to Melbourne Water and the NHMRC guidelines, the allowed outside sources being solar.vic.gov.au, cer.gov.au, esc.vic.gov.au, Melbourne's water authorities, nhmrc.gov.au and health.vic.gov.au), fresh-air heat-recovery ventilation, circadian lighting, how lighting changes the mood of your home (about atmosphere, never a health outcome); they end with a link to the Home Health check, and the two battery guides end with the WHOOP line while it's open), reading a bill, feed-in tariffs, how solar and inverters work, roofs, phase, myths, EVs, looking after panels, what happens after reserving, three quotes vs one plan), each ending with "Start with your address". House style (our difference from long, technical solar blogs): every guide opens with "The short answer" (`answer`, sage box), then short numbered or plain headings, lists over paragraphs (paragraphs ≤ 480 characters, reads ≤ 6 minutes, tested) and a "Revo's tip" (`tip` block); `/learn` groups guides by `GUIDE_TOPICS`. Static pages with Article JSON-LD, listed in `sitemap.ts` (`publicSiteUrl`: always https://www.renuabl.com.au). Search indexing (`searchIndexing` in `domain/site.ts`, tested) follows Vercel, not preview mode: the production deployment (`VERCEL_ENV=production`) gets robots.txt `Allow: /` (private areas disallowed) with the sitemap and no noindex; Vercel preview deployments and local builds get `Disallow: /` and noindex; `ALLOW_INDEXING=true/false` overrides. Guides are linked from the home page (header on desktop, "New to solar? Learn with Revo" under the address, the "New to solar? Start here." strip of three guides, and the `LearnPopup`: Revo's card with three guides after `LEARN_POPUP.delaySeconds`, home page only, never while someone is typing, and once closed or used it stays away `quietDays` (7) on that device), the footer and the mobile menu; guides have their own Ask Revo context (`learn`). Every claim must be true: no "exact/precise/guaranteed", no "AI", no "installer", no "first/best/leading" (unprovable, Australian Consumer Law; positioning is `GUIDES_TAGLINE`); rebate amounts aren't quoted, link to CER / Solar Victoria. Keep guides in step with the sizing and booking rules above and update `updated` when facts change.
- SEO (`domain/seo.ts`, tested): the home page title/description (`HOME_TITLE`, `HOME_DESCRIPTION`, canonical `/`) and Organization + WebSite JSON-LD (no ratings or reviews); guides add BreadcrumbList + Article (`abstract` = the short answer). Share images are generated in brand colours (`app/opengraph-image.tsx`, per guide `learn/[slug]/opengraph-image.tsx`) plus `apple-icon.tsx`. A page that sets its own `openGraph` replaces the site-wide one, so it must repeat `siteName`, `locale` and `images`.
- Meta Pixel (`NEXT_PUBLIC_META_PIXEL_ID`, `domain/meta-pixel.ts` tested, `services/meta-pixel.ts`, `MetaPixel` in the root layout): loads only on public pages and the flow (`pixelAllowedPath`: `/`, `/battery`, `/quote`, `/learn`, `/start`, legal pages; never `/my`, `/login`, `/deposit`, `/installer`, `/admin`, whose URLs can carry sign-in links or record keys). Its own route-change tracking and autoConfig are off; we send PageView per route, one `Step…` custom event per flow step reached (see Lead capture and funnel), the custom `BillUploaded` once per visit when `/api/bill` has read a bill (`billTracked`; no contact details exist yet), the custom `SpendEstimated` once when a spend range is picked instead (`spendTracked`), the standard `Contact` when "Don't have your bill handy?" is saved, `Schedule` when a call is booked on `/book-a-call` (both are real leads in HubSpot), and `Lead` once when a date is reserved (`/api/reserve` succeeded, `reservedTracked`), so a Meta lead always matches a HubSpot contact and staff email. Event names in `META_EVENTS`; no parameters ever. Never send Meta the bill, its contents, addresses, names, emails, phones or prices; keep automatic advanced matching off in Events Manager. The privacy policy's "Advertising" section describes it.
- HubSpot tracking code (7 Oct 2026, `domain/hubspot-tracking.ts` tested, `services/hubspot-tracking.ts`, from `AdTags`): HubSpot's embed script for account 443761924 (region ap1, `js-ap1.hs-scripts.com`), loaded only on `hubspotAllowedPath` (the same pages as the Meta Pixel; never `/my`, `/login`, `/admin`, `/installer`, `/deposit`, `/brief`, `/home-health`); it counts its first page itself and we push `setPath` + `trackPageView` on later in-app page changes. It can't be unloaded, so moving in-app from an allowed page to a private one reloads the page without it. The privacy policy's Advertising section mentions it. In HubSpot, keep "non-HubSpot forms" collection off (our forms already reach HubSpot through the server, and it could copy what people type).
- Google Ads (`domain/google-ads.ts` tested, `services/google-ads.ts`; `AdTags` in the root layout sends page views to Meta and Google on `pixelAllowedPath` pages, which include `/book-a-call`): gtag.js loads only when the tag ID is set: `GADS_ID` (AW-…; short Vercel names, read on the server by `googleAdsConfig` in the root layout and handed to `AdTags`; the long `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_ADS_*` names also work), with its own page views off and enhanced conversions off. Three conversions, each sent only when its label is set: `reservation` (`GADS_RESERVATION`, with Meta's Lead), `call` (`GADS_CALL`, booked on `/book-a-call`) and `follow-up` (`GADS_FOLLOW_UP`, "Don't have your bill handy?"). Same rules as Meta: no values, no customer details, ever. The ad source in HubSpot comes from `utm_source`, else `landingSource` reads Google's gclid/gbraid/wbraid as "google" and fbclid as "meta". `/api/status` says which tags and labels are set.
- Legal pages (`/privacy`, `/terms`, `/contact`, `LegalPage`/`LegalFooter` in `components/consumer/legal-page.tsx`, details in `domain/legal.ts`): business name, ABN, contact email (`LEGAL.email`, must be a real mailbox), SMS use and "Reply STOP". Drafted for launch (Twilio and SendGrid check for them): have a lawyer review before relying on them. The home page footer links them. Consent (`ConsentBoxes`, wording in `legal.ts`): the reserve step and "Don't have your bill handy?" have a required, unticked box ("I've read and accept the Terms of Use and Privacy Policy." + `CONTACT_CONSENT` / `FOLLOW_UP_CONSENT`) and a separate optional box for tips and offers (`MARKETING_CONSENT`; never a condition of reserving). `/api/reserve` and `/api/follow-up` refuse without the tick (`readConsent`, `CONSENT_MISSING`) and put `consentRecord` (wording, `CONSENT_VERSION`, server time, marketing yes/no) in the HubSpot note and staff alert. Bump `CONSENT_VERSION` whenever the wording changes; only email marketing to contacts whose note says "Marketing tips and offers: yes".
- `/api/status` reports which services are connected (yes/no only; Stripe test/live and whether the key is accepted; `hubspotCheck` asks HubSpot live and says "ok" or what's wrong in plain words, with `hubspotTokenLooksRight` for a "pat-…" value). The HubSpot token is read everywhere through `hubspotToken()` (`HUBSPOT_PRIVATE_APP_TOKEN`, or the shorter `HUBSPOT_TOKEN`; spaces, quote marks and a leading "Bearer " are forgiven, `domain/hubspot-token.ts`, tested). Staff can open `/api/admin/hubspot-test` (linked on `/admin` as "Test HubSpot"): it does what a lead does (contact, then note) on RENUABL's own test contact website-test@renuabl.com.au and shows which step failed with HubSpot's reply; staff email alerts carry a "HubSpot:" line too.
- Accounts (`src/lib/domain/accounts.ts`, `src/lib/server/accounts.ts`): `/login` asks "I'm a customer" or "I'm a partner" and emails a one-time sign-in link (30 min; needs email (SendGrid, else Resend) and `SITE_URL`; under `next dev` on a developer's own computer with no email service the link is printed in the terminal instead: `signInDelivery`, tested, never on a built or hosted site). No passwords. Tokens and sessions are stored hashed; the answer never reveals whether an account exists. Staff are `ADMIN_EMAILS` and land on `/admin`. The header's account link and the mobile menu say "Log in"; on the home page the mobile header shows "Log in" (partners apply from the menu or `/login`).
- **Staying signed in (23 Nov 2026, Martin: "Im being signed out of my session far too quickly").** Two causes, both fixed. (1) **A database slow to wake read as signed out**: `/api/design/me` used the forgiving session read, so when Neon took too long it answered `signedIn: false`, the device forgot it was signed in and the sign-in page showed. It now reads strictly (`designAccess(true)`: a failure answers 503), and the browser (`ask` in services/design-account.ts) asks again after 0.8, 2 and 4 s on a device someone has signed in on before saying the account couldn't be reached. (2) **Sessions now roll**: `SESSION_DAYS` is 90 (was a fixed 30 from sign-in), and each check pushes the end out again once a day has gone (`sessionNeedsRenewing` in domain/accounts.ts, tested in `session-roll.test.ts`; the update in `readSession`), with `/me` sending the cookie again at its full life (`renewSessionCookie`). Someone who uses RENUABL at least once in 90 days stays signed in. Signing out still ends it at once. **A Home Screen app on an iPhone keeps its own sign-in apart from Safari's** (iOS gives it its own cookies), so it is signed into once on its own. Not yet: a passkey (Face ID or Touch ID) to sign in, or a PIN to unlock after a while away.
- Database (`src/lib/server/db.ts`, Neon via `DATABASE_URL`, `pg`): partners, jobs, offers, login tokens, sessions, and the roof designer's businesses (see Roof designer accounts); tables are created on first use. `offers-db.test.ts`, `healthy-db.test.ts` and `design-db.test.ts` run against a real Postgres when `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set (use `--no-file-parallelism`: they share the database). Local database (`tools/local-db/`, its own `package.json` so the site's build never installs it): `npm run db:install` once (real Postgres 17 from the `embedded-postgres` package, no Docker), `npm run db` starts it on this computer only (port 54329, data in `.local-db/`, ignored by git) and prints the `DATABASE_URL` for `.env.local`; `npm run db:stop` stops one left running; `npm run test:db` runs every test with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set (its own `renuabl_test` database), starting Postgres for the run if none is up. Never point either variable at the hosted database.
- Partners: applications save as pending (database + Blob + HubSpot contact with the whole application as a note); staff approve on `/admin` (approval emails the partner and notes HubSpot; "Installer of choice" = priority). Only approved partners can sign in.
- Jobs and offers (`src/lib/domain/offers.ts`, `src/lib/server/offers-engine.ts`): each reservation becomes a job, offered to one partner at a time (approved, insured ≥ $10M, home within their radius; installer of choice first, then nearest). 24 hours to accept (`OFFER_HOURS`), else it goes to the next partner; with nobody left it waits as "unassigned" for staff. Offers move on when pages load and from a daily cron (`/api/cron/offers`, `CRON_SECRET`). Until accepted, the partner sees only suburb, system and date (`portalJob`).
- The partner portal (`src/lib/services/installer.ts`) is async and scoped to the signed-in partner (`requirePortal`): they only ever see their own jobs and open offers. In preview, "Explore the sample partner portal" on `/login` opens the mock portal (demo cookie "1"). Live, the sample portal opens only through the private link `/demo/<PARTNER_DEMO_KEY>` (`server/demo-access.ts`, tested: key ≥ 20 characters, compared in constant time, rate-limited, the cookie holds a hash of the key for `DEMO_HOURS` (12), changing the key closes every open demo; a wrong key goes to the home page; `/demo` is disallowed in robots.txt). Job record writes (handover, documents, variations, connection) are allowed only for the job's own partner (`mayWriteRecord`) or the sample portal.
- Signed-in customers see their reservations at the top of My RENUABL (`getMyReservations`), in customer words ("Matching your installation partner", …).
- Roof view (`src/lib/domain/roof-view.ts`, `/api/jobs/[key]/roof`): a Google satellite image (Maps Static API, `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`, server-side only) of each job's roof, only for the partner whose accepted job it is; offers and the sample portal show the install photo (`public/brand/install-crew.webp`, supplied by the business; an aerial would give away the address). `JobPhoto` falls back to it when the view can't load.
- Roof and weather (`solar-roof.ts`, `weather.ts`; `server/google-solar.ts`, `server/google-weather.ts`): Google's Solar API gives roof faces, pitch and usable area → `panelsThatFit` (our panel size, 90% packing) and `roofFit` (comfortable / snug / check). Customers see "Your roof" on the system step (`RoofCheck`, `/api/roof`, rate-limited; lookups cached 180 days in `roof_cache`), always called an estimate confirmed on the call. Google returns the closest building, which can be a neighbour's: the address point must be on the matched building's outline box or within `HOME_EDGE_METRES` (10 m, a front yard) of it, else it's ignored as `not-this-home` (`isThisHome`; without a box, its centre within `HOME_MATCH_METRES`, 30 m), and the sun map starts from the matched building's centre, not the address point. Recommendations never exceed the panels Google's roof data says fit (`roofPanelLimit`, never below the minimum system), and the system step's sage card (`DESIGN_ON_CALL`) says the system, including the exact products and how many panels fit, is designed before the 15-minute call and shown on it, nothing final or charged until they're happy (products aren't listed online so customers don't stall comparing brands, but it must never read as hiding them); the reserve button carries `RESERVE_NO_COMMITMENT`. While the roof photo or sun map loads, `MascotLoading` shows the mascot spinning a sun. Partners see roof faces and the install-day forecast on each job, offers included (suburb-level only, no address); risky weather (rain ≥ 60% or ≥ 5 mm, storms ≥ 30%, gusts ≥ 50 km/h) raises a dashboard alert. Customers see the install-day forecast in My RENUABL within 10 days, in calm words.
- Savings use Google's roof sunshine alongside NASA (`blendedYieldPerKw`): 60% the roof's usable faces (`usableRoofSunHours`: faces with ≥ 70% of the best face's sun, area-weighted; includes shading and direction), 40% NASA, the roof figure kept within 60–125% of NASA's. The flow fetches the roof once per address (`state.roof`); "Setting up your plan" names Google Solar only when its data arrived.
- Tomorrow's solar day (`solarDayOutlook`, `TomorrowSolar` on /my): for signed-in customers with an installed system, from Google Weather at their home ("Tomorrow looks sunny: expect a strong solar day" + a tip). No kWh figures until monitoring is connected. Everyone else sees it marked as an example.
- Panel layouts (`src/lib/domain/roof-layout.ts`, `RoofDesigner`): Google's Solar API roof model (every panel spot, best first) drawn on the sharp Maps Static satellite photo moved into line with Google Solar's own aerial photo (dataLayers RGB GeoTIFF → JPEG, `server/solar-photo.ts`; the image the spots were measured on; UTM `GeoFrame`, `utm.ts`) by `server/roof-align.ts` (`bestShift`/`trustworthy` in `image-align.ts`, kept in Blob per home), since the satellite view leans on taller buildings. No trusted match → Google Solar's photo itself (blurrier, exact); no Solar photo → the satellite image as is (`/api/roof/photo?frame=1` returns `frame` + `shift`). Satellite image (zoom 20, 640 px at 2x, Web Mercator `toPixel`; must match `ROOF_VIEW_SIZES.design`). `project`/`panelOutline` handle either frame. Auto-layout = tidy groups on as few roof faces as possible (pricing allows for `assumedArrays`: 2, or 3 above 18 panels), each a rectangular block of rows on the face's grid (`compactBlock`) among spots within 10% of its best sunshine, no group under `MIN_ARRAY_PANELS` unless nothing else fits. Unit addresses (`isUnitAddress`) add `SHARED_ROOF_NOTE`. Customers never see an automatic layout (not reliable enough): the system step shows "Your roof" at the top (satellite photo via `/api/roof/image`, centred on the address with a dot marking it, direction and pitch only, never how many panels fit: "We'll work out how many panels fit on your roof, and where they go, on your 15-minute call"), with a "Looking at your roof from above…" placeholder while it loads; My RENUABL (`/my/layout?record=`, signed-in owner only) shows panels only once the partner has saved a layout, else the roof photo. They never choose panels. Sun map (switched off on the system step after it showed a neighbour's roof at 2 Hanwell Court, Glen Waverley; the code stays for later; `sun-map.ts`, `server/sun-map.ts`, `/api/roof/sun` (preview only), `SunMap`; `sun-map.ts`, `server/sun-map.ts`, `/api/roof/sun`, `SunMap`): Google Solar's annual sunlight layer coloured over the home's own roof (mask, `homeRoof` drops neighbours), yellow = most sun, blue = least, relative to the roof's best, with "Most sun on the north-facing roof (about 60 m²), then east and west" and the shaded share; it shows Google's measurements only, never panel positions. One Data Layers lookup per home (its RGB photo is kept for the satellite alignment), stored in Blob. Partners design on the job's Design tab (`panel-plan.ts`, `PanelPlanner`, `JobDesignPanel`): panels start in a tray beside the satellite photo (nothing is placed automatically); "Add panel" puts one beside the last, they drag it into place (snaps beside a neighbour within 35 cm and 4°), turn it through 360° with the round handle or ±1°/±15°/90° buttons, remove the selected one, zoom and pan; saved as lat/lng + rotation on the Maps Static photo they see (`/api/jobs/[key]/layout` `{panels}`, own accepted jobs only, never offers; arrays = groups of touching panels, `planArrays`). "Save layout", "Email customer" and "SMS customer" sit at the top of the tray; sending works only for a saved, unchanged layout (`/api/jobs/[key]/layout/send`, own jobs only, message and link to `/my/layout?record=` built on the server: `layoutReadyEmail`, `layoutReadySms`; says plainly when email or SMS isn't set up). The sample portal designs on 112 Leopold Street, South Yarra (Google Places lookup in `server/sample-roof.ts`), keeps its layouts on the device (localStorage) and never sends. Older layouts saved as Google spots (`slots`) still show; the layout's arrays feed the materials list (`CostingInput.arrays`), not the price or payout: a different panel count or extra array is a variation. Obstruction check (`obstructions.ts`, `server/obstruction-check.ts`, `/api/jobs/[key]/obstructions`, partner-only, on demand): Claude (vision, `readWithClaude`) boxes vents, skylights, aerials, existing panels and tree shade on the satellite image; panels within 0.3 m are outlined amber with "Remove them"; saved on the job, always "a helper, not a site inspection". 3D view (`House3d`, `services/house-3d.ts`): Google photorealistic 3D tiles (Map Tiles API) in CesiumJS from jsDelivr, loaded only when opened, for the job's partner and the homeowner; needs a separate key `GOOGLE_MAP_TILES_KEY` (server env; `/api/map-tiles/key` hands it only to the job's partner or homeowner when they open the view) restricted to Map Tiles + the site's referrer (never the server key).
- Roof designer (`/design`, from 10 Oct 2026, in development; `components/design/`, `domain/design/`, tested): **a standalone product for installers and solar retailers**, the first piece of a planned CRM for small solar businesses (customers, proposals from templates with their own logo and colours, an installation planner, procurement with a draft wholesaler order). It is not part of the homeowner quote flow and doesn't change the rule that customers there never see a layout or choose panels; the partner's Design tab (`PanelPlanner`) is separate too. Brought across from a separate project and restyled (nothing shared with it at run time). An address first (`DesignStudio`), then the roof from above to scale (`/api/design/roof`, 640 × 400: must match `ROOF_IMAGE`) with a bar of tools: trace roof faces and panels fill them in rows, place, move and turn single panels or groups, tilt and direction with the year's output month by month (`tilt.ts`, Melbourne's sunshine record: VERIFY before use outside Victoria), strings checked against the inverter's input limits (`electrical.ts`: every figure is a VERIFY item, and a product whose limits aren't on file is never passed), inverter suggestions by rule (`inverter-suggest.ts`), labels, guide lines, comments, undo, and up to five designs a roof. **The quote builds as the design does** (`DesignQuote`, `domain/design/quote.ts` `costSystem`): panels, the inverters and battery named on the design (none is added otherwise), racking by rule, meter, isolator, sundries, installation and commission, then margin, GST and STCs (`stcValue`: live rules from `/api/rebates`, the business's own price per certificate); any line can be typed over or taken out (`lineKey`, `editedLines`), and **a product with no cost on file is a line marked `missing` at $0, never a guess**. The customer's bill (the Bill button on the picture's bar) gives bill now, bill after, saving and payback (`designFinance`: always an estimate). **Each business has its own settings** (`Workspace` in `domain/design/pricing.ts`, edited at `/design/settings`, `DesignSettings`): supplier price lists uploaded as Excel or CSV (`/api/design/price-list` reads the file and keeps nothing; every priced row is kept by its code, and its panels, inverters and batteries fill the designer's lists), the parts every job needs (a product code looked up on the lists, or a cost typed in, which wins), installation rates, margin, fixed costs and the bill a new job starts with. The figures a workspace starts with are placeholders, not anyone's real rates. Nothing of RENUABL's own costs is used: with no list uploaded the designer offers our range without prices. **Where it is kept** (`services/design-store.ts`, the seam: designs, bill and quotes per roof; the workspace): for a signed-in business, on the server; signed out, on the device (see Roof designer accounts, below). Noindex, not linked from anywhere, no ad tags.
- Roof designer accounts (10 Oct 2026; `domain/design/account.ts` tested, `server/design-repo.ts`, `server/design-context.ts`, `services/design-account.ts`, `components/design/design-account.tsx`; `design-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): each business that uses the designer has an account with one or more members. **A business is the designer's own customer, never a RENUABL installation partner: nothing here touches the `partners` table.** Sign-in (18 Oct 2026, Martin: simpler, less about links) is one short form (`SignInForm` in `sign-in-form.tsx`, on the front page and `/design/sign-in`): **Continue with Google** (`/api/auth/google` → Google → `/api/auth/google/callback`, `server/google-sign-in.ts`: authorisation code flow, openid email, state cookie compared in constant time, a verified email only; needs `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET` with `<site>/api/auth/google/callback` as the redirect, else it says it isn't set up), or **email and password** (`/api/design/sign-in`, rate limited; scrypt hashes in `design_passwords`, `server/design-passwords.ts`; `domain/design/password.ts`: at least 10 characters), or **email alone**, which sends the one-time link (`/api/auth/link` with `as: "business"`) and says only "We've sent a sign-in link to …". An email with no password typed with a password gets a link too: **a password is only ever set by someone already signed in** (Account → Password, `/api/design/password`), so a new email is always proven first by a link or Google. All three start the same session (`openSession` in `server/accounts.ts`; lands on `/design`): anyone may sign in (so that route has its own limit per visitor), and a new email then names its business and ticks the terms (required, never pre-ticked; `BUSINESS_TERMS_VERSION` and the server's time are saved on the member; `/design/terms` is **draft placeholder wording for legal review**, as is `designSignInEmail`). **One sign-up form** (5 Nov 2026, Martin: the company belongs in the same form as the name and phone; the business name had been filled by the browser without him noticing): `SetUp` in design-account.tsx asks first and last name, the company name (a new business names it; an owner whose business is named can change it, `renameBusiness`), mobile and password (both optional), what the company does (a new business only) and the terms (anyone yet to accept them), and is shown until all of it is given (`state` not ready, or `needsProfile`). **The welcome on Today** (5 Nov 2026, Martin: the first visit shouldn't open on Show me my day; `domain/design/welcome.ts` tested, `components/design/today-welcome.tsx`, `/api/design/onboarding`, `business_members.welcomed_at`): `/api/design/me` says `welcome` until it is finished or skipped (never while viewing as someone); Sol types a greeting in place of the greeting screen (`welcomeLines`), then each step as he gets to it: **connect email and calendar** (Gmail, Google Calendar or Outlook, each in a small window over the page, already connected ones ticked), **add your team** for the owner only (an email and **Admin** or **User**, `TEAM_LEVELS`: Admin is every permission, User sees their own deals and sends proposals without costs, prices or settings, set through `addMember` and `setMemberPermissions`; nobody is emailed), and **Show me around**, which ends the welcome and runs a spotlight tour over Today (`WELCOME_TOUR` through `Spotlight` in new-look.tsx: Today's tabs, Sol's box, the designer, the menu, Sol's panel, the Marketplace, the rail; places not on screen are passed over); every step can be skipped and "Skip setting up for now" ends it. **About you** (5 Nov 2026, Martin: the first sign-in greeted him by a name guessed from his email): once the business is ready, everyone in it, owner or not, gives their first and last name, a mobile if they like (`normaliseMobile`) and, if they want one, a password to sign in with next time (the same `SetUp` form in design-account.tsx, `cleanProfile` in `domain/design/profile.ts`, tested; `/api/design/profile`; `business_members.first_name`, `last_name`, `mobile`); `/api/design/me` says `needsProfile` until a first name is kept (never while viewing as someone or in a staff sample business) and gives `firstName`, which is the only name Today greets by (`greetingName`'s guess from the email is no longer used). A fresh account is broadcast to every screen holding it (`ACCOUNT_EVENT`). One business per email; the owner adds or removes people at `/design/account` (no email is sent to them; they accept the terms on first sign-in); every member designs and sees prices and margin. Roles are checked in one place: `can(role, action)` through `requireMember`. **Tenant rule: every designer table carries `business_id`, every function in `design-repo.ts` takes the business id first and filters by it, and that id only ever comes from the signed-in member (`design-context.ts`), never from the browser.** Tables: `businesses`, `business_members`, `design_settings` (parts, rates, margin), `design_price_lists` (one row a list, sent and kept apart from settings because a list can be thousands of rows), `design_projects` (one roof per place per business: address, lat/lng, the packed `DesignSet`, `DesignExtras`, and a `version` that turns a save from an out-of-date copy into a 409 instead of an overwrite). Customers, proposals and jobs will be further tables with `business_id`. Everything from the browser goes through `cleanRoofWork` / `cleanWorkspaceSettings` / `cleanSupplierList` (the designer's own `clean*` functions) on the server first. `services/design-store.ts` is the seam: signed in, the server is the only store; signed out, the device as before (so the designer can be tried first), with "Sign in to keep this". The first sign-in on a device brings its roofs and settings into the account once (`importDeviceWork`, `/api/design/import`: never over anything the account already has) and removes from the device what the server confirmed. No staff view of businesses on `/admin` yet. Defaults chosen without Martin's sign-off, each cheap to change: open sign-up, one business per email, members see costs, device copy removed after import, name-only sign-up.
- Roof designer layout (11 Oct 2026, after several passes with Martin, who asked for it to be thought about the way Apple's designers would; `roof-designer.tsx`). It must not look like the tool it was first modelled on, and **there are no steps or modes** (tried as Roof → Panels → Electrical → Quote and removed: not useful). **Above the picture**: the figures in one strip of three boxes (1 Nov 2026, as Primero's): Solar (kW, panels, the daily yield and the inverter beside them), Battery (kWh, modules), and For this home on white (home uses, bill now → after, saves a year, payback), red with "Add the customer's bill" until the bill is in. **The picture is as wide as the window allows** (`FIT`; 640 × 400, the widest shape the map service gives at this zoom), and everything else floats on it as one family of soft see-through plates and rounds (`GLASS`), each in a fixed place. **Two pads in the top corners**, each a circle with two smaller half-rounds growing out of it (Martin's design): top left, **Select** in the circle (the main tool, never to be looked for) with **Add a panel** to its right and **Erase** beneath; top right, the mirror, **String panels** in the circle with **Trace a roof face** to its left and the **Guide line** beneath. **The system, top centre**: the kit as three rounds, **the panel the largest in the middle** (showing how many are on the roof) with **the inverter and the battery** as half-rounds either side, and the design and the customer's bill as chips beside them; each opens its choices beneath it. **Getting about**, on the left edge (the hand, zoom, undo, redo). **The dock**, bottom centre, holds the two tools that annotate (`SECONDARY`: label, comment), the drawing and shade views, and at its right end **Design it for me** (a wand; Martin, 18 Oct 2026). **Suggest a layout was removed** (Martin): Design it for me lays panels by the same rules; its box and the three designs it made still open bottom right of the picture. **The main switchboard** (`RoofDesign.switchboard`, kept by `cleanDesign`, tested): a round beside the battery's (`SwitchboardGlyph`) puts an MSB mark on the picture, drawn in the inverter and battery's line style (a board of breakers, "MSB" under it), dragged with Select to where the board is (`msbAt`), and taken off by pressing again or with the eraser. What is picked (or the strings) shows top right under the second pad; the scale bar is bottom left. The eraser takes away the panel, label or guide line clicked, or everything dragged across, as one step to undo. **The quote opens beside the picture only when asked for** (the Quote button in the bar at the top, which shows the price; closed by default so the picture has the width; the choice is kept on the device; it stays worked out while closed). **Short view** (Martin: more room for the picture): "Short view" in the quote narrows it to 13rem with smaller type, each group's total instead of its lines, cost, margin, the price and after STCs, and the missing-cost warning; "Every line" goes back (`DesignQuote` `compact`, kept on the device as `renuabl.design.quote-short`). Panels are drawn as dark glass with a thin grey edge; **orange is only for what is picked**. **Laying panels by rule** (`domain/design/suggest.ts`, `pack.ts`, tested; used by Design it for me; 18 Oct 2026, Martin, after layouts at 2 Hanwell Court and 3 Bill Place came out landscape for no reason, stacked oddly, across two roofs and over the edge): panels are laid by the installer's rules in `pack.ts`. **From the gutter up, in a pyramid**: the first row runs along the roof's lower edge and is the longest; each row above is no longer than the one below and centred over it (`packArea`; rows are planned first, then the panels shared out so no row is a lone panel: one moves down a row instead, `MIN_ROW` 2). **Portrait and landscape mixed to fit the most** (Martin, his own layout at 3 Bill Place: a portrait row along the gutter, landscape rows above as the hip narrows): each row is tried both ways and the mix that holds the most panels on the face is kept, by a search over the rows (`plan` in `packArea`, memoised); ties go to fewer panels on their side (landscape takes more brackets and rail on tile), then fewer rows. The pyramid is in metres: no row longer than the one below. **Quick on any roof** (2 Nov 2026, after a 400-spot commercial roof froze the designer for minutes): whether a point is on Google's spots is answered from a 2 m grid (`coverer` in suggest.ts), the faces and the rows found on them are kept between counts (`areasFor`, `rowsFound`), and the row search stops after `SEARCH_BUDGET` steps a face, laying the rest row by row. **Clear of every edge, for wind**: every panel as drawn is inside its face by twice the gap under it (panel underside to roof surface) or 200 mm, whichever is greater (`clearanceFor`, `MOUNT_STANDOFF` 100 mm PLACEHOLDER; Martin's rule, VERIFY against the racking maker's wind guide), or a traced face's own setback when larger. **One face per array, the fewest arrays** (`packAreas`): one array when a face within 5% of the best sun holds them all, else at most two (three above 18 panels), the sunniest first, none under `MIN_ARRAY_PANELS`, faces under 80% of the best sun or steeper than 45° left alone. With faces traced, they are packed this way as panels placed by hand (`packFaces`; the faces' own grids switched off, and any place someone switched off kept clear as an obstacle). **Square with the roof lines** (Martin: panels are never laid askew to the roof): a pitched face's rows follow Google's measure of it; a nearly flat one's (under `LOW_PITCH`, 12°, where Google can't tell which way it looks) are turned square with the house (`houseGrid` from the pitched faces, `squareTo`). **Drawn at their true size from above** (`planSize`): a panel on a slope or a tilt frame is shorter down it by the cosine of its tilt, for panels placed by hand and laid by the rules alike (two full-length rows reached past the ridge on the picture at Bill Place); faces traced from 18 Oct 2026 draw their grid that way too (`Face.plan`), older ones keep full-length places so their switched-off places and strings still mean the same panels. Without, each of Google's faces is its spots reaching `FACE_EDGE` (0.35 m, as Drawing mode draws it) past them, never a point on another face's spots (`hip-roof-model.json` is Bill Place's model as metres from the middle, no address). **Google's model is checked against today's photo first** (`loadModelFit` → `/api/roof/photo?frame=1`): lined up, it is moved into place (`modelOffset`); when Google's own photo of the roof doesn't match today's (`shift` null: often a roof rebuilt since; Hanwell Court's is December 2018) nothing is placed and it says so with the date and asks for the roof to be traced (`mismatchNote`); with no Google photo to compare, it is used as it is. Design it for me goes the same way, and **prefers an inverter whose input limits are on file** (`INVERTER_ELECTRICAL`) when the cheapest by size isn't, up to about a step bigger, so its designs can be strung; the most independence is every panel the roof takes in tidy arrays. It replaces the design's panels (asked first), is a starting point to check against the picture, and says so plainly when there is no roof data (the Solar API must be enabled on the Google key). **Portrait or landscape** (18 Oct 2026, Martin; since 1 Nov a card, see the designer in the frame): choosing one sets the way new panels are laid (`addLandscape`, `laidAs` in pack.ts; a ghost beside a panel only follows panels laid the same way), lays any picked panels that way too, and the fan folds away; with panels picked, Add a panel opens the fan without leaving Select, so they stay picked and the lit bubble is the way they lie now (`reorient` in pack.ts: each row and its count kept, the gutter row's edge kept, rows above spaced again for the new shape, each centred where it was; ids, tilt and the way they look kept, so strings keep their panels). **Panels click into place** (18 Oct 2026, Martin: lining a row up flush by eye overlapped or left a gap): panels dragged, or a row being drawn with Add a panel, that come within `SNAP_REACH` (35 cm) of a panel running the same way (or a quarter turn round: a landscape row above a portrait one) are shifted as a group to sit edge to edge with the usual gap, above or beside, and lined up along the row (centred, edges in line, or panel over panel, the nearest), never onto another panel (`snapShift` in `snap.ts`, tested); with no panel near, they click to the wind clearance (`clearanceFor`, or the face's own setback when larger) from a traced face's edge or a guide line they run along and come within reach of (`edgeShift`, `placeShift` in roof-designer.tsx); holding Alt places freely. **Design it for me says what it will do first** (Martin): the button opens a box with the three designs and what each aims for (`SELF_DESIGN_AIMS`), what they are laid from (traced faces or Google's roof data), a note with Add the bill when there is none, and that they replace the roof's designs; nothing is made until "Make the three designs". **Quote quantities can be typed over** (`LineEdit.qty`): the line is priced again at its cost each, unless a cost is typed too, which wins; kept with the design (`cleanExtras`: whole numbers up to `MAX_LINE_QTY`) and carried into the published proposal and its order. **Turning what is picked**: the −5°, −1°, 90°, +1°, +5° buttons sit in a small bar just under the picked panels (over them when they are low on the picture), not on the side dial (Martin: close to the panel just laid); the dial keeps the tilt and the bin. **The strings box closes** (Martin) with its cross, Esc, or a click off the panels, which goes back to Select; **a string's check opens only from the tick or cross beside it** (`checking`), never by hovering, with its own close, and sits clear of the strings box where the picture is wide enough. **No harsh black** (Martin): the figures strip is the soft graphite (`bg-primary`, white type, orange figures), as are the studio's accents (`--primary`, `--forest`), not black; `--ink` stays near-black for text. A new tool joins a pad or the dock, never a new bar.
- Roof designer CRM, slice 1, server side (10 Oct 2026; `domain/design/crm.ts` tested, `server/design-crm-repo.ts`, `/api/design/customers`, `/api/design/jobs`, `/api/design/overview`; `design-crm-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): a business's own customers, the jobs it is doing for them and a record of what happened. Nothing to do with RENUABL's homeowners, reservations or partners. Stages are fixed for every business for now (`JOB_STAGES`, twelve: lead, designed, proposal, won, ordered, scheduled, installed, inspection, coes, grid, claimed, lost; `STAGE_LABELS`), grouped as `OPEN_STAGES` (lead, designed, proposal), `WON_STAGES` (won and everything after it but lost), `PRE_INSTALL_STAGES` (won, ordered, scheduled) and `INSTALLED_STAGES` (installed to claimed). A job also carries `kw` (the solar on its design, as last saved from the designer), `wonAt` and `installedAt` (when it first reached a won stage, and an installed one). Customers have a first and a last name (`firstName`, `lastName`; `name` is the two together, `splitName` for older ones). Sales shows one of the `PIPELINES` in `domain/design/deal.ts`, chosen from a dropdown: Sales (lead to won), Post sale (won to installed), Post installation (installed to claimed), plus every stage, lost, and any the business has saved. The overview (`design-home.tsx`) is drawn from `dashboard(jobs, today)` in `deal.ts` (tested): sales for the financial year and the month against the same days of last month and last year, new deals, deals sold and conversion, kW sold, installations booked and done, deals at grid connection and the open pipeline. The front page (signed out, 18 Oct 2026, Martin: the landing page is the sales tool) opens on `FrontHero` (`front-hero.tsx`; Martin: Apple, Tesla, SpaceX, less is more, light not black; the reaction wanted is "this is so damn good": fast, no guesswork, the hard admin done): what RENUABL is in black and white above the punchline (`LANDING_HERO.what`, "The all-in-one platform for solar businesses"), **"From roof to revenue."** huge (what the businesses we sell to want: sales, revenue, less complication, speed), the line beneath answering by itself a phrase at a time (`follows`: It's quoted. It's sent. It's signed. It's ordered. It's booked.), what it covers in one line (`covers`), Get started and See it work, and "Roof to signed proposal, in one sitting." (`speed`; no time figure until real roofs have been timed: Australian Consumer Law). The second fold is the product at work: "It prices as you draw." over the live designer demo, rising and settling flat as it is scrolled to. Then a sentence a screen (`CHAPTERS`, each `live` only once the product does it): "Three designs. One click." (`ThreeDesignsDemo`; hidden until the designer's three-designs button is in), "Send it before you leave the driveway.", "Signed. Then it runs itself.", each fading in (`Reveal`) with its moving example, and the close: "Draw once." / "The hard part's done." with the sign-in. No feature lists or comparison table. Each demo starts from its beginning when it scrolls into view (`useTimeline`). The examples: the automations as moving examples (`designer-showcase.tsx`, copy in `domain/design/landing.ts`, tested: no unprovable claim, no other product named): the quote building itself on a drawn roof (site from the lead, split array, tilt frames, inverter suggested, three-phase fee, battery, strings), a note and proposal sent from the designer and answered like a text into the CRM, and what a signature sets going (signed proposals, Ordering Station, Install Scheduler, and once installed, Installer Invoices), then the usual way against this one. Each feature is `live` or `soon` (`soon` ones carry "Coming soon"); since 18 Oct 2026 all are live. Made-up home and customers, example prices, still with reduced motion. The demo home is drawn to read like an aerial photograph (`aerial-home.tsx`: a hipped tile roof with lit faces and weathering, a ribbed garage roof, lawn, trees and shadows, pool, drive, street, grain; Martin: a flat drawing made the designer look basic), with panels drawn as panels (cells, silver frame) laid as a designer would: landscape rows shortening under the ridge, the west hip, the garage. A narration under the demo says each step in plain words (`NARRATION`, with "3 of 9" and a progress bar; every line something the designer does today), the demos run about 3 seconds a step so they can be read, and a spotlight follows each step (`FOCUS`: the part of the picture lit and ringed, the rest dimmed; the quote lines and overlays that go with it lit, the rest faded), and the inverter and battery are chosen as in the designer: the kit rounds top centre, "Suggest one for these panels" / "Recommended for this home" opening beneath, "Use this one" pressed. The messaging demo shows the note arriving by email with the link (never by text: SMS isn't offered for this), then the proposal (roof, system, price, go ahead) with the chat beside it. Tables `design_customers`, `design_jobs`, `design_activity`, under the same tenant rule as the accounts (business id first in every repo function, from `requireMember` only); jobs and activity point at their customer together with the business, so a row can't hang off another business's customer, and an id that is unknown or another business's answers the same 404. Everything from the browser goes through `cleanCustomerInput` / `cleanJobInput` / `cleanActivityInput` (an edit leaves alone what wasn't sent; a place on the map is both figures or neither, inside Australia). A new customer with an address gets a first job (a lead called "Solar"). Who wrote an activity and when are the member's email and the server's time, never the browser's; a move between stages is written by the server ("Moved to Proposal sent"), and can't be posted. **Designs and jobs find each other by the roof they share** (`roofKey` of lat/lng): when a roof design is first kept or saved (`linkJobsToRoof` from `createProject`/`saveProject`), or a job is made or moved to a place the business has drawn, the job gets the design's id and a job still at "lead" moves to "designed"; doing it again changes nothing, and deleting a design leaves the job. A new business gets one made-up customer (five until 21 Nov 2026, see One sample deal, and the tour of a deal) (`SAMPLE_CUSTOMERS`, `sample: true`: plainly fictional names, "Sample Street"-style addresses with no place on the map, example.com emails, mobiles from the range set aside for fiction) the first time its customers, jobs or overview are read (`businesses.samples_added_at`), and never again once deleted. The overview's won value counts won and installed jobs; its open value counts lead, designed and proposal.
  **Screens** (10 Oct 2026, Martin: a dedicated home page with sign in, landing on an overview, with a left-hand nav): `/design` is the product's front page with the sign-in form on it when signed out, and the business's overview when signed in (`DesignHome`); every signed-in screen sits in `DesignShell` (`design-shell.tsx`: Overview, Sales, Customers, Design tool down the left, Prices and settings and the account at its foot; icons only on the design tool, where the roof needs the room; across the top on a phone; `PATHS` holds the addresses). `/design/customers` and `/design/customers/[id]` (`design-customers.tsx`: list with search, New customer with the address search, details, jobs, history), `/design/sales` (`design-sales.tsx`: a column a stage, jobs moved by dragging or by the stage on the card). **The design tool moved to `/design/roof`**; `/design/roof?job=<id>` opens a job's own address, shows the customer in the bar, and on saving writes the primary design's price after STCs to the job (`value`). Screens talk to the server only through `services/crm.ts`. The CRM needs a signed-in business (no on-device version); the design tool and its settings still work signed out.
- Roof designer proposals (11 Oct 2026; `domain/design/proposal.ts`, `brand.ts`, `system.ts` tested; `server/design-proposal-repo.ts`, `design-proposal.ts`, `design-images.ts`, `proposal-pdf.tsx`; `services/proposals.ts`; `components/design/proposal-view.tsx`, `design-proposal.tsx`; `design-proposal-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): what a business sends its customer once a roof is designed and priced, **under the business's own name, logo and colours, never RENUABL's** (the customer's page, its share details and the PDF name RENUABL nowhere). **A proposal is published, not live**: publishing works everything out on the server from what is saved (the job's roof design read by `systemSpecOf`, the one function the designer's quote builder also uses; the bill; the quote's hand changes; the business's price lists; today's rebate rules) and keeps a fixed copy. The browser sends only the choices (template, 7/14/30 days, a note) and the saved roof's version, so what was previewed is what goes out (409 otherwise). It is refused while a quote line has no cost or nothing is drawn. **Two halves, kept apart**: `ProposalView` (the customer's copy: system, price inc GST, STCs, you pay, estimates, branding) is built without ever holding a cost; `ProposalInternal` (cost lines, margin) is the business's record in its own column, which no public function reads. Tests check that no cost word (`COST_WORDS`), figure or product code is in anything sent to a customer. **The customer's link** is `/p/<48 random hex>`: no sign-in, noindex, no-referrer, no ad tags, `/p/` disallowed in robots, a wrong link is a plain 404. From it: read, download the PDF (`@react-pdf/renderer`, one layout in the business's colour, made from the customer's copy alone; the roof is drawn as the panels on a plain ground), "I'd like to go ahead" (a typed name and their own tick, kept once with the server's time; the job moves to Won and it is written on the record) and the chat with the business (see Roof designer chat; it replaced the one-question box). First opening is written on the record once (told by the page in a browser, so a link preview doesn't count). A price lapses after its last day (readable, not acceptable); "Take it back" leaves only how to reach the business. **Versions**: one link a job, for good. While the customer hasn't opened the newest version, publishing again brings it up to date in place (so the design tool's "Publish design", which publishes on every save for a roof opened from a deal, doesn't pile up versions); once opened or accepted it stays as they saw it and the next is a new version. The design tool's Publish leaves an accepted proposal alone (a changed one is sent on purpose from `/design/deals/<id>/proposal`); a roof opened without a deal, or signed out, is saved and says why there is no proposal. Publishing moves a job at lead or designed to proposal and sets its value to what the customer pays. Activity kind `proposal` carries sent / opened / accepted / withdrawn (a message in the chat is kind `message`). **Branding** (`/design/settings/brand`, `design_brand`): trading name, contacts, ABN (checksum), licence lines (not checked by us), about, points, warranty and payment words, conditions, five designs (`TEMPLATES`, 18 Oct 2026, Martin: Classic, Compact, Bold, **Minimal** (fine lines, big light type, no boxes, parts numbered) and **Split** (photo or a block of the main colour beside the title, headings down the left); how each opens and sets its parts is `TEMPLATE_LOOK` (`lookOf`, `lookVars` CSS variables for the part's box), read by the page, the PDF and the pickers' small pictures (`proposal-thumbs.tsx`), so they agree), a chart style for the year's output (`CHART_STYLES`: bars, line, shaded area, table; `theme.chart` on the view, bars on older proposals; with a chart the bill now and after is two bars), three page palettes plus the business's two colours as the site's own tokens (`themeVars`; six hex digits only, text on the main colour picked by contrast). **Pictures** (`design_files`, in Postgres): JPEG, PNG or WebP by their own bytes, never SVG, always redrawn smaller with `sharp` so nothing but the picture is kept (no location data); one logo, one cover, six photos; taken out of the kit they are only marked removed, so a sent proposal keeps its pictures; a link serves only the pictures its proposal was sent with. Wording the templates always carry: every output and saving is an estimate, a saving is rounded down, nothing guaranteed, no shade in the figures; output and savings are left out for homes outside Victoria (the sunshine record is Melbourne's); no 25-year figures. **A battery proposal opens on the battery** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: the roof array isn't the story when the sale is a battery; `domain/design/battery-hero.ts` tested, `batteryHero(view, { roofShown, chosen })`, following the option the customer has picked): solar and battery keeps the roof with its panels as the cover picture and lays the battery over it (a dark fade in one corner, a dashed flow from the panels to a card: "And your evenings", the kWh, a charge bar filling and emptying, Afternoon / Evening / Small hours, "Holds up to N kWh for your evenings", the share of a day's use from the bill rounded down to the ten (an estimate), and the backup line when the option has backup), the roof block below then left out; a battery alone is the home's day (a fine-line home, the sun's arc, the battery on the wall, the grid for the small hours, a 14-second loop) with the same lines, and the headline figures lead with the battery's kWh. CSS only, one still frame with reduced motion; no output or saving figures in it (`heroWords` checked against `COST_WORDS` and the claims we never make); the PDF has a still card on its cover. Solar only is unchanged. Not yet seen on a real battery proposal published from a design, on a light brand colour, or on Safari. Not built: emailing a proposal for the business, drawn signatures, payment. Defaults chosen without Martin's sign-off beyond the eight he approved: the link's token is kept as it is in the database (the proposal it opens is in the same database); the share of power from the roof isn't shown to customers; the PDF has no satellite picture.
- Roof designer settings for proposals, and permissions (11 Oct 2026; `domain/design/brand.ts`, `blocks.ts`, `permissions.ts`, `account.ts`, tested in `settings.test.ts` and `proposal-terms.test.ts`; `components/design/settings-proposal.tsx`; the slugs are in `settings-list.ts`). Everything below is part of the business's brand kit (`design_brand`), checked by `cleanBrandKit`, used by the next proposal published, and frozen into that proposal's copy. **Payment** (`payment`): prices written with GST in one figure or broken out (`gst`; the price always has GST in it: a no-GST price isn't offered, because the quote builder adds 10% and that is quote maths, not wording), a deposit (none, a percentage of what the customer pays, or a set amount), payment milestones (named shares that must add up to 100, or none: the reason is said when they don't), and bank details (name, BSB of 6 digits, account number, a reference: all or none). **Bank details are sensitive**: kept only in the brand kit and the proposals that carry them, shown only on that business's own proposals and PDFs, never logged, never in HubSpot or any email. **Warranty** (`warranty`): the business's own workmanship warranty in years with its own words; the proposal labels it "<business>'s workmanship warranty" with `warrantyNote` (RENUABL gives no workmanship warranty: this is the business's statement to its own customer). **Installation time** (`installation-time`): a choice from `INSTALL_TIMES`, always shown with `TIMING_NOTE` as an estimate. **Special conditions** (`conditions`): the business's own, in a block of their own on the page and PDF, accepted by a customer going ahead. **Terms and conditions** (`terms`, 18 Oct 2026, Martin: ours by default, or the business's own): one of `TERMS_CHOICES` (`BrandKit.terms`, `standardTerms` kept in step; `termsInUse` decides): ours (below), **the business's own** as a PDF it uploads (`/api/design/brand/terms`: a whole PDF by its own bytes, `isPdf`, up to 10 MB, kept in `design_files` kind `terms`; `ownTerms` on the kit is set only by the upload, never by a save; uploading chooses it, removing it goes back to ours; the published copy carries `view.ownTerms`, served only from that proposal's link at `/api/proposal/<token>/terms` as a download with a sandbox policy, `termsResponse`; the tick names them, `acceptWithOwnTerms`; the PDF says they go with it), or none. **Our standard terms** ( wording in `standard-terms.ts`, a draft for legal review that this slice never rewords): on for a business that hasn't chosen; when on, the published copy carries the terms as they stood with `STANDARD_TERMS_VERSION`, the names filled in, and the New Energy Tech consumer information (the "Approved Seller" line only when the business has ticked that it is one; we don't check it); the page shows them last behind "Read the terms and conditions" with a Details part built from the proposal's own figures (`contractDetails`), the PDF prints them in full, and the tick to go ahead names them (`acceptLine`). **Fonts** (`theme`): `FONTS`, two choices using faces the site already loads; the PDF uses the faces built into every PDF (Helvetica, Times for Editorial headings) and embeds no font file, so page and file are close, not identical. **Signed proposal emails** (`signed-emails`): up to five of the business's own addresses; when a customer accepts, `tellAccepted` (after the response, via `after()`) sends each a plain message **from RENUABL, never in the business's name and never to the customer**: who accepted, which job, a link to the deal, and nothing of the proposal (no price, no bank details). Only when email is set up (`canNotify`); the page says when it isn't. **Proposal customiser** (`customiser`): `BLOCKS` (cover, system, roof, output, savings, price, payment, installation time, warranty, about, three blocks of the business's own, going ahead, special conditions, standard terms); each is switched on or off, moved up or down, and given a heading, words and one picture (`design_files` kind `block`), except that the cover is always first, the standard terms always last and never reworded, and cover, price, going ahead, special conditions and standard terms can't be switched off; the notes that mark a figure as an estimate belong to their blocks and can't be edited. `cleanBlocks` always answers with a whole, valid list; the page and the PDF both render from `view.blocks` (`viewBlocks`), and a block with nothing in it is left out. **Users and permissions** (`users`): the owner may do everything; for each other member the owner sets `costs` (sees costs and margin), `prices` (changes prices and settings; needs `costs`) and `publish` (publishes and withdraws proposals), kept on `business_members.permissions` (null = all, as before). **Enforced on the server, in `can(role, action, permissions)` through `requireMember`, not by hiding buttons**: without `costs` the workspace is answered with every cost taken out (`withoutCosts`: products stay so they can design, price-list rows go, rates and margin are the placeholders) and `costsHidden`, a roof's quote edits are not sent (`withoutCostEdits`) and are kept as the business had them when that member saves (`keepCostEdits`); the designer then shows no quote or price and hands on no price when saving. An invitation is still the owner adding an email (no email is sent). **Billing** (`billing`, `BillingPanel` in billing-page.tsx, the owner only): the business's plan with RENUABL (status, users, monthly or yearly, trial end or next bill), its design jobs left with the ledger, buying a pack, and Stripe's own page for the card, invoices, users and cancelling (see RENUABL's plans, below).
- Roof designer chat (11 Oct 2026; `domain/design/chat.ts` tested, `server/design-chat-repo.ts`, `server/design-chat.ts`, `services/chat.ts`, `components/design/chat-panel.tsx`; `design-chat-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): a conversation between a business and its customer, one for each job, in its own table `design_messages` (business id first, tied to the job together with the business, gone with the job; not the design's comments, so it outlives a design being deleted or a proposal published again). It replaced the proposal's one-question box. **Customer side**: `CustomerChat` under "I'd like to go ahead" on `/p/<token>` (`id="chat"`), their own lines on the right under their first name, the business's on the left under "<Business> team", **never a member's name or email**; Enter sends; it asks again every `POLL_SECONDS` (20) while the page is in view. `/api/proposal/[token]/chat` is keyed only by the proposal's token and opens only that job's conversation; only the words are taken from the browser (`cleanLine`, `MAX_LINE` 2,000), the server decides who a line is from, a job takes at most `MAX_CUSTOMER_LINES` (200) from its customer, and both verbs are rate limited. What the customer is sent is `publicMessages` (id, side, words, time) under the key `messages` (never `lines`, one of the `COST_WORDS`), and a test checks it carries no cost word, member email or read state. A withdrawn proposal closes the chat (nothing shown, nothing taken); a lapsed or accepted one keeps it. **When a customer writes** (`customerSays`, each step on its own so one failing never loses the message): saved; unread for the business; written on the customer's record (activity kind `message`); the business told by email from RENUABL's sender (`chatMessageEmail`, to the brand's `notify` addresses, else the owner; only when email and `SITE_URL` are set up; after the answer has gone back). **Business side**: Messages in the left nav with an unread count (`/design/messages`, `MessagesPage`: conversations newest first, each opening its deal at `?tab=chat`, with "Mark read"), a Chat tab on the deal (`DealChat`: opening it in view or answering marks the customer's lines read), and a Chat button on the design tool's bar beside View proposal with a count while the customer's latest lines are unanswered (`unanswered`). Every member may reply (`requireMember("design")`); who wrote a reply is kept for the business only. **A reply is also emailed to the customer** (`memberReplies`, `chatReplyEmail`: RENUABL's sender, names the business, the message, a button to `/p/<token>#chat`, and "open your proposal to answer; a reply to this email isn't read") only when the business's `emailReplies` setting is on (default on; Settings → Emails), the customer has an email, email and `SITE_URL` are set up and a proposal is out; the member is told which (`replyOutcome`: "Sent, and emailed", "Sent. Not emailed: …"). There is no inbound email: nothing reads replies to those emails. **Sending a proposal with a message** (18 Oct 2026, Martin; `domain/design/send-message.ts`, tested): once published, the proposal screen's "Send to customer" opens the job's chat in a panel with a ready-written draft in the box (`SEND_TEMPLATES`: Friendly hello, Short and simple, After our visit or call, Updated proposal (offered first once a version has been sent before); filled in with the customer's first name, the business and the day the price stands until; no figure, promise or person's name), changed as wanted and sent as an ordinary reply (chat, record, the email with the link when replies are emailed).
