- **Installers and electrical inspectors join RENUABL (1 Nov 2026, Martin: the network; installers and inspectors join free; an installer nominates the inspector for a job, never the retailer; the directory is opt-in; installers may use the design tool, which charges them by the design job).** `domain/design/business-kind.ts` (tested). Every business has a **kind** (`businesses.kind`, `BUSINESS_KINDS`, `cleanKind`: anything unknown, and every business made before, is a **retailer**), chosen when the business is named (`SetUp` in design-account.tsx, "What does your business do?", each kind with what it is for and its price line; `/api/design/business` takes `kind`; `createBusiness(email, name, terms, kind)`). **A retailer** has everything, as before. **An installer** (free to join; design jobs at $3 each in packs, no user fee) has Today, **Jobs**, **Marketplace** and **Connections** (`KIND_SECTIONS` in sections.ts), the design tool's orange round, team chat, and settings for price lists, datasheets, parts, installation rates, margin, standards, users and billing (`settingsFor`). **An inspector** (free) has Today, **Inspections** and **Connections**, team chat, and settings for standards and users; no design tool (`designsRoofs`). Neither has the CRM, procurement, proposals, messages, the Gmail inbox, notifications, search or Sol (his answers read the CRM and cost credits). **Enforced on the server**: the proxy sets `PATH_HEADER` on every `/api/design` request (replacing any sent), and `requireMember` refuses an area not in the kind's list (`apiAllowed`, `KIND_REFUSAL`, 403); in the browser `DesignShell` sends a page that isn't the kind's (`pageAllowed`) back to `/design`, asks no CRM counts, and shows the kind's own foot bar on a phone (`KIND_TABS`). Their Today is `KindToday` (kind-home.tsx: what the login is for, a card each); `/design/jobs`, `/design/inspections` and `/design/connections` say plainly what comes next (`ComingNext`). The design tool's home asks no deals or deal heat for an installer and says "Design a site." **Connections** (step 2, 1 Nov 2026; `domain/design/connections.ts` tested, `server/design-connections-repo.ts`, `/api/design/connections`, `services/connections.ts`, `components/design/connections.tsx`; `design-connections-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): every kind has `/design/connections` (a retailer's under Installation), with an orange count of requests waiting on it (`?count=1`, refreshed on `renuabl:connections`). **The directory is opt-in** (`design_directory`, one row a business, made on first use): a business appears only once it ticks "List my business in the directory" and gives its suburb and state (`cleanListing`; an ABN when given is checked, `isAbn`, and finds it by its whole number); it shows its name, kind, suburb and state, never its people, emails, customers or jobs, and **another business is known only by its directory key**, never its id. Search (`searchDirectory`, `matchesSearch`: every word starts a word of the name, suburb or state; by kind: Everyone, Retailers, Installers, Inspectors; at least `MIN_QUERY` letters, `MAX_RESULTS`) lists listed businesses, not itself, each with where it stands (none, requested, incoming, connected). **Asking** (`askToConnect`, only a listed business, refused for itself or when a request or connection is already there, `requestRefusal`) makes one row a pair (`design_connections`, the lower id first, pending); the other business's owner is emailed from RENUABL (`requestEmail`: who asked and the way to answer) when email and `SITE_URL` are set up. **Only the business asked accepts or declines; only the asker cancels; either disconnects** (`answerConnection`, under a lock); accepting emails the asker's owner (`acceptedEmail`); declining, cancelling and disconnecting remove the row, so asking again is possible (rate limited). Changing the listing and every request or answer needs the `publish` permission. `areConnected` is for the steps that pass jobs. **Jobs sent to a connected installer** (step 3, 2 Nov 2026; `domain/design/job-shares.ts` tested, `server/design-job-shares-repo.ts`, `/api/design/jobs/[id]/share` (the retailer: GET, POST `{key}`, DELETE), `/api/design/installer-jobs` (the installer: GET, `?count=1`, POST `{id, do}`) and `/[id]`, `services/job-shares.ts`, `components/design/share-job.tsx` and `installer-jobs.tsx`; `design-job-shares-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): the retailer's job card has **Send on RENUABL** (`ShareJob`, above Email the job card): a connected installer chosen from a list (`installersToSendTo`: connected, kind installer, by directory key), refused unless connected, an installer, and the job not already with someone (`sendRefusal`; one live share a job, `design_job_shares`, unique while sent or accepted); then where it stands (`statusWords`) and **Take it back**. The installer's owner is emailed from RENUABL (`jobSentEmail`: who, the suburb, the day). The installer's **Jobs** (`/design/jobs`, with an orange count of jobs waiting) shows a job waiting with **the suburb, the day and the system only** (`jobsFor`, `SharedJobLine`) and Accept / Decline (`answerJob`, under a lock, only the installer it was sent to, only while still connected; `answerRefusal`); accepting names it the deal's installer and both are written on the retailer's record, and whoever sent it is emailed (`jobAnsweredEmail`). **Accepted, it opens at `/design/jobs/<share>` as the same job card as the installer's private link** (`openShared` hands the page the job card's own link, made by `installerLink` with installer id `biz:<business>`, `BUSINESS_LINK`; `InstallerJobCard framed`): the day, the address, the customer's name and phone (never their email), the drawings, one thread of notes and photos, the as-built. **`cardByToken` keeps such a link open only while the share is accepted and the two are connected** (`linkedBusiness`), so taking a job back, declining or disconnecting closes the card. Sending and answering need the `publish` permission. **The installer nominates the inspector** (step 4, 2 Nov 2026; `domain/design/inspections.ts` tested, `server/design-inspections-repo.ts`, `/api/design/installer-jobs/[id]/inspection` (the installer: GET, POST `{key}`, DELETE), `/api/design/inspections` (the inspector: GET, `?count=1`) with `/[id]` (GET the job and the inspection; POST a form of state, day, notes and certificate, or JSON `{do: "decline"}`), `/[id]/files/[fileId]`, `/[id]/roof` and `/[id]/certificate` (the retailer, installer or inspector of it only), `/api/design/jobs/[id]/inspection` (the retailer), `services/inspections.ts`, `components/design/inspections.tsx`; `design-inspections-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): on a job it accepted, the installer's card has **Inspection** (`NominateInspector`): a connected inspector from a list (`inspectorsToNominate`), refused unless the job is accepted, the inspector connected and one, and no live inspection on the job (`nominateRefusal`; `design_inspections`, one live a job), written on the retailer's record, the inspector's owner emailed (`nominatedEmail`); **Take it back** while nominated or booked. **The retailer never nominates.** The inspector's **Inspections** (`/design/inspections`, with an orange count to book) opens a job **read only** (`ReadOnlyJobCard` in job-card.tsx, `inspectionCard`): the day, the address, the customer's name and phone (never their email), the system, the drawings and every note and photo on the job card (served through the inspector's own addresses, `inspectionFile`), only while the inspection is live, the job's share still accepted and both pairs (retailer and installer, installer and inspector) still connected (`openFor`), so the installer taking it back, the retailer taking the job back or a disconnection closes it. The inspector records **Booked** (a day needed), **Passed** or **Defects found** (in words) with notes and **the certificate** (`cleanOutcome`; a PDF or photo under 4 MB checked by its own bytes, `certificateProblem`, `looksLike`; kept on the inspection, a later one replaces it), or **Declines** while nominated; each is written on the retailer's record and the installer and whoever sent the job are emailed (`outcomeEmail`). The retailer's job card shows it under Send on RENUABL (`InspectionStatus`: `inspectionWords`, the notes, the certificate, who nominated). Nominating and recording need the `publish` permission. **The Marketplace for all three kinds** (step 5, 2 Nov 2026): while it is closed every kind sees the launch page; once open, a retailer has all of it, an **installer** bids on job listings and trades equipment but lists nothing (no My listings: it has no deals; `NOT_PAGES` in business-kind.ts), and an **inspector** trades equipment only (its Marketplace starts at Equipment; `marketplaceFor` in sections.ts; the API lists give it `trade`, never `marketplace`). Trading gained a **Test equipment** kind. **Your base** for distances is asked for in place on Job listings and Equipment when none is marked (`BaseNote` in marketplace.tsx, `/api/design/base`: GET, PUT `{base}` with the `prices` permission, the same base the Job map keeps, every kind). **A bid won by an installer is the job sent and accepted on RENUABL** (`awardBid`): the two businesses are connected (choosing a bid is working together), any earlier share of the job ends, and an accepted share is made, so the job is in the installer's Jobs as its job card at once (as-built, inspector nomination); the winner's listing shows **Open the job card** (`ListingDetail.handover.jobCard`) and its email links there (`awardedEmail` `jobCard`). A job already with an installer on RENUABL can't be listed (`createListing`). A retailer that wins a bid gets the address and contact as before, no job card. Tested in `business-kind.test.ts` and `design-marketplace-db.test.ts`. **Billing by kind** (2 Nov 2026; `billingParts`/`billsFor` in business-kind.ts, tested): a retailer is billed for its plan, Sol credits and design-job packs; an **installer for design-job packs only** (Settings → Billing shows `PACKS_ONLY_NOTE`, its design jobs left, the ledger, the packs, its tax invoices and billing details: no plan, users, trial or Sol); an inspector has no Billing. **Enforced on the server**: `/api/design/billing` answers an installer `parts`, no plan or Sol, and refuses `portal` and `sol` (403); `/api/design/subscribe` refuses an email already in a business that pays no user fee (`NO_PLAN_FOR_KIND`, 409); `claimSubscriptions` never attaches a subscription to a non-retailer (no trial jobs either). A business with no plan gets a Stripe customer of its own the first time it buys a pack (`ensureBillingCustomer` in `server/design-billing-customer.ts`, table `design_stripe_customers`), so its pack invoices list and its tax invoices open (`billingCustomer`: the plan's customer first). **Polish for the installer's login** (2 Nov 2026): Today's tiles say what waits (jobs to answer, inspections to book, requests to connect); inside the login a job card knows the member (their email's handle, `person` from `/api/design/installer-jobs/[id]`) and doesn't ask for a name; the design tool's home asks no CRM for an installer (it waits for the account before asking, so no 403s), searches addresses only and offers no Put on a deal; the designer for an installer (`RoofDesigner sitesOnly`) has no Publish, no Proposal view and no Ask Sol about this design (Ask Sol to change it stays: it is the design tool's own helper). **My Jobs and the installer's menu** (2 Nov 2026, Martin): an installer's menu is **My Jobs · the designer (orange round) · Marketplace**, with Connections in the gear menu (`MY_JOBS` and `KIND_SECTIONS` in sections.ts; phone tabs My Jobs, Designer, Marketplace, Connections). `/design` for an installer is My Jobs (`MyJobsPage` in my-jobs.tsx; `/design/jobs` redirects there, a job's card stays at `/design/jobs/<id>`): jobs waiting for an answer (Accept / Decline), then the accepted jobs for **Today / Tomorrow / This week / This month** (`JOB_PERIODS`, `periodDays`: a week Monday to Sunday, the calendar month, the market's today; the choice kept on the device), each a sheet (`jobSheet` in `domain/design/my-jobs.ts`, tested; `installerSheets` in design-job-shares-repo.ts, `GET /api/design/installer-jobs?sheets=1`): the roof from above with the panel layout (`/api/design/installer-jobs/[id]/roof`, only for the installer it was sent to while connected), the retailer, the job address, the customer's name and phone (never their email), the system (solar kW, battery kWh, EV charger), each product as qty × make and model (`kitLines`: from the design, else what was typed on the deal), single or double storey and the roof profile (`roofProfile`: Tin, Flat, Klip-lok when typed so, Tile), and **Equipment collection** from the retailer's order (`collectionOf`: each wholesaler, its SO number else the PO reference, its collection point from the retailer's Job map); reading never opens the retailer's order (`jobOrder(…, false)`). Jobs with no day are listed under them. **The Marketplace's toggles** are Job listings, **Buy & sell** (equipment trading's name since 2 Nov 2026, for every kind) and **Messages** (`/design/marketplace/messages`, `MarketplaceMessagesPage`: every trade conversation the business is in, as seller or buyer, newest first, unread counted, `tradeThreads`, `GET /api/design/trade?threads=1`); for an installer My bids and My equipment are links inside Job listings and Buy & sell; a retailer keeps My listings, My bids and My equipment as toggles too. **Staff view of kinds**: `/admin` → Design tool businesses lists every business with its kind, the day it joined and its people, with counts by kind (`countByKind`, tested; `listDesignBusinesses` in `server/design-admin-repo.ts`, the one read across businesses, read only, staff only). **Not built yet**: rating each other after a job; a subscription bought by someone whose business is then named an installer stays unattached (cancel it in Stripe).
- **Email: each member's own Gmail (1 Nov 2026, Martin: Primero's copied across; wave 7).** `domain/design/inbox.ts` (tested), `server/gmail.ts`, `server/design-inbox-repo.ts` (tables `design_gmail`, `design_email_talks`, `design_email_notes`, business id first), `/api/auth/gmail` and `/callback`, `/api/design/inbox`, `services/inbox.ts`, `components/design/inbox.tsx`, `/design/inbox`; `design-inbox-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`. **An envelope in the top card** (with Gmail's own unread count, asked each minute) and Email in the phone's More sheet. **Each member connects their own Gmail once** (Connect Gmail: Google's sign-in for `gmail.modify` (`GMAIL_SCOPE`; read, and labels changed, never sending; it was `gmail.readonly` until 5 Nov 2026) with openid email, suggested their own address, offline access; the same Google client as Continue with Google, **with `<site>/api/auth/gmail/callback` added as an authorised redirect and the Gmail API enabled on that project**; the refresh and access tokens kept encrypted (AES-256-GCM keyed from `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`), never sent to a browser or logged; Disconnect tells Google to forget it). Until Google reviews RENUABL, only test accounts can connect (the card says so). **One or more mailboxes** (5 Nov 2026, Martin): a person may connect up to `MAX_MAILBOXES` (5) Gmails, say their own and sales@ (`design_gmail` keyed by business, member and address; an older table keyed by member alone gains the address on start-up), each by **Add another Gmail** (`/api/auth/gmail?add=1`: Google asks which account, no hint; past the limit `?gmail=full`); the inbox reads them all as one (`inboxMessages` → `mergeInboxes`, newest first, a mailbox that stops answering left out while the others show), each email carrying its mailbox (`InboxEmail.box`, "to …" under the sender while all are shown), opened and its files read from that mailbox (`?box=`), the unread count is all of them together, and a row of chips picks one mailbox or All and disconnects one (`DELETE ?address=`). **Connected then and there** (5 Nov 2026, Martin: never leave the page to connect): Connect with Google and Add another Gmail open Google in a small window over the page (`connectGmailHere` in services/inbox.ts, `?popup=1`, the state carrying ".p"); the way back is a closing page that tells the inbox how it went (`postMessage`, same site only, fixed outcomes) and shuts, and the inbox refreshes where it is, on Today's tab or its own page; a blocked window falls back to going to Google and back. **Inbox is also a tab on Today** (`TODAY_TABS`, between Today and Calendar, Gmail's unread count on it, the same page inside the pad). **The inbox**: the newest 30 in INBOX, grouped by the market's day (Today, Yesterday, This week, the month; `dayGroup`), each row the sender's initial (orange with a deal, ink for a person, a bot for a machine), the sender, **their deal** (matched by the sender's address to the business's customers, the newest deal the member may see), the subject and a line of it, an orange bar when unread, a count of notes; machines' emails (no-reply, notifications, bounces, DMARC; `isAutomated`) marked Automated and drawn quieter; All / Customers / Unread / Automated and a search over those shown; Refresh. **An email opens over the list**: the subject large, the sender, when, the deal as a chip, Open in Gmail, **the email as sent** in a sandboxed frame (nothing in it runs; links open in a new tab; its own `cid:` pictures put in place, up to 8 and 4 MB, `withPictures`) or its words, and its files to download (from the member's own mailbox only). Beside it, **Notes on this email**: only the member and anyone tagged see them (`mayReadTalk`); **@tagging a teammate shares that one email with them** (a copy of its sender, subject, date and words up to 8,000 characters, kept with the notes when the first note is written; never the mailbox), shown under **Shared with you** at the top of their inbox whether or not their own Gmail is connected; and **Sol**: Summarise (for the team, in three parts, `SUMMARY_INSTRUCTIONS` with a structured answer cleaned by `cleanEmailSummary`: In short (the takeaway, one sentence, larger), They're asking (left out when nothing), and Next step in a sun-tinted box, else "Nothing to do"; `SummaryParts`, 8 Nov 2026, Martin: it read as one block) or Draft a reply (Copy, then send from the deal's Email tab or Gmail); nothing is sent and nothing Sol writes is kept (120 an hour). **Read, unread and archive, the same in Gmail** (5 Nov 2026, Martin): opening an email marks it read in Gmail (the GET of one email, where its mailbox allows it), and the open email has **Archive** (e) and **Mark unread** (Shift+U), both going back to the inbox, with an archive round on each row on hover (POST `{do: "read" | "unread" | "archive", id, box}`, `changeEmail` in gmail.ts: Gmail's `modify`, only labels, `labelChange`; the list updated in place, `afterAction`; the shell's count told by `renuabl:inbox`). Each mailbox keeps whether Google granted modify (`design_gmail.can_modify`, `canChangeMail`); one connected before is read only and refused (`ReadOnlyMailbox`), and the inbox and the email say "Connect again" for it. **Compose** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: a new email from scratch): beside Refresh, or C; a card above the list with From (the mailboxes that may send, the one picked first), To, Cc, a subject and the words (with the microphone), sent through the member's own Gmail (`sendFromGmail`, Gmail's `messages/send`, which `gmail.modify` allows; POST `{do: "send", box, to, cc, subject, body}`, 60 an hour) so it comes from their address and sits in their Sent; `cleanCompose` checks the addresses (up to 10 each), keeps the subject to one line so nothing typed adds a header, and `rawEmail` builds the plain-text UTF-8 email (tested in inbox.test.ts); a read-only mailbox can't send and says Connect again; never while viewing as someone. **Pictures, files, links, size and Sol** (8 Nov 2026, Martin): a toolbar over the words (`EmailFormatBar plus`: one **+** for pictures and files, bullets, numbering, symbols; then a link round what is picked, `withLink`/`cleanLinkUrl` (web and mail links only), kept as [words](address) and made real in the HTML; the text size for the whole email, `EMAIL_SIZES`; **Polish** and **Sol** on the right): a screenshot pastes or drops straight in (`useEmailFiles`, shrunk to fit, 6 files and 4 MB together as the deal email), pictures go inside the email under the words and other files are attached; the email is HTML and plain text (`composeHtml`, `plainBody`, `rawEmail` as multipart, tested) sent through Gmail's upload address as message/rfc822 (`sendFromGmail`), the form checked again on the server (`formFiles`). **Polish** has Sol tidy the words (quick tier) and **Sol** takes what the email should say, typed or spoken, and writes it (deep tier; POST `{do: "compose-sol", mode}`, `composeHelpPrompt`: never a figure, price, date, product or promise not in their words or request, no sign-off name; `cleanComposeHelp`), one `inbox` credit as other inbox answers, written into the box with **Undo**; nothing is sent until Send. **Compact** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: see more of the inbox in one fold): a switch beside Refresh makes the list smaller (rows 37 px from 53, 13 px type, a smaller initial, the deal kept in the row before the subject, no second line), kept on the device (`renuabl.design.inbox-compact`). Not written on any deal's record. Not tried against Gmail or Claude from this machine: VERIFY one email with a pasted screenshot and a PDF. Replying to an email stays in Gmail or the deal; nothing is ever deleted. While the owner views as someone, that person's mailbox isn't read. - **Each member's own calendar from the date tile (5 Nov 2026, Martin: Google Calendar or Outlook, connected without leaving the page).** `domain/design/my-calendar.ts` (tested: `cleanEvents` for Google, `cleanOutlookEvents` for Outlook, `CONNECT_RESULTS`), `server/my-calendar.ts` (table `design_calendar` with `provider`, one calendar a member, kept as `design_gmail` is: tokens sealed with gmail.ts's `seal`; Outlook's new refresh token kept on every refresh), `/api/auth/calendar` (`?provider=google|outlook&popup=1&back=`) and `/callback`, ending in `done.ts` (`calendarDone`), `/api/design/calendar` (GET `?from&to`, DELETE), `services/my-calendar.ts` (`connectMyCalendar`), `components/design/my-calendar.tsx`. Pressing the date and weather tile (`DayWeather`) opens a calendar over the page: the month, Monday first, today ringed orange, a dot under days with something on, and the picked day's events (read only, only they see it; time, place, Join call for Meet or Teams, Open in Google or Outlook), Disconnect at its foot. **Month or Day** at its top (kept on the device, `renuabl.design.calendar-view`; "Day view" beside the picked day's events): Day is that day by the hour (`DayTimeline`, laid out by `dayLayout`: all-day events above, timed ones at their time and length, at least `MIN_BLOCK_MINUTES` tall, overlapping ones side by side, a line at the time now on today, opened just before the first event, else 7am; `dayStartHour`), the arrows stepping a day. With none connected it shows **Google Calendar** and **Outlook** buttons: one press opens that sign-in in a small window over the page, which tells the page how it went (`postMessage` to our own site, `CALENDAR_MESSAGE`) and closes itself, and the events fill in; nobody leaves the page. Only if the browser blocks the window does the page go to sign in and come back (`?calendar=`, read by `DayWeather`, which opens the calendar). Outlook is read through Microsoft Graph `calendarView` with its times in Melbourne's zone (`OUTLOOK_TIME_ZONE`). Every kind of business has it (`calendar` in `COMMON_API`); never while viewing as someone. **Google** needs `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` with the Google Calendar API enabled; **Outlook** needs a Microsoft Entra app (work and personal accounts) as `MICROSOFT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` with the delegated Graph permission Calendars.Read; both with `<site>/api/auth/calendar/callback` as a redirect. Until set up, pressing a button says that provider's sign-in isn't set up. Not run against Google or Microsoft from this machine: VERIFY with test accounts. **Sol books meetings in it (16 Nov 2026, Martin: "i need sol to be able to create calendar events, create a google meet")**: Sol's tool `book_meeting` (design-sol.ts; `domain/design/sol-meeting.ts`, tested) puts an event on the asking member's own Google Calendar (`bookMeeting` in server/my-calendar.ts: `events.insert` with `conferenceDataVersion=1` for a Google Meet link and `sendUpdates=none`, **no guests, so Google emails nobody**) and hands back the Meet link (`meetLinkOf`: only meet.google.com links) for the member to send the customer themselves (Text on the deal's chat, or an email); with a deal it is noted on the deal's record by Sol. Only when the member's own words ask for a meeting (`asksForMeeting` on the question, so nothing a customer wrote books one), only for a time they gave or that was plainly agreed (`cleanMeeting`: the market's local time, to come, within a year, 10 to 240 minutes, 30 when not said; with no time he asks), at most two an answer, never while viewing as someone. Works in his panel, Today and @Sol on a deal's chat. The Google scope is now `calendar.events` (read and add; nothing is changed or deleted): **a calendar connected before reads on but must be connected again to book** (Sol says so), and **the scope must be added to the Google project's OAuth consent screen**. Outlook isn't booked into yet (Sol says so). Not run against Google from this machine: VERIFY one booking with a Meet link. **A card to confirm, then reminders (17 Nov 2026, Martin: the Meet link had to be copied and pasted; "how could sol… remind me of the call?… 1 hour, 30 mins, 10 minutes before"; he had asked for today and Sol booked it, only then saying Mike agreed tomorrow: "sol should have first replied with a decision card… yes / no and if i click yes, he then has me reconfirm the time")**: over the paragraph above where they differ, `book_meeting` no longer books: it keeps a proposal (`proposeMeeting` in `server/design-meetings.ts`, table `design_meetings`, business id first; checks a Google Calendar is connected first) and puts **a card** under Sol's answer, in his own chat (`SolTurn.meeting`) and on a deal's team chat (`design_deal_lines.meeting`, `DealLine.meeting`); `MeetingCard` in meeting-card.tsx reads it from `/api/design/meetings/[id]` (GET; POST `book`, `drop`, `remind`; only the member whose calendar it goes in acts on it, a deal's card only for who may see the deal, never while viewing as someone). When the customer agreed a different day or time (Sol reads the deal first and passes `agreed_with_customer`, `agreed_day`, `cleanAgreed`), the card asks first: "The customer agreed to … Still book it for …?" **Yes, keep it** / **No, change it** (the agreed day put in); then the day and time to confirm and **Book it** / Not now; booked (`bookProposed`, noted on the deal), **Join Google Meet** to press and Open in Calendar, then **Want a reminder before the call?** 1 hour / 30 min / 10 min (`remindChoices`: only those still to come), kept as a reminder in the member's own chat with Sol with the link in it (`remindBefore`, `reminderWhat`). **Links are pressable**: https addresses in a deal chat line (`linkRuns`) and in Sol's words (`solRuns` kind `link`) open in a new tab. - **The call note-taker and View as (1 Nov 2026, Martin: Primero's copied across; wave 7).** **Note-taker** (on the deal under Email; `domain/design/call-notes.ts` tested, `components/design/call-notetaker.tsx`, `/api/design/jobs/[id]/call-notes`): switches the computer's microphone on for a call taken on the phone, through the browser's own speech recognition (Chrome and Edge send the sound to Google or Microsoft to turn into words; others have none and the button says so). **It hears the member's side only because the call is on their phone, to their ear or a headset, never on speaker**: the bar says so. Nothing is recorded or kept: the words live in the page until written up. A dark bar at the foot shows what is heard, with **Add a note** / **Add a design note** (what is said until Note done is marked), a box to type one, and **End and write up** (from `MIN_WORDS`, 8): Claude (`callNotesPrompt`: their side only, numbers as kW and kWh, never invent a figure, product, price, date or promise) returns the summary, the conversation for the recap email, what to send, the next step, what to check, then the intended system and design notes (`callNotesText`), which the member checks and adds to the deal as a call. Needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; 60 write-ups an hour. **It sums up as it listens** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: not word for word, the salient points, arranged at the end to inform the proposal, design and price): the bar shows "So far", up to 8 short labelled points (Bill:, Battery:, Worry:, Timing: …), re-summarised after at least 40 new words and 20 seconds, never while a request is out (`/api/design/jobs/[id]/call-notes/points`, the quick model, 240 an hour a member, **no Sol credit**; `services/call-points.ts`; the previous points sent back so the list stays steady), the words heard behind "What was heard"; without Claude, over the limit or on a failure it shows the words as before and says so. The write-up groups its result under **Design**, **Proposal** (`forProposal`), **Price** (`forPrice`), **Next step** and **To check** after the summary and "From the conversation" (`callNotesText`; a line that only repeats a system field is dropped). Not yet tried on a real call: the live points and their timing. **On and off by hand, typed up live** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: only a fragment of a call was captured): the bar opens with the microphone off; **Start listening** / **Pause** / **Listen again** switch it (Sol's points are asked only while it is on); what is said is typed into the bar as it is said (`LiveWords`: the words still being made out lighter, a caret while on, kept scrolled to the latest), with Sol's points under it; when Chrome's recogniser stops itself after a pause a fresh one is started 250 ms later and the half-heard words are kept as a line (`keepHearing`), where restarting the same one from onend used to fail and leave it deaf. Not tried with a real microphone here (the Browser pane blocks it): VERIFY on a real call. **View as** (`domain/design/view-as.ts` tested, `/api/design/view-as`, `src/proxy.ts`): **the owner only**, from Users and permissions (**View as** on each member who has joined), opens the whole product as that member sees it (their role, permissions, the deals they may see, Today), with an orange bar over every page saying whose view it is and **Back to my own view**. `designAccess` honours the cookie (`renuabl_view_as`, httpOnly, an email only, 8 hours) only for an owner and only for someone in their own business (`mayViewAs`), giving the member with `viewedBy`; **it is read only**: the proxy on `/api/design/*` refuses every change while the cookie is set (`passesWhileViewing`: reading, and DELETE on the view-as route to go back), so nothing is saved, sent or published in someone else's name.
- **Updates and Feedback (1 Nov 2026, Martin: Primero's copied across; wave 7).** Both under the gear (with Settings and Your account) and in the phone's More sheet. **Updates** (`/design/updates`, `domain/design/updates.ts` tested, `UpdatesPage` in updates-feedback.tsx): what has changed in RENUABL, a day a row, newest first, each change a few words with where it applies, an orange point on the newest day; **written by hand: add a line to `UPDATES` with each change someone using the product would notice**, in plain words, never a file or function name (tested). A dot on the gear and a count in its menu say how many came in since that member last opened it (`unseenUpdates`; `design_updates_seen`, `/api/design/updates`; never opened, the newest day's). **Feedback** (`/design/feedback`, `domain/design/feedback.ts` tested, `server/design-feedback-repo.ts`, tables `design_feedback` and `design_feedback_comments`, `/api/design/feedback`): anyone in the business raises an Improvement, Request or Issue (up to 2,000 characters) and comments on any (Enter sends, 100 an item); **only the owner ticks one off** (ticked again to reopen), and only the person who raised it or the owner removes it; To do / Done / All, open first. - **Approvals before booking, and the install calendar's capacity (1 Nov 2026, Martin: Primero's copied across; wave 7).** `domain/design/approvals.ts` (tested), `/api/design/approvals` (`?count=1` for the badges), `components/design/approvals.tsx`, `/design/procurement/pre-approvals`, `/solar-victoria`, `/book`. Procurement now reads **Pre-approvals, Solar Victoria, Book an installation**, Ordering station, Job map, each gate with an orange count (refreshed on `renuabl:approvals`). Every sold job (won, ordered, scheduled) with no installation date is filed at its **first gate not passed, in order** (`gateOf`): the network's pre-approval (`preApproval`: Approved or Approved, export limited is through; nothing filled in is Not submitted), then Solar Victoria (`solarVicStatus`: Eligibility to check and Applied wait; nothing filled in is Not applying), then booking; so a job approved during the sale goes straight past. Each row shows both approvals in words (green through, amber waiting, red missing or declined) with a dropdown to set each where it stands (written to the deal's details, so the job moves on), the longest waiting since sold first; Book an installation opens the deal to set its date. The whole team's, like the calendar. **Capacity** (`capacityWeeks`): the install calendar shows the next six weeks (This week, Next week, w/c …) as booked over what the business can do (`BusinessPrefs.weeklyCapacity`, Settings → Installers, 5 to start as a placeholder; `cleanCapacity`), green once full, the weeks on screen outlined; pressing one opens that week.
- **The installation job card, in place of ServiceM8 (1 Nov 2026, Martin: Primero's copied across; wave 7).** `domain/design/job-card.ts` (tested), `server/design-job-card-repo.ts` (tables `design_job_links`, `design_job_notes`, `design_job_files`, business id first), `server/design-job-card.ts`, `/api/design/jobs/[id]/card` (GET, POST a note as a form, PUT `{send}`) and `/files/[fileId]`, `/api/job/[token]` (GET, POST) with `/files/[fileId]` and `/roof`, `/job/[token]`, `services/job-card.ts`, `components/design/job-card.tsx`; `design-job-card-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`. **Clicking an installation on the Install calendar opens its job card over the page** (`JobCardDialog`, drawn on the page body; **Calendar event** in it opens the event card as before); the deal page has **Job card** too. The card: the install day and stage, the address (to Google Maps), the customer (phone to tap; email for the team only), the system lines filled in (`systemDetails` from the deal and its design: the system the customer chose when they signed, else the primary), **the panel layout and the string design drawn over the roof picture** (`jobDrawing`: the layout with INV, BAT and MSB marks where the design puts them; the strings filled in the designer's own colours (`STRING_COLOURS`), lettered A, B, C at the first panel with the wiring traced; cropped close on the panels; SVG made from numbers and fixed words only), the strings in words (`stringsInWords`), and **one thread of notes and photos** both sides add to, newest first (photos and PDFs, up to six and 4 MB each, made smaller in the browser first; every note also written on the deal's record). **Installers on file** (Settings → After the sale → **Installers**: name, email, phone; `BusinessPrefs.installers`, `cleanInstallers`, ids made on the server). **Email the job card** goes only to an installer on file (chosen, never typed: nothing typed decides where a customer's details go), from RENUABL's sender in the business's name, reply-to the member, with the details, the customer's name and phone (never their email), the strings, both drawings as PNG attachments (sharp, over the roof picture) and their link (`jobCardEmail`); the card says when it last went to each and a note says who sent it. Needs email and `SITE_URL` (it says so when not). **The installer's card is `/job/<48 hex>`**: one link for each installer on each job, for good (`installerLink`), opening only that job and **only while that installer is still on file** (`cardByToken`: taking them off the list closes their links); no sign-in, noindex, no referrer, rate limited, `/job/` disallowed in robots, no ad tags; never the customer's email. They give their name once (kept on their phone) and add notes and photos, named "Jo (Sparks Electrical)" (`installerBy`), shown in the pale sun colour. A file is served only from its own job's card. The dispatch board stays the Install calendar. **As built (2 Nov 2026, Martin: design through to as-built)**: the job card (the team's and the installer's link alike) has an As built part (`AsBuiltPanel` in job-card.tsx; `domain/design/as-built.ts`, tested) started from the design (`asBuiltFrom`: panels, panel, inverters, battery) for what was really installed: the install date, counts and models, every panel serial, each inverter's serial and each battery module's (`serialsOf`: one a line, cleaned, no repeats), notes; it shows how it differs from the design (`asBuiltDifferences`) and what is still to record (`asBuiltMissing`). Kept in `design_as_built` (business id first, one a job; `saveAsBuilt`/`asBuiltOf` in design-job-card-repo.ts; `PUT /api/design/jobs/[id]/as-built` for the team, `PUT /api/job/[token]/as-built` for the installer, named for them), written on the deal's record once with the differences. The design itself isn't changed: the as-built is the job's warranty and service file beside it. `design-job-card-db.test.ts` covers it. **The as-built on every job, and the card shows what was installed (22 Nov 2026, Martin, on a battery job with no roof design whose card had no as-built: "the job card always maintains what was actually installed… a future point of reference")**: the As built part is on every job card, design or not. With no design it starts from the system typed on the deal (`plannedFromDetails` in as-built.ts, tested: panel and count, inverter and its count, additional inverter, battery, modules and storage, EV charger; `Designed.from` "deal", whose differences say "planned"), else from nothing (`NOTHING_PLANNED`). The as-built now also keeps **the EV charger and its serial** (`AsBuilt.charger`) and **the battery's storage in kWh** (`battery.kwh`); both are optional, so older records still read. Inverters, a battery and a charger can be added or taken away there. **Once kept, the card's System part is "As installed"** (`asBuiltLines`: the install date, models with serials, storage, how many serials are kept, and who recorded it), with the plan folded under "As planned on the deal". On an installed job with nothing recorded, the part reads "System as planned · Not yet confirmed as installed", and the as-built is ringed with a prompt to record it (`JobCardView.job.installed`). The as-built is kept apart from the design, so a later change to the design never changes it. The product watch reads its charger too. Not seen in the browser: VERIFY on Con Hanzzopoulos's job.

Signed out, the introduction and the device's roofs as before. A roof opened from the home is kept in the address (`?at=lat,lng&address=…`) so a reload opens it again; the roof last opened no longer opens by itself. **The designer sits in the frame's pad** like every other page (`DesignShell` without `compact`; bare inside a deal's drawer); its bar is a white card in the pad, no longer stuck to the window, with **Design home** at its start. **Beside the picture, one view at a time** (`SIDE_VIEWS` in roof-designer.tsx, chosen in the bar, pressing the one open folds the column away; kept on the device as `renuabl.design.side`; every view stays on the page, only hidden, so the quote keeps working out the figures): **Designer** (roof, storeys and phase as choices, `HomeToggles` in design-quote.tsx, which the quote no longer shows (`homeless`); Ask Sol about this design (`SOL_TOGGLE`); the deal's notes with a box to add one, `DealNotes` in designer-side.tsx), **Quote** (the quote builder; not for someone who can't see costs), **Proposal** (View proposal; the chat with the customer, `DealChat`, with a count on the tab while the customer waits, `useChatWaiting`; then **the timeline**, `ProposalTimeline`, `/api/design/jobs/[id]/timeline`, `timelineOf` in `domain/design/timeline.ts` tested: newest first, each publish an orange point with what went out option by option (system and price), then opened, signed, taken back, the deposit, the chat lines and the emails sent from the deal as small points; built from the versions, the deal's record and its conversation, nothing new stored; the record's own "Proposal sent" line isn't drawn twice), or for a site with no deal, why there is none. Publish design stays in the bar. **The column lies over the picture** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: the picture as large as the first fold allows): folded away by default, a handle on the right edge (Designer, Quote, Proposal written down it) brings it out when hovered or pressed (`peek`), and it folds again when the pointer leaves it (not while typing in it), with its cross or Escape; Q, D and the Fix buttons bring it out too. **Save** in the bar (and Ctrl+S, even while typing) saves without publishing; **Make primary** beside the designs makes the open design the primary in one press. **In the pop-up** (`tight`, 8 Nov 2026, Martin: no menu bar, the map taller and wider, the buttons down one side): the bar is a rail of rounds down the left of the picture (the customer's initials to open the deal, a map pin to find another address, Save, the draft's discard, Ask Sol, Ready to install with its count, Make primary, the designs as 2/3, Share live, the chat, Publish; each says what it is on hover and opens its box to the right), the figures strip is one slim line (kW and panels, kWh a day, battery kWh; bill now and after, saving, payback), and the designs are flicked through on the map under the kit (‹ Design 2 of 3 ›). **Folded into rounds** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: too many controls, "like a swiss army knife", opening like a stack on the Mac Dock): `ToolFan` (tool-fan.tsx) is a round whose legs come out one after another on hover or a press and fold when the pointer leaves (unless pressed open or a box in a leg is open, `hold`). Beside the window, a **Quote** round under Design and Proposal brings the designer's quote out in one press (`?side=quote`, or told by message when the designer is open), and a proposal that can't be sent links to **Open the quote**. Then (8 Nov 2026, Martin: fewer icons on the map): in the pop-up the ⚙ round is the one knife, its controls coming out down the left and the drawing tools (string, trace, guide line, undo, redo, the whole picture, a label) out to the right (`ToolFan` `extra`), so the map keeps only Select, Add a panel and Erase, the arrays, the kit, the pitch, the move round and the dock's comment, shade and Design it for me; the designs are flicked through at the end of the figures strip; the rounds beside the window go Design, Quote, Proposal; the quote opens across most of the picture with its groups side by side (`DesignQuote wide`), and its round (or the Quote handle) toggles it, to flick between drawing and pricing. The knife is a card, not loose rounds (8 Nov 2026, Martin: the rounds had no names and looked like the blocks under them): `KnifeMenu` opens beside the ⚙ round with a row for each control and its name (`data-leg`, else its label or title), under This design and Draw. **A traced roof face can be taken away**: the eraser pressed inside it, Delete with it picked, or Delete this face on its dial (`removeFace`, its row panels and their string places with it). **The home is set once for every design on the roof** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: phase, storeys and the roof toggled per design, and costs decided on one design left the other designs' proposals blocked): the quote form (phase, storeys, roof, pitch, Solar Victoria) and the decision on each cost decided on each job (`DECIDED_LINES`) are shared, `sharedHome`/`withHomeOf` in quote.ts; a roof whose designs differ is brought into line with its primary as it opens, with Save lit. **As Primero's (1 Nov 2026, Martin)**: the bar is one row (Design home, search, address, saved, designs, Publish); Designer / Quote / Proposal are tabs at the top of the column, which always shows (a tab no longer folds it); the picture is sized to the first fold, measured from the figures strip to the foot of the window (`fitPx`, `stripBox`); the wheel alone scrolls the page, Ctrl or ⌘ with it (or a pinch) zooms, and a plain wheel says so (`wheelHint`); a right click on the picture opens every tool where the pointer is (`toolMenu`: Make portrait / Make landscape first when panels are picked, a panel not picked is picked first). **The roof pitch** (`QuoteForm.pitch`, `pitchSet`, kept by the quote cleaner): until a person sets it for the design, a card asks for it after a moment (`PitchFirst`: stepper, quick picks, Set, or It's a flat roof), then a small roof-angle mark on the left edge changes it (`RoofPitchMark`); new panels in the open and new traced faces start at it (`homePitch`; a flat roof's lie flat at 0°). **Add a panel opens a card** ("New panels go down": Portrait and Landscape drawn with their cells, `PanelDrawing`, and Follow the roof face (or the panel beside it), `addLandscape` null, the default); with panels picked it lays them that way instead. It replaced the fan of bubbles. **The customer's name in the bar opens their deal** (an orange "<name> · Open deal"): inside the deal's drawer it folds the designer away to show the deal (`tellDeal({ fold: true })`, heard by `DesignDrawer`); on its own it goes to the deal page. **Edges and tilt**: panels let go near a traced face's edge or a guide line, within 8° of square, turn square to it and sit at the wind clearance, centred between the face's ends when nearly so (`settleToEdges`/`applySettle` in `domain/design/edge-gravity.ts`, tested; not when joined to other panels, not with Alt); an array just laid offers its tilt (− N° + Keep) at the start of the turn buttons while it is picked (`tiltPop`). Opens zoomed in (`OPEN_ZOOM` 1.6); the hand may drag the view up to half past the picture's edges (`within`); product lists show each model number (`ModelNo`) and fit the picture's height (`--pic-h`); datasheets match product codes written differently (`codeKey`). **The bill box in kWh** (`domain/design/design-bill.ts`, tested): typing kWh works the dollars out at the rate plus the supply charge and typing dollars clears it (`billWith`, `BillFields.usage`), with the kWh a dollar bill comes to as a hint (`usageFromAmount`); on a deal, **Find it in the customer's file** (and opening an empty bill box, once a visit) has Sol read the deal's details, notes, calls, emails and chat (`server/design-usage.ts`, `/api/design/jobs/[id]/usage`, needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, 60 an hour) and put the figure in with the words he found it in, to check before saving; he never works a figure out. **The picked-panel dial is graphite with an orange ring**, with Portrait and Landscape inside it; a newly traced face's pitch pulses in orange until changed or kept (`pitchDue`). **A chat round** in the bar lights orange with the count while the customer waits and opens the Proposal view. A deal with no place but whose customer has one offers "Design it at the customer's address" in one press. **A Designer role** (`MemberRole` "designer", `ROLES`, `cleanRole` in account.ts, tested; the owner picks Member or Designer for each person on Users and permissions, `setRole`, `PUT /api/design/members {email, role}`): designs, publishes and talks to the customer, **sees every deal** whatever `allDeals` says (`seesAllDeals`), never changes prices or settings or people; whether they see costs is the owner's "Sees costs and margin" as for anyone. **Designers land on the design tool**: `/design` sends a designer arriving from outside the app (signing in, a bookmark, a new tab: no referrer from a `/design/` page other than sign-in) to `/design/roof`; Today is still a press away.
- **The design home's waiting deals are compact rows (20 Nov 2026, Martin: "can you make the waiting on a design cards smaller. they take up too much of the page. redesign so theat they are more efficient cards"; over what the note above says about the queue as a feed of posts with the roof wide).** `FeedPost` in design-tool-home.tsx is one row a deal, about 60 px tall (it was about 370), 8 px apart: a small roof thumbnail (a deal on the map) or the customer's initials (one that isn't), "<name> wants solar" on one line, under it when it came in, the address (cut short when room runs out) and the latest sign from them, then the deal clock's pill and the one button at the right: **Design it**, or **Place on map**, which opens the address search under the row only when pressed (it was always shown in a grey box). On a phone the sign and the clock pill are hidden. Up next and Carry on are unchanged. Seen in the browser on the local example business at 1200px (19 rows, the address search opening); not seen at phone width, and an address not picked through to a design.
- **Customers and contacts, and the group email (1 Nov 2026, Martin: Primero's copied across; wave 5).** `domain/design/customers.ts` (tested), `server/design-people-repo.ts`, `/api/design/customers/group-email`, `/u/[token]` with `/api/u/[token]`, `components/design/people.tsx`; `design-people-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`. The Customers page splits into **Customers** (someone with a job installed, `isCustomer`) and **Contacts** (everyone else) (`PEOPLE_TABS`, `?tab=contacts`), each row marked with what their jobs put in (`HasMarks`: solar, battery, EV charger, from `jobHas`: the deal's system type, kW, battery and EV charger details, or its title; never more than they say; lost jobs don't count), filterable by them (each chip steps any, has, hasn't: "with solar and without a battery"). **Email these N** opens the group email (`GroupEmail`): a subject and words with `{firstName}`, sent to the people on show who have an address and haven't unsubscribed, at most `GROUP_EMAIL_MAX` (150), each their own copy from RENUABL's sender in the business's name, replies to the member, written on each one's record as an email; who is left out and why is said first. Sending needs the `publish` permission and email and `SITE_URL` set up (it says so when not). Each copy carries the person's own link to **stop group emails** (`/u/<40 hex>`, `design_customers.unsub_token` made on first send; the page names the business and stops them only on a press, never on opening, so a mail app checking links can't unsubscribe anyone; `unsubscribed_at`; `/u/` disallowed in robots). Unsubscribing only stops group emails: an email to one customer from their deal still goes. The list marks those who did ("No group emails").
- **Sales, deals and installations (11 Oct 2026).** The left nav is Overview, **CRM** (Customers, **Deals**: the pipelines; the Deals page's button is **New deal**, `NewDeal` in design-customers.tsx: an existing customer found by search or someone new, then what and where, opening the deal; "What are they interested in?" (9 Nov 2026, Martin: "What it is" didn't say what what was) is Solar, Battery and EV charger, any together, naming the deal ("Solar and battery", `interestTitle` in `domain/design/deal-interest.ts`, tested), or Something else typed; the same on a customer's New job), **Sales** (Proposals Sent, Proposals Signed), **Procurement** (Install Scheduler, Ordering Station, at `/design/procurement/...`), **Installations** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: the install calendar, Upcoming and In the works were the same jobs seen two ways, so they are one page, **Installations** at `/design/installations` with **Calendar | List** (`InstallationsPage` in installations.tsx, `?view=list`; `/design/procurement/scheduler` and `/design/installations/underway` redirect there): the calendar is the Install Scheduler, the list every sold job not yet marked installed, booked soonest first then to book, a job whose day has come carrying **Mark installed** (moves it to installed). **The calendar fits the month and takes entries by hand** (10 Nov 2026, Martin: fit more in view; make entries straight on it): the title with a bell for customer reminders (an orange dot while they are off, linking to Settings), Month | Week and Calendar | List on its row, the month and its arrows under it, shorter days, and each week's capacity in the calendar's own first column (`WeekLoad`: booked over can do with a fine bar, green once full, "+N" tentative; pressing a month's week opens it; 10 Nov 2026, Martin: the pills crowded the calendar). Clicking a day opens `EntryEditor` in design-scheduler.tsx: a **Tentative** install (optionally for one of the business's open or sold deals, never changing its install date; dashed in the signal colour, counted as "+N" in its week's column), a **Day off** (who is away, over one day or up to a month) or a **Note**; clicking an entry changes or removes it. `domain/design/calendar-entries.ts` (tested: `cleanEntryInput`, `entriesOn`, `entryLabel`, `tentativeInWeek`), `server/design-calendar-entries-repo.ts` (table `design_calendar_entries`, business id first; a deal named must be the business's own), `/api/design/schedule/entries` (GET `?from&to`, POST) and `/[id]` (PUT, DELETE), every member, `services/calendar-entries.ts`. Not yet: entries on Today's Calendar tab or in Sol's day, dragging an entry to another day, and a day off taking from the week's capacity. **After install** (`/design/installations/completed`, `AfterInstallPage` in install-lists.tsx) takes each installed job through four steps in order, `AFTER_INSTALL_STEPS` in deal.ts, tested: invoice the customer (paid), follow up the CoES (`details.coes` received, or the deal at CoES received or past it), claim the STCs, raise the grid connection (raised counts: the rest is the network's); the next step is the first not done (`nextAfterStep`), the four as tiles with how many jobs are at each, every row the four as dropdowns with the next ringed; `paperworkDone` is all four. **Grid connections** (`/design/installations/grid`, `GridConnectionsPage` in install-lists.tsx; 8 Nov 2026, Martin): every installed deal whose inspection is done (the CoES in, on the deal or by its stage, `inspectionDone`), until it is connected (`gridConnectionJobs`, `gridStateOf`: To raise, Raised, Connected; To connect / Connected / All), each with the electricity retailer, the distributor, the NMI (typed in the row) and the grid connection; how many installed are still waiting on the inspection is said above. **The distributor fills itself** (`distributorFor` in networks.ts, tested): from the NMI's first four digits when the deal has one (certain), else the state's one network (SA, TAS, ACT, NT), else most of a Victorian postcode's network (`VIC_POSTCODES`, a guess marked "From the postcode, check the NMI"; VERIFY near boundaries); shown as filled on the deal's Network tab and the page until something is typed, never stored by itself. Installer Invoices as before), Messages, then (18 Oct 2026, Martin: it reads as a job's life, people and deals, then what was proposed and won, then the install) the **Design tool in orange** (`main`: it is the main tool and keeps its orange wherever you are) (`NAV` in `design-shell.tsx`; a section with `under` is a heading over its subsections). `/design/sales` redirects to `/design/sales/pipelines` (`PipelinesPage` in `design-sales.tsx`): deals are jobs, seen through a pipeline, which is a run of stages (`PIPELINES` in `domain/design/deal.ts`: Sales, Won to installed, Every stage, Lost, or any stages ticked), as cards (columns, drag to move) or a list. A business saves its own pipelines beside the four set ones (`design_pipelines`, `/api/design/pipelines`, `cleanPipelineInput`: a name and stages, shared by every member, up to `MAX_PIPELINES`; rename, give it the stages now ticked, or delete). Only which pipeline and view a person last used is kept on their device (`renuabl.design.pipeline`). Clicking a deal opens `/design/deals/[id]` (`design-deal.tsx`): down the left the deal's details and its **system details** (`systemDetails`: each line is what was typed in by hand, else what the job's design and its quote settings say (`designDetails`), else "--"; any line can be typed over on the page, kept on the job as `details` (`design_jobs.details`, known keys only: `DETAIL_KEYS`, `cleanJobDetails`, sent whole), marked as typed in with the way back to the design's; property type, optimisers, switchboard, distributor, NMI and site access are only ever typed in), beside it Overview and Activities (by kind, searchable, by month). `/design/installations` (`design-installations.tsx`) lists installations in the works: jobs at Won, booked ones soonest first, then those to book (`inTheWorks`, `installOrder`).
- **After the sale (18 Oct 2026, Martin: bring Primero's CRM pieces across for the landing page's selling points; adapted, not copied: Primero runs on HubSpot and Blob documents, this is per-business Postgres; nothing of Primero's name or rates).** Nav: Sales has Pipelines, **Proposals sent** and **Signed proposals** (`design-proposals-list.tsx`, `listProposals`); Installations has In the works, **Install Scheduler**, **Ordering Station** and **Installer Invoices** (`PATHS`; the most particular subsection is marked, `here`). Server: `server/design-after-sale-repo.ts` (tenant rule as everywhere: business id first). **A signature sets it going**: `/api/proposal/[token]` accept → `after()` → `afterSigning`: the order opens (`design_orders`, once) and the installation allowance is fixed (`design_install_costs`); each failing alone, never undoing the signature. Proposal versions now keep each quote line's `sku` and `qty` in `internal` (never the customer's copy) so the order is the exact lines sold. **Ordering Station** (`domain/design/orders.ts` tested, `design-orders.tsx`, `/api/design/orders`): only hand changes kept (qty, taken off, added, supplier chosen, ticked); worked-out quantities always from the signed version; allowances left off; grouped by supplier (the business's ranked price lists, `prefs.ts`); statuses To order / Ordered / Delivered with server stamps; purchase order emailed per supplier (`purchaseOrderEmail`: no prices, the business's name, from RENUABL's sender, reply-to the member, `Email.replyTo`), written on the record (activity kind `order`); CSV with costs, formula-guarded. **Installer Invoices** (`installer-invoices.ts` tested, `design-invoices.tsx`, `/api/design/invoices`): allowance = the signed version's Installation lines (non-zero), fixed at signing (backfilled `later` for older ones, or "work it out again"); until the job is installed (an installed stage, or its date has come) it shows "Not installed yet" (`upcoming`), never "Awaiting invoice", since installers bill after the install; the installer's invoice is read from a PDF or photo by Claude (`server/invoice-reader.ts`, `/api/design/invoices/read`, nothing kept, the business checks the figures) or typed in; only figures kept (`design_installer_invoices`), against a job or waiting (`matchScore` suggests); difference within the business's tolerance (default 0) matches. No Xero (Primero's used it). **Install Scheduler** (`schedule.ts` tested, `design-scheduler.tsx`, `/api/design/schedule`): month and week, Monday first, holidays and weekends shaded, each job green/amber/grey by readiness (stock ordered, deposit received or none asked). **Customer reminders** (Settings → After the sale → Customer reminders; `design_prefs`, off until switched on): `installReminderEmail` N days before (1, 2, 3, 5 or 7), from the daily cron (`sendInstallReminders`), marked sent first (`design_reminders_sent`), named for the business, reply-to its brand email, activity kind `reminder`. Settings also has Suppliers and ordering (rank, names, PO emails) and Installer invoices (tolerance). **Designer**: "String automatically" (`auto-string.ts` tested: by orientation, length range from the voltage limits, near-equal strings, snake order; missing data → no strings and says so) and "Recommended for this home" in the battery menu (`battery-suggest.ts` tested, adapted from Primero's rule: median spare of the 12 months, capped by the bill's evenings, ÷ 0.9, same maker as the inverter preferred; a suggestion, never added by itself); Publish design opens a box for a note to the customer (`onNote` → the job's chat, emailed when replies are; "Publish only" or "Publish and send"). `design-after-sale-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`.
- **The configurator: the proposal as Tesla would sell it (19 Nov 2026, Martin: "compare it with ordering a car with Tesla… $65,000 and yet it doesn't feel like a huge commitment"; built beside the proposal page, never in place of it).** `domain/design/configurator.ts` (tested in `configurator.test.ts`), `configuratorFor` and `optionFrom` in `domain/design/proposal.ts`, `server/design-configurator.ts`, `/api/proposal/[token]/configure` (POST `{action: "order", option, week, name?, agree?}`; "hold" is read the same), `/p/<token>/configure` (`ConfiguratorPage` in configurator-page.tsx; `?mix=` and `?week=` open it where the customer was) and `/p/<token>/order` (`OrderPage` in order-page.tsx); all noindex, no-referrer, as the proposal page; a proposal with no configurator opens its own page. **What can change, and why only this (Martin):** the layout is fixed, so the sizes are designs: the roof's offered or goal designs when there are two or more, else the primary as drawn and worked back from it (`sizeCounts`: as drawn, 80%, 60%, never under `MIN_CONFIG_PANELS` 6; `trimDesign` takes the least sunny panels off first and drops the strings). **Draw the fullest system the roof takes and the configurator works back from it.** What isn't placed on the roof is swapped on any size: the panel's maker (`panelChoices`: the designed one, then up to `MAX_OTHER_PANELS` 2 other makers on the business's lists, nearest in watts; `withPanel` keeps the designed panel's size so the places don't move), **the battery** (none, or up to `MAX_BATTERY_CHOICES` 3, `batteryChoices`: the one designed, then the battery suggestion's pick of each maker that works with the inverter, AC coupled with its controller where it isn't the inverter's maker), **blackout backup** with a battery (`withBackup`: one module more held for blackouts, the backup circuits on the quote) and **an EV charger** (the design's, else the first on the business's lists, `chargersOf`; `withCharger`). **Every mix is priced in full when the proposal is published** by `workOut` as a design of the roof's own would be (a mix with no cost on file is left out, never guessed), kept as `ProposalView.configurator` (sizes with their roof outlines, panels, batteries, charger, variants: each a `ProposalOption` keyed `cfg-<size>-<panel>-<battery|n>-<ev><backup>`, `ConfigKey`, `configKey`, roof sent once on its size) and the business's side as `ProposalInternal.configurator`; `viewForChoice` and `internalForChoice` read a `cfg-` key (`variantOf`), so going ahead, the deposit, the order and the installation allowance follow the mix. A Doug Mitchell example came to 114 mixes and a 237 KB view: watch the size if makers multiply. The first proposals' keys (`cfg-<size>-<panel>-<b|n>`) are still read (`readConfigurator`). **The page:** the home from above on the left, framed on the largest size's panels (`Roof` `frame`, exported from proposal-view.tsx), with **a bar joined under it** (`Performance`): what it makes a year, the **grid offset** (the bill's share of the home's power the system supplies; the panels' share, then the battery's on top in the business's accent, "Solar 40% · battery +50%", never said to be more than 90%, `OFFSET_CAP`) and the yearly saving, all changing with the battery; **the battery and EV charger are drawn on a dark plate over the picture** (`BatteryArt`: a tall white unit with its modules as seams, the blackout module tinted, a light bar in the accent that fills; its kWh and maker; `ChargerArt`), never a maker's product photo and nothing of RENUABL's. On the right: Size, Panels, Battery (by maker), Add-ons (Blackout backup and EV charger switches, each with what it adds), Installation (**four week tiles**, `installWeeksFor`: from the booking assistant's lead days, weeks with an install day left and fewer installs booked or pencilled than the weekly capacity) and Price (with the payback). A bar along the foot with what they pay after the federal rebate and **Order**. **Order opens the order page**, plain on purpose (Martin: "this page should be boring, so that the consumer is incentivised not to dwell on it"): what is being ordered (the mix in words, `mixWords`, and the system lines), the week, the price with GST, the federal rebate, Solar Victoria's note, deposit and milestones, the costs that may apply, the agreement's details (`contractDetails`), the New Energy Tech consumer information, the terms in full (or the business's own PDF) and special conditions, then the order. **A final proposal's order is going ahead** (`orderMix`: name and tick with `acceptLine`, `acceptProposal` with the mix, the business told and the order and allowance opened as from the proposal page, the week pencilled on the install calendar Monday to Friday by "customer" and written on the deal, and "Ordered in the configurator: …" with **"Update the design to match before ordering stock"** when the mix isn't the drawn design, `chosenWords`: the order and price are the mix's, the job card still draws the design it came from). **An indicative proposal's order is a request** (no terms, nothing agreed or charged): the week pencilled in and the customer's own line in the deal's chat (`holdMessage`). **The way in:** the proposal screen's **Open the configurator (new)** and **Copy the configurator link**; nothing on the customer's own page links to it yet. Seen in the browser on the local example business: three designs × three panel makers × no battery or Sigenergy, Sungrow or Enphase (with and without backup) × EV charger; offset and saving moving with the battery; four weeks; an indicative order request landing on the calendar and in the chat; a final order of Quickest payback, Tindo panels, a Sigenergy battery with backup and the EV charger, whose stock order came out 14 Tindo panels, three modules and their controller, and the charger. The Browser pane, while hidden, froze CSS animations at their first frame (the picture looked washed out): the map no longer fades in. Not yet: a monthly figure (needs a finance partner or a loan rule), the job card drawing a worked-back size, a link from the customer's proposal page, the configurator in the PDF, the payment schedule's later amounts in the foot bar. VERIFY on the live business with a real proposal, and have a lawyer confirm the order page carries what a sale must. **Due today and a special (19 Nov 2026, Martin, from Tesla's order page: "lets add due today: and a deposit amount where the % is modifiable… and an announcement bar at the top so we can announce any specials")**: the proposal screen has **The configurator** card (`OrderSettings` in proposal-extras.tsx), changing the business's brand kit as Settings → Payment does (`prices` permission): **Deposit when they order** as a % of what they pay (0 asks for none; a fixed amount is said and replaced once a % is typed), and **Announcement for a special** with **Shows until** (`BrandKit.announcement`, `cleanAnnouncement`: one plain line up to `ANNOUNCEMENT_MAX` 140, a date or none; `announcementShows`, tested in `announcement.test.ts`). Both go with proposals published from then on: the announcement is copied onto the view only while it shows on the day of publishing (`ProposalView.announcement`), and the configurator draws it as a graphite bar across the top until its day has passed ("· Until 30 November 2026"). Check any offer announced is one the business can honour. The foot bar shows **Due today** beside Order: the mix's deposit (`terms.deposit`, each mix its own), or "$0 · nothing to pay to order"; on an indicative proposal $0 with the deposit due once the final proposal is confirmed. **Once ordered, the order page shows the payments at once** (`openProposal` in services/proposals.ts; `PaySchedule` and `PayDeposit` as the proposal page has them: card through the business's Stripe, bank transfer, or "will be in touch"). Seen in the browser on the local example business: a 10% deposit ($2,496.60 on $24,966), the bar, and a final order showing the deposit to pay. **Backup only with a battery, and a locked configurator says why (19 Nov 2026, Martin: "im unable to toggle the backup option"; then "perhaps it shouldnt appear unless a battery is selected")**: Blackout backup shows under Add-ons only once a battery is picked, and Add-ons hides when there is no battery and no EV charger (the steps after it renumber); switching it on goes to that mix with backup, else the nearest priced one with backup (`backupPick` in configurator.ts, tested). The choices lock once the proposal is ordered, lapsed or withdrawn, and a card above them now says so ("You ordered this system on 19 November."); before, the switches only looked broken. **Inverters to choose from, and the choices on pads (19 Nov 2026, Martin, from Tesla's configurator: "further over to the right hand side, and its on pads. their titles are also more distinguishable… an icon for panels, inverter, battery and EV… different inverters to choose from that work with the design")**: an **Inverter** step between Panels and Battery (`inverterChoices` in configurator.ts, tested): the one designed with, then up to `MAX_OTHER_INVERTERS` (2) of other makers the designer knows (`isKnownMaker` in makers.ts), the same supply and kind (hybrid or not), big enough for that size's panels, stringing onto it when both input limits are on file (`autoStringDesign`), never a maker needing parts behind every panel (`NEEDS_PANEL_PARTS`: SolarEdge, Enphase, Tigo) unless designed with; a design with two inverters offers only its own. **Each inverter has its own batteries** (`ConfigBattery.inverter`, `batteriesFor`), priced at publish for every panel, battery, backup and charger mix (`withInverter`); a mix's key gains `-i<n>` only for an inverter other than the designed one (`configKey`, older keys still read); `switchInverter` and `switchSize` keep the battery's and inverter's makers where they can; the order page and the deal's note name the inverter when there was a choice. **Each mix's system lines are sent once** (`packLines`: `ConfiguratorView.say`, `sayIds`, read back by `readConfigurator` and `variantOf`; never named `lines`, one of the `COST_WORDS`): Doug Mitchell's 174 mixes are 206 KB. **The page**: white, wider (1680px), the choices hugging the right edge on grey pads (`Step`: an icon in a round, the title 22px semibold, the options white on the pad; the step numbers gone): Size (a home), Panels, Inverter, Battery (the deal banner's `PanelIcon`, `InverterIcon`, `BatteryIcon`), Add-ons (a plus; the EV charger with `ChargerIcon`), Installation, Price. Seen in the browser on the local example business at 1600px and phone width; not on the live business. **One fold, and a battery worth looking at (19 Nov 2026, Martin: "the map needs to fit along with the performance bar and battery all in one fold… space around it… for the RENUABL logo to stand out"; the battery "so uninspiring… not exciting", with Tesla's Powerwall pictures as the bar)**: on a wide screen the heading, the picture, its figures and the battery share one width worked from the window's height (`.cfg-home` in the page's CSS: the picture is 1.6 wide to 1 tall, so `(100dvh − 28rem) × 1.6`, 2.5rem more off with the special's bar), centred with white space round them; the header has more room. `BatteryArt` is a white unit seen from three-quarters (light across its face, a graphite side with a status light in the accent), on a dark ribbed wall with its shadow, taller for more modules, a cool blue strip for blackout backup; beside it the kWh large and a label with the maker and a charge bar filling in the accent. Our own drawing, never a maker's photo or mark. The plate scales with the picture (a container query: 0.66, 0.82 from 48rem, whole from 64rem). Still with reduced motion. Seen in the browser on the local example business at 1920×1000 and 1280×760. Not yet: a line from the panels on the roof to the battery, as Tesla's Solar line. **Storage, decided costs, rings and a living battery (19 Nov 2026, Martin: "how come i cant select the inverter?… add ons should contain battery bollard, battery fire sheeting, switchboard upgrades, RCBO's… these aren't components a customer would self select… make it a unique to renuabl gif type image where the bars light up to the top… imagine if Apple were making home batteries… apple watch inspired analytics… an option to increase or decrease the storage by adding or removing a module")**: (1) a proposal published before inverters could be chosen shows its designed inverter (read from its system lines) as the selected card; choices come with republishing, and only from inverters on the business's lists that pass `inverterChoices`. From then on an accepted version is read as agreed everywhere (`asAgreed` in design-proposal-repo.ts; `signedVersion` in design-after-sale-repo.ts with `internalForChoice`): its price, deposit and milestones, the lists, the scheduler, the order and the installation allowance are the chosen system's.
