It is only an outline (the plan has no heights): a flat roof is laid on as one face; a pitched one is put down for its slopes to be traced (`outlineNote`), its slope never read from Google's heights (of the old roof). The right click on the picture offers **Roof outline from Nearmap** on a Nearmap picture. Not run when the roof opens by itself. Tried live at 2 Hanwell Court (a 352 m², 20-corner outline from the 25 Apr 2026 capture); not yet seen in the browser. **Nearmap's heights (9 Nov 2026, Martin: wire the designer to Nearmap's DSM)**: while a roof's picture is a Nearmap capture, the slope of each traced face and the shade analysis read Nearmap's heights, not Google's (often years older: Hanwell Court's is 2018), and say so ("Nearmap's height data (27 Jan 2026)"). `nearmapHeights` in server/nearmap.ts takes the newest capture with the detailed DSM (Tile API `tiles/v3/surveys/<id>/DetailDsm/20/…png`, about 12 tiles), else the standard `Dsm` at zoom 18 (`dsmSource`), and `terrainFromTiles` in `domain/design/nearmap-dsm.ts` (tested) sets them on the designer's grid. **How the tiles carry heights, read from the tiles themselves (VERIFY with Nearmap)**: 257 × 257 (one row and column shared with the next tile), a cell's red, green and blue as one 24-bit number in 1/256ths of a metre, 0 for no reading, and each tile on a base of its own in whole metres, put back in step by the shared edge (`alignTiles`). Kept by place for every business (`nearmap_heights`, RENUABL's own key's data; none found is asked again after 30 days), served by `/api/design/roof/nearmap/heights` (409 while Google's picture is in use); `loadTerrain(lat, lng, { nearmap })` asks it first and falls back to Google's. The key reaches DetailDsm, Dsm, Dem, DetailDtm and TrueOrtho through the Tile API (the 403s were the Static Map coverage endpoint, not the data); only the AI `roof_char` pack is refused. The detailed layers are only in the 27 Jan 2026 capture round Glen Waverley and Rowville. **The roof's faces from Nearmap's heights (9 Nov 2026, Martin: not Google's roof model, which at 29 Shearer Drive was 2018's)**: on a Nearmap picture with nothing traced, Design it for me and "Fit N kW" first put down the home's faces read from the heights inside its Nearmap outline (`nearmapRoofFaces` in roof-designer.tsx; `facesFromHeights` in `domain/design/roof-faces.ts`, tested on a made-up hip roof, tuned on Shearer Drive and Hanwell Court): each quarter-metre gets its slope from a plane through the heights round it (`ROOF_FACES.window`), alike neighbours grow into faces, every part of the roof then goes to the plane it sits on (so faces meet at their hips and ridges), the edges are tidied by a vote of the cells round them, outlines traced and simplified; pitch and direction from each face's plane (`faceFromAspect`), as traced faces to check and change (`facesFoundNote`). Nearmap's outline can take in the house next door (it calls touching houses one building): only faces joined to the address's face by a smooth seam (`seamStep`), or with their middle within `nearHome` (10 m) of the address, are kept; a steep narrow band (the wall where one roof steps down) is dropped. Else Google's roof model as before. Tried on two roofs only: VERIFY on more; on faces under about 30 m² the packer still lays fewer panels than it might. **What is already on the roof (9 Nov 2026, Martin: at 29 Shearer Drive, Rowville, Design it for me laid panels over the home's two existing arrays; Google's data was from 2018)**: before Design it for me and "Fit N kW" lay anything, the picture in use (Google's, or the Nearmap capture) is looked at once for existing panels, vents, skylights, air conditioners and the like (the partner portal's obstruction check, `checkObstructions`, at 960 px wide on `ROOF_CHECK_MODEL` claude-sonnet-5-5, about 10 s; `server/design-roof-check.ts`, kept in `design_roof_checks` by business, roof and picture; POST `/api/design/roof/check`, no Sol credit; `services/roof-check.ts`); each box is set on the roof in metres and grown by `OBSTRUCTION_SETBACK_M` (`roofThings`, `keepOuts` in `domain/design/roof-check.ts`, tested), and the packer keeps every panel off them (`Area.avoid`, `avoiding` in pack.ts; `suggestPanels` and `packFaces` take `avoid`; `SelfDesignInput.avoid`). They are drawn dashed on the picture with their names (`thingLabel`), and the card says what was kept clear of (`keptClearWords`) and, when there are panels already, `HAS_SOLAR_NOTE` (likely a battery or an addition: ask on the call). **Existing panels being replaced** (9 Nov 2026, Martin): the button under Sol's answer and Design it for me's card ("The existing panels are being replaced: use their roof"), or the words ("ignore the existing panels", `replaceExisting`/`keepExisting` in design-commands.ts), leave the existing panels out of what is kept clear (`withoutExisting`, `REPLACING_NOTE`), lay the last thing again, and draw them faded as "Being replaced"; for the visit. **The clear parts of a face are packed apart** (Martin: only four panels went in a corner and the open roof past the existing panels was never used): `regionsOf` in pack.ts splits a face that places to keep clear cut up into its joined clear parts, each its own array (the pyramid climbs from the gutter, so a row cut short stopped every row after it); `packAreas` takes its best sun from faces with room for an array. A large "something else" (over `MAX_OTHER_M2`, 6 m²: a pergola, a glass roof) isn't kept clear of. While Sol works on a request in the designer, what was asked shows with him thinking (`SolThinking`). A helper from the picture, never a site inspection. Nearmap's AI on our plan has no solar pack, so this is Claude's look, not Nearmap's. **The house from its four sides (9 Nov 2026, Martin: the 3D model was "useless", not sharp, nothing to do with it; "why wouldn't you use Nearmap's angled photos?")**: a **Sides** button beside Google | Nearmap (picture-menu.tsx, signed in, Nearmap set up) opens Nearmap's angled photos of the house, Looking north / east / south / west, with ← → to go round (`SideViewsButton` in side-views.tsx, on the page body). `nearmapOblique` in server/nearmap.ts: the newest capture with that Panorama view (`tiles:North` …), its zoom 21 tiles over an 800 px square round the point, each turned north-up by the view's heading and laid as the vertical ones are (the same tile numbers cover the same ground, Nearmap's sample code), the square turned back so the camera's way is up and foreshortened to 3/4 height (Nearmap's own 192/256 for 45° photos); `/api/design/roof/nearmap/oblique?view=` (signed-in members, the key on the server, the last 60 kept in memory). Nine-odd tiles a side. Nothing is drawn on them. **Nearmap's 3D model (9 Nov 2026, kept off since)**: the code stays (mesh-3d.tsx, `showNearmapMesh`, the mesh routes) but no button opens it: (picture-menu.tsx, signed in, Nearmap set up) opens the roof in Nearmap's textured 3D mesh in a window over the page (`Mesh3dButton` in mesh-3d.tsx, on the page body; drag to turn, scroll to zoom; Escape, the cross or a press outside closes it), with CesiumJS (`showNearmapMesh` in services/house-3d.ts, the same viewer as the job card's Google 3D). Coverage lists each capture's mesh with its `rootPath` (`/meshes/v1/stores/…/Mesh/VIC.Melbourne.json`, Cesium 3D Tiles, draco geometry, JPEG textures, unlit); our key reads it (not in Nearmap's public docs: found in Coverage, 9 Nov 2026; captures 2023, 2024 and 26 Jan 2026 round Rowville). `nearmapMesh` picks the newest; the browser never holds Nearmap's key: `/api/design/roof/mesh` gives the root as an address on our server and `/api/design/roof/mesh/part/[...path]` reads each part from Nearmap with the key (signed-in members only, paths under `/meshes/v1/stores/` only, `meshPartAllowed`). For looking only: the design is drawn on the picture. A model is some hundreds of parts of up to about 1 MB each, read once a view (VERIFY what it counts against the Nearmap plan). The proposal, job card and design home still show Google's picture.
- **View as a retailer, installer or inspector, for RENUABL staff (3 Nov 2026, Martin, under Settings).** `domain/design/staff-view.ts` (tested: `SAMPLE_BUSINESSES`, one of each kind on a `.example` domain nothing is delivered to), `server/design-staff-view.ts` (`staffBehind`, `readySamples`; `design-staff-view-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`), `/api/design/staff-view`, `components/design/staff-view-as.tsx`. **Staff only** (`ADMIN_EMAILS`): Settings → RENUABL staff → **View as** (`staff: true` in settings-list.ts, shown by `SettingsFrame` only when `/api/design/me` says `staff`; the page is open to every kind in `pageAllowed` and shows nothing to anyone else; the route answers 404 to anyone else). Pressing a kind makes the three sample businesses on first use (listed in the directory, the retailer connected to the installer and the installer to the inspector) and signs the staff member into that one (`openSession` for its email), keeping their own session id aside in the httpOnly `STAFF_RETURN_COOKIE`; `staffBehind` reads it so they stay staff while inside a sample. An orange bar over every page (`staffViewing` on `/me`) names the kind and the business, with **Switch** and **Back to my account** (`kind: "own"` ends the sample session and puts their own back). Changes made inside a sample are real changes to sample data. Not built yet: a job sent from the sample retailer to the installer and on to the inspector, so Jobs and Inspections show one.
- **Datasheets found online by themselves (3 Nov 2026, Martin: "an auto function that does this each time a product is added"; Primero has no such thing: there a person always gives the sheet).** `server/datasheet-finder.ts` (`findSheetLinks`: Claude with web search and fetch, `DATASHEET_MODEL`, default claude-sonnet-5-5, asked for the maker's own datasheet PDF for that exact model; only links the search really showed or opened are tried, PDFs naming more of the product code first, `sheetCandidates`, tested, because Claude can write a plausible address that doesn't exist; `safeDownload`: https only, every redirect and the host's addresses checked against private and local ones, `fetchableUrl`/`isPrivateAddress` in `domain/net.ts`, tested, up to `MAX_SHEET_BYTES`, a real PDF; read by the datasheet reader for that model only, `forModel`; checked as a person's save is, `foundFigures`), kept by `saveFoundSheet` with the sheet (under `KEEP_SHEET_BYTES`), `found_url` and `unchecked` on `design_datasheets`, never over figures already kept. **When**: after a price list is saved, its panels and inverters with no figures anywhere (`missingSheets`, by `codeKey`, RENUABL's own included; `FIND_AT_ONCE` 8, after the answer, `maxDuration` 300), and on demand from the strings box when String automatically stops for want of figures (**Find its datasheet online**, `onFindSheet`, POST `/api/design/datasheets` `{do: "find"}`, 30 an hour). **Used at once, marked to check** (Martin's choice): the figures carry `unchecked` (the sheet's address), every string on them is a caution ("Its figures were found online and aren't checked yet"), so Ready to install stays amber, until someone confirms them in Settings → Product datasheets (**I've checked them: confirm**, `{do: "confirm"}`, `confirmSheet`, who may change prices), where the row shows "Found online · to check" and the link. **The reader's answer form has no "a number or nothing" fields** (the API refused it with 18; every reading, uploads included, was failing): a figure not read clearly is 0 and `readingFrom` makes it null. **Sol swaps a sheet himself** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: he asked Sol to replace an EU sheet with the Australian one and Sol told him to do it): tool `update_datasheet` (design-sol.ts; `prices` permission, at most two an answer, never in a turn that read a customer's own words) tries a PDF link Sol saw in that answer, else searches as the finder does, or reads a sheet attached to the message (`attached_file`, `replaceDatasheetFromFile`), and keeps it **over** what is on file (`replaceWithFoundSheet`, unchecked again, to confirm in Settings); a failure says what was tried and why (`Tried`: a link that wouldn't open, a sheet without this exact model). Searching prefers Australia's sheet (`sheetRegionScore`: .au sites or AU/OC marks up, EU/US marks down) and the reader is asked for the product code exactly (a -DV sheet isn't a -DB panel's). Some makers' sites turn the downloader away (jinkosolar.com.au answered 404): then the paperclip. RENUABL's own panel (`JKM475N-48QL6-DB`, All Black) had no figures (the built-in ones are the -DV): found online in 24 s on a test.
- **Datasheets are shared across every business (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "can we ensure that any datasheet downloaded by any user then becomes available to all users").** A datasheet is a maker's public document, not a business's own data, so this is the one read across businesses in the designer's tables, kept in `server/design-datasheets-repo.ts`. `listSheets` answers the business's own sheets, then **those shared with it** (`sharedSheets`, `sharedFor` in datasheets.ts, tested in `datasheets-shared.test.ts`): other businesses' sheets for the products it has none of its own for, one a product by `codeKey`, a checked one before one only found online, then the newest; `businessDatasheets`, the designer, the string check, Design it for me, the price-list finder (`missingSheets`, so RENUABL stops searching for a sheet another business has) and Sol's `datasheet_lookup` all read through it. **Its own always wins.** `sheetFile` opens a shared sheet's file. **Never whose it is**: a shared sheet's `checkedBy` is `SHARED_BY` ("another business on RENUABL") and its figures' `basis` is rewritten (`sharedBasis`: the product, the file and the day; the original carried the checker's name). **A business only ever writes its own rows**; a shared sheet can't be removed by it (Settings hides Remove; saving its own for the product takes its place). **Made-up businesses share nothing** (`REAL_BUSINESS`: owners on `.example`, so demos and the staff samples). **Trust (Claude's choice, told to Martin)**: a shared sheet a person at another business checked counts as checked; one only found online stays to check for everyone; **I've checked them: confirm** on a shared one makes it the business's own, checked by that member (`adoptSharedSheet`, through the route's `confirm`). Settings → Product datasheets lists shared ones after the business's own with a Shared pill. `design-datasheets-db.test.ts` covers it (`npm run test:db`). Not seen in the browser with a shared row (the local database has no second real business with sheets): VERIFY on the live site. Not yet: a staff view of the shared library on /admin, a way to report a wrong shared sheet, and makers' sheets RENUABL keeps itself.
- **Every shared datasheet is to check until each business confirms it (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "make every shared sheet 'to check' until each business confirms it"; over the trust choice in the note above).** A sheet shared from another business is `unchecked` for the business reading it whoever checked it where it came from (`sharedSheets`; `checkedElsewhere` says a person there did), so every string on it is a caution and Ready to install stays amber until someone who may change prices presses **I've checked them: confirm** in Settings → Product datasheets, which makes it the business's own (`adoptSharedSheet`). The business that added it sees its own as before. The string check's line reads "Its figures haven't been checked by your business yet" (electrical.ts; it said found online) and the strings box "Not checked by your business yet"; a shared sheet with no address of its own sends "Where it came from" to the Settings page. Settings shows "Shared · checked by another business on RENUABL on <date> · to check". Database test updated. Not seen in the browser with a shared row.
- **The roof pitch card, and a pitch for each roof (3 Nov 2026, Martin).** `PitchFirst` is smaller (`w-52`): the figure is a box to tap and type over (`PitchStepper`, Enter sets it; the quick picks of 10° to 35° are gone as noise), and **It's flat** is a pill beside **Set 22°**. The left-edge mark is `RoofPitches`: one mark a roof (`QuoteForm.pitches` after the main `pitch`, up to `MAX_ROOFS` 4, cleaned by `cleanExtras`, tested in `roof-pitches.test.ts`), shown for a flat roof too ("Flat", with **It isn't flat: set a pitch**); the ringed one is the roof new panels and traced faces go down at (`activeRoof`, `homePitch`); pressing another makes it that one and lays any picked panels at its pitch; pressing the ringed one opens it to change (roofs after the first can be removed); **+** adds a roof (a garage, a patio). Add a panel stays on after an array is dragged out (the array stays picked with its tilt offered). Design it for me says nothing when it runs by itself as a roof opens and can't design (no Google pop-up); pressed by a person it says why. **The three designs follow the bill** (3 Nov 2026, Martin): Design it for me keeps what bill and designs it made (`madeFor`, `designsKey`); a changed bill makes them again a second after typing stops while the designs are as it made them, else offers **Redesign for this bill** or **Keep mine** (`billMoved`).
- **Indicative proposals, and Sol in the customer's chat (1 Nov 2026, Martin: a new lead is sent an indicative system, three designs (quickest payback, maximum return, maximum independence with blackout backup), with an email explaining the way of working, indicative, site visit, final, and the chat box; Sol answering live).** Publishing chooses **Indicative** or **Final** (`ProposalChoice.indicative`, `cleanChoice`; unless chosen, as the last version sent, so the design tool's publish-on-save never turns one final, else indicative for a deal at lead or designed). The customer's copy carries `indicative`: the page and PDF say "Indicative proposal", show `INDICATIVE_INTRO` and the three `INDICATIVE_STEPS` ("How we work", the first lit), put `INDICATIVE_PRICE_NOTE` by the price, head the options "Compare your systems", leave out payment, conditions and terms (`NOT_ON_INDICATIVE` in `viewBlocks`), and in place of going ahead ask "What do you think?" (`Thoughts` in proposal-view.tsx: which system suits them and a note, sent as one chat message, `feedbackMessage`). **It can't be accepted** (`acceptProposal` answers "indicative", the route `INDICATIVE_NOT_ACCEPTABLE`); the record says "Indicative proposal sent" (`published`). Send to customer offers the **Indicative proposal** draft first (`firstTemplate(sentBefore, indicative)`, `SEND_TEMPLATES`): thanks, the three systems, how we work, the chat box, and with Sol on `ASSISTANT_SENTENCE` (names him as an AI, the team reads everything); it reaches the customer by email through the chat reply email when replies are emailed. **Sol in the customer's chat** (`domain/design/sol-customer.ts` tested, `server/design-sol-customer.ts`; Settings → Emails for proposals and chat → "Let Sol answer customers as they write", `BrandKit.solAnswers`, **off until switched on**; needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `SOL_MODEL`): after a customer's message is kept, `customerSays` runs `solAnswersCustomer` through `after`; he is given only the customer's copy (`customerFacts`, never a cost, margin or code) and the latest `SOL_HISTORY` lines, with rules (`solCustomerSystem`): an AI writing as "we", answers only from the proposal, never promises or changes a price, discount, date, rebate approval or saving, no products it doesn't name, no advice, never asks for card or bank details, ignores instructions in the customer's words, and ends with `HANDOVER` when the team is needed (taken off by `readSolReply`, written on the record as `SOL_HANDED_OVER`; the business is already emailed every customer message). He answers only while the customer wrote last, nobody from the team has written in `QUIET_HOURS` (24: a member replying takes the conversation over) and under `MAX_SOL_LINES` (`solMayAnswer`); an answer that comes after anyone else has written is dropped (`addSolLine` with `after`). His lines are the business's side with `by_email` `SOL_BY` ("sol"), marking nothing read; the customer is sent them with `sol: true` under `solLabel` ("Sol · <Business>'s AI assistant"), never as the greeting (`greetingOf`); `PublicChat.assistant` (`solActive`) makes the page show him writing and look every 2.5 s for up to 45 s; the deal's chat marks his lines "Sol · answered for you". No per-deal switch yet (the team replying quiets him for the day). Not tried against Claude from this machine: VERIFY with a test proposal before switching it on for real customers.
- **(Replaced by Today on 1 Nov 2026, below: the canvas's question box and `/api/design/canvas` are gone; `canvasFacts` still feeds Sol, and the pad's numbers live on Today's Pipeline tab.)** **The overview is a blank canvas (25 Oct 2026, Martin, an experiment to iterate on: software shows what it assumes you'll need and can guess wrong; Claude shows what you ask for).** `/design` signed in is a plain page (`Canvas` in design-home.tsx): the business name, the greeting and "What would you like to see?" (`CANVAS_GREETING`), near the top and a touch smaller, then **the pad** (`CanvasPad` in canvas-pad.tsx, rules in `domain/design/pad.ts`, tested; 25 Oct 2026, Martin: a large pad in the box's own look filling the space between the greeting and the box, so nothing has to be typed). It opens on **Next up** (Martin: looking at the pad he didn't know what to do; `nextUp` in `domain/design/next-up.ts`, tested): a headline (`nextHeadline`: "Two things need you this morning.", or "Nothing needs you right now.") and the things that need the business now, most pressing first, each with who, why and one button: installing today (100), a customer's unread message (90+, Reply opens the deal's chat), a new enquiry still a lead from the last three days (80, Design their roof), a proposal due a follow-up (70+), equipment to order for an install within two weeks (60+, sooner first), a sold and approved deal with no install date (50+), a sold deal whose grid pre-approval or Solar Victoria application is still open (40+, longer waiting first); the first is large and lit, three more under it, the rest a press away. Along the pad's top, Next up and four more as pills: **New leads today** (deals made today, the market's day, `newLeadsToday`), **Sales today and this month** (today and month to date in $, deals and kW, the sun crossing the month and the pace, `salesToday`), **Installations today** (`installsToday`, with the installer named) and **Sales closed this month** (`closedThisMonth`, with the total); each with its count, pressing one shows it in the pad (deals open their deal page), pressing it again goes back to Next up. Then a box at the foot of the page (sticky). Each question goes to `/api/design/canvas` (`requireMember("design")`, the member's own deals only, `viewerOf`): Claude answers in one to three plain sentences from `canvasFacts` (`domain/design/canvas.ts`, tested: the journey's counts, sold this month and financial year, each live deal by the customer's first name with its stop, stage, value, kW, sold and install dates; never emails, phones or addresses) and names the panels to draw on a last `SHOW:` line (`readCanvasAnswer`, at most two of `CANVAS_PANELS`: journey, today, sales, upcoming, completed), drawn under the answer, newest last; the conversation lasts for the visit (last 10 turns sent as history). Without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, when Claude fails, or past 120 an hour (`allow`), the question's words pick the panel (`guessCanvas`). The journey, Today and the three views below are now panels for the canvas; `?view=` still opens a view on its own.
- **The overview is the journey (25 Oct 2026, Martin: RENUABL is the end to end solar business tool, and the screens weren't showing that clearly and simply; the nav competed with the page).** The nav starts folded to icons (`FOLDED` in design-shell.tsx: folded unless the person opened it out on that device). The overview (`OverviewPage` in design-home.tsx) is, in order: the business name over the greeting and the date, with New customer and Open the design tool; **the journey** (`JourneyLine`; `domain/design/journey.ts`, tested): every job at one of nine stops in three parts, **Win the work** (Enquiries, Design, Sale), **Deliver it** (Approvals: grid pre-approval or a Solar Victoria application still open, `approvalsOpen`; Procure: until ordered with an installer named; Install; Post-install: until invoice paid, STCs claimed and grid connected, `paperworkDone`) and **Look after it** (Running, "Monitoring coming soon"; Service, from the open cases), read from each job's stage and details (`journeyStopOf`, nothing stored), a count over a dot on one line (lit where there are jobs), each a link to where that work is done (`JOURNEY_PATHS`); across the page on a wide screen, down it on a phone; then **Today** (`YourDay`); then "Look closer" links to Sales, Upcoming installs and Completed installs (`?view=`), which open with a way back to the overview.
- **Overview: your day (24 Oct 2026, Martin: the default overview a greeting, with the sun tracker showing what needs doing today).** Before Sales, Upcoming or Completed is picked, the overview greets the member and shows `YourDay` (design-home.tsx; `domain/design/today.ts`, tested): on the graphite card the sun on its arc from today's real sunrise to sunset in the market (`sunTimes`, the sunrise equation at `LAUNCH_MARKET.capitalPlace`; `sunNow`), the clock, and one hairline for how much of today's list is clear set against the sun's line ("Ahead of the sun", "N to clear before the sun sets", "The sun has set"); beside it the list (`dayList`): installs today, follow-ups due (`dueFollowUp`), messages to answer, mentions to read, orders still to place with the install within two weeks (`orderDueSoon`), sold deals with no install date, service cases open; each waiting thing a small glowing count, each clear one a quiet tick, each a link to where it is done. Today's installs are the day's work, not counted in what is clear (`dayCleared`). The three views' one-line cards stay under it.
- **Overview: Upcoming and Completed installs (18 Oct 2026, Martin: too much to look at, and the text too big).** `install-lists.tsx` (the overview's Upcoming and Completed views; Sales stays in design-home.tsx): a black banner of three (Upcoming: **Awaiting install**, **Need an installation date**, **Install revenue MTD** = what is still payable on the contracts of jobs installing this month, the price after the federal rebate less any deposit received, `installRevenueThisMonth`; Completed: completed installs this financial year, paperwork open, invoices not paid), this week as a calendar, then the list: job address (customer under it), solar/battery/EV marks lit when present with the sizes, and for Upcoming the install date, **Installer booked** (an Installer typed in the row; booked once there is an installer and a date, `installerBooked`) and **Materials ordered** (from the Ordering Station), for Completed **Customer invoice** (Paid/Pending/Not issued), **STCs** (Claimed/Not claimed) and **Grid connection** (Completed/Underway/Pending), each a dropdown in the row. These live on the job's details (`PROGRESS`, `INSTALLER_KEY` in crm.ts, cleaned by `cleanJobDetails`; `evCharger` is a system detail on the deal), set by hand and separate from the pipeline stages. Battery size, what is payable and the deposit come from the signed proposal (`/api/design/installs`, `installExtras`). Overview type is a notch smaller throughout (page title 22px, banner figures 26px, tiles 20px).
- **Install Scheduler event card (18 Oct 2026, Martin: like a Google Calendar event, with a way to put it in Google Calendar; never for the customer, since it lists the parts and where to collect them).** Clicking a job in the scheduler opens `EventCard` (`event-card.tsx`, from `/api/design/schedule/[jobId]`): the title as bookings are titled (`Solar + Battery: <business> | <kW> <n> storey <customer>`), the day and the install hours (`INSTALL_DAY`, 8am–4pm), the address (a map link), and the job sheet in three parts (Martin: the system to be installed doubles as the pick-up list): **Collect from** (each wholesaler with its **SO number** and what is there, typed on each supplier's card in the Ordering Station, `OrderState.salesOrders`; a supplier with no name set reads one from its price list's file name, `nameFromList`; items with no supplier flagged), **System to be installed** (Solar, Inverter, Battery, EV charger, Roof and racking, Electrical, Other: each with its facts from the deal and the exact items to collect for it, each tagged with its wholesaler; a fact an item already says is left out), and **On site** (the customer's name, phone and address, the site notes, the installer). The customer's email is never in it, and no one is invited by it (no attendees in the .ics). **Add to Google Calendar** opens a pre-filled event (`installEvent` in `domain/design/install-event.ts`, tested) and **.ics file** downloads one; the Google link carries the whole job sheet too, headings in bold (Martin: the short version wasn't enough), with the customer's name and phone for the installer but never their email. RENUABL can't see a business's Google Calendar, so the button is always offered. **Pickup slip** (Martin: not a link to the job, but evidence for the counter of what was ordered, with the wholesaler's reference): each wholesaler's line in Collect from reads our PO and their SO (`referenceLine`, `poText`), and the event (card button, Google description, .ics) links the job's **pickup slip** at `/pickup/<48 hex>` (`design_pickup_links`, one a job for good, made when the event is first opened, `server/design-pickup-repo.ts`; no sign-in, noindex, no-referrer, no ad tags, `/pickup/` disallowed in robots): one printable page a wholesaler with the business that ordered, our PO, their SO, the collection day, the suburb only (`suburbOf`), the installer, the day ordered, every item with code and quantity, tick boxes and lines to sign; items with no wholesaler listed apart. Built by `pickupSlip` (`domain/design/pickup-slip.ts`, tested): it goes to a wholesaler, so nothing of the customer (no name, phone, email or street) and no prices. The link to the deal is no longer in the event.
- **The designer is always a drawer (5 Nov 2026, Martin: never a new screen; the intention is not to stay in it for long, especially once it designs itself).** `components/design/design-drawer.tsx`: `DesignShell` holds one `ShellDesignDrawer`, so any link to `/design/roof` on any page (the orange round, the design home's cards, Deals, a customer, an order, a proposal) slides the designer over that page instead of going there; code uses `openDesigner` (the design home picking an address or a site opens the roof that way, the home staying underneath). Inside the drawer's frame every page is bare (`useInDrawer`, so the designer can move from roof to roof within it), and a link anywhere else in the product folds the drawer and goes there on the page underneath (`DrawerFrameLinks`, `goUnderneath`; the customer's name opens their deal so). Ctrl or ⌘-click and Own tab still open it as a page; `/design/roof` loaded directly is still a page. **Since 8 Nov 2026 it is a centred pop-up, not a drawer** (Martin: in the same space, the page's grey either side, so it reads as quick): `DesignDrawer` is a window about as wide as it is tall (`sm:h-[min(92dvh,62rem)]`, width up to 1.3 times that), rounded, over a dimmed page, opening with a short zoom (none with reduced motion), the whole screen on a phone; closed with work not saved, **Back to the design** waits at the foot of the page. The same pop-up on a deal (see The deal view). **The proposal is the pop-up's second tool** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: the proposal is a tool like the designer, on the deal, the feed and in the menu): a deal's proposal screen (`/design/deals/<id>/proposal`, `ProposalStudio`) opens in the same window (`isPopupTool`, `toolOf`, `designHref`/`proposalHref` in design-drawer.tsx; links inside the frame to either tool stay in it), and for a deal the window has its two tools as rounds in the grey to its left on wide screens (Design orange, Proposal white with an orange page; the one open ringed; `onSwitch`). The Proposal round sits beside the designer's on the deal page (`?proposal=1` opens it), as a Proposal button beside Design on each feed card, and in the top menu beside the designer's round (opening Proposals sent; retailers only).
- **The pop-up widens, and the map with it (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "the ability to expand horizontally the size of the designer"; then "i dont need a widening button, i just needed the ability to hover over an edge and drag it wider" and "widening it only widens the grey padding area surrounding the map. i wanted it all to expand together").** `DesignDrawer` (design-drawer.tsx): either side edge of the window drags its width (it stays centred; from `POP_MIN` 720 px to the screen less room for the tool rounds, `popMax`), a double click on an edge puts back the usual 1.62 times its height; no button. The width is kept on the device (`renuabl.design.popup-width`). **In the pop-up the picture takes the window's whole width** (`fills` in roof-designer.tsx; the figures strip too), its height still the first fold's, so a wider window shows a wider band of the photo, larger: the view kept is always the photo's own shape (`keptView`, what zoom, pan and Whole picture set) and what is drawn is that view's width cut to the picture's shape (`shownView`, `across` measured from the picture's box), so everything placed by a share of the view stays in line; panning and the wheel's zoom read the band. Outside the pop-up the picture is as before. Seen in headless Chrome at 1920px on the local example business (the picture 1256 to 1584 px wide at the same 750 px height; no roof photo there, no Google key). **Sol's cards over the roof are wider and a point smaller** (Martin: "sols messages get in the way"): the guide's step card (`SolGuide`) and his suggestions (`SolNudges`) are up to 46rem wide, never past the picture, at 13px with tighter lines, so a message runs one line where it ran two. **The shade widget no longer covers Payback**: in the pop-up's slim figures strip the Bill figure is left out while the folded shade widget sits beside the figures, and the figures are clipped so they never run under it.
- **Sol always asks "Is this the home?" first (21 Nov 2026, Martin: a new site at 12 Hanwell Court opened on "First, the home." with no check that it was the right house).** The question had been asked only once Victoria's land register answered (`lot` in `guideFacts`); a register that was slow, down or had nothing skipped it. Now `guideFacts.lot` is set for any site with an address (roof-designer.tsx), so the `house` step always comes first. With the register's lot it is outlined as before; without it the card asks about the house in the middle of the picture, and once the register has failed it says it couldn't check it against the land register (`lotMissedAt`). No takes them to search the address or find the house themselves. The register lookup is tried twice (`features` in server/property-lot.ts), and its route may run 45 s. Seen in headless Chrome on the local example business with the register blocked (the card's words). Why the register failed for Hanwell Court on the live site is not known: this container can't reach it.
- **The pop-up's tool rounds follow the window beside Sol's panel (21 Nov 2026, Martin: with Sol open in the right-hand drawer "the 3 floating icons dont move over and therefore sit overlaying the other items").** The Design, Quote and Proposal rounds left of the pop-up (`nav.popup-tools` in design-drawer.tsx) are placed by CSS from the window's width (`--pop-w`, set on them as on the window), in globals.css: beside the centred window as before, and with Sol's panel open (`html.sol-docked`, from 1280px where the rounds show) beside the window in the room left of the panel, the window keeping a 5rem gutter either side for them (`max-width: calc(100vw - var(--sol-w) - 10rem)`). Measured in headless Chrome at 1600px with the panel's state set by hand (the rounds 16 px clear of the window); not seen with Sol's panel itself open.
- **The inverter list opens at its top, its search always to hand (21 Nov 2026, Martin: opened from Sol's prompt it showed scrolled down to the search; "it should open from the top. search should remain sticky at the bottom when scrolling down but otherwise would be at the top").** The inverters' menu in the designer (roof-designer.tsx) focuses its search without scrolling to it (`focusQuietly`, `preventScroll`; it was `autoFocus`), so it opens on what is on the design and Sol's suggestion. The list no longer scrolls in a box of its own: the menu scrolls as one (`kitMenu`). The search sits in its place under Add one; scrolled past it (`kitScrolled`, `searchDown`), it moves to a bar pinned at the menu's foot, keeping its focus and words, and comes back when scrolled up or opened again (`openMenu`). Seen in headless Chrome on the local example business (opened at the top with the search focused, pinned at the foot when scrolled down, typing still filters). **The battery and panel lists too** (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "do the same for the battery and panel lists"): both menus scroll as one, open at their top with the search focused, and pin it at the foot once scrolled past (`kitSearchHere`, `kitSearchFoot`, shared with the inverters); the panel list gained a search of its own (by name or code, `seeks`). Seen in headless Chrome on the local example business opening at the top and filtering; its lists were too short there to scroll. **And the EV charger list** (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "do the same for the EV charger list"): the same, its own inner scroll box gone; its search uses `seeks` too. Typecheck and lint only: the local example business has no chargers on its price lists, so it wasn't seen.
- **The deal page on a phone (21 Nov 2026, Martin, from his iPhone: the menu should be smaller and "anchored on the left hand side hard up to the gutter", the back to Deals an arrow first in it, the customer card smaller and higher with the kit "vertically stacked on the right hand side", Sol's text left aligned and his sun smaller).** Under 640px (`max-sm:` throughout): the deal is a two-column grid, a slim rail and the page. **The rail** is `DealMenu mini` (design-deal.tsx): sticky at the top, pulled into the pad's padding, 36 px rounds with 9.5px words: the back arrow to Deals, Design, Proposal, Call/Email/Files on their plate (`CallMenu mini`, its choices opening to the rail's right, kept on screen), Details, the Job card and Service when they apply, Sol, and Delete (asked first) at its foot; the Deals link and Delete over the banner are hidden, and the row of rounds under the banner is from 640px to the wide screen's rail only. **The banner** (deal-banner.tsx): 20px name, smaller address and chips, the kit (`Kit`) stacked down its right with 28 px icons, the deal clock and heat on their own line under both, the roof 6.5rem. **Sol's card** (sol-on-deal.tsx): his sun is 24 px and floated in his first line so his words run from the card's left edge; the Context label sits inline with its words wrapping under it (context-line.tsx). Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size on the local example business (a won deal and one with a proposal out); not seen on a real phone, with the designer or a Call menu opened from the rail, or with a roof picture. **Tighter again (21 Nov 2026, Martin: the cards to reach the pad's right edge, the top card still too big, Sol's sun taking room, the draft email's text smaller, from a mail app's card)**: on a phone the deal's column runs into the pad's right padding (`max-sm:-mr-3`); the banner is shorter (18px name, 11.5px address, 22 px chips, 24 px kit rounds, the clock and heat pills smaller, the roof strip 4.5rem); Sol's sun is a 16 px mark leading his first line, his words 13px, his buttons 28 px tall, the card's padding 12 px; a draft's head is a small Mail label (`draftHead`), its fields From, To, Cc and Subject at 10.5px labels and 11.5px values in tighter rows, the words 12px. Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size; **the draft email not seen** (no Claude key locally, so Sol drafted nothing). **No rail: pills, the banner's own Call and Email, and an app on the home screen (21 Nov 2026, Martin: the rail took a fifth of the width and cut words short; Sol's card repeated the banner and asked to send a draft it hadn't shown; the roof strip was too thin; Safari's keyboard and the home screen)**, over the rail above: on a phone the deal runs the pad's width (`max-sm:-mx-3`) with no rail; the shell's phone header reads **← Deals** in place of the wordmark on a deal page; **Call and Email** are rounds in the banner's top right (`DealBanner` `actions`, `CallMenu round="banner"`, its choices opening below); the kit is one row of badges with the clock and heat after them (`KitBadges`); the roof strip opens tall on a tap (`roofOpen`, 17rem, with Smaller and Open the design), the design only from there; under the banner **a row of pills that scrolls sideways** (`DealPills`: Design, Proposal, Details, Files, Job card once sold, Service once installed, then Delete); no Sol pill (he is the foot bar's). **Sol's card** shows his draft above the buttons that send it (`hasDraft`, the copy beside them only from `@3xl`), leaves the Context line out on a phone, and its first row reads Send, Write my own, Not now on one line (Open deal an icon only). **Safari**: typing boxes were already 16px on touch screens and the foot bar already padded for the home indicator; the foot bar (`.phone-tabbar`) now steps aside while a box has focus (globals.css, `:has`); `src/app/manifest.ts` (standalone, opening on `/design`, icons `public/pwa/icon-192.png` and `-512.png`: Sol's sun on the graphite) and `appleWebApp` in the root layout let it be added to the home screen and open without Safari's bars. Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size on a deal with no place on the map; **the tall roof, the banner's Call menu, the draft above the buttons and Add to Home Screen not seen** (no roof photo or Claude key locally, no real iPhone): VERIFY on the phone. Not yet: the Sol box docked above the keyboard (Safari scrolls the box into view itself). **Icons, a Sol sheet and the kit down the right (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "Turn the design etc pills back into their icons"; Sol's card "a pop up which can be collapsed or reintroduced via the centre sol icon at the footer"; the kit "vertical as before a little larger with the text to its left")**, over the above where they differ: `DealPills` is a row of 40 px icon rounds with a 10px name under each (`PillRound`; Design orange, the one open graphite); the banner's right column is Call and Email, then the kit down the right edge, each value left of its 32 px icon (`Kit`, `max-sm:flex-row-reverse`; `KitBadges` is gone), the clock and heat under the chips; Sol's card is `DealSolSheet` (design-deal.tsx): on a phone a sheet over the foot bar (up to 58dvh, scrolling inside), its handle folds it away, and while one is on screen `<html data-deal-sol>` makes the foot bar's Sol round toggle it (`toggleSolHere`, `DEAL_SOL_TOGGLE` in services/sol.ts) instead of opening his panel; folded or not is kept for the tab, and the page gets room at its foot for it (`--deal-sol-h`). One element either way, so the card is never mounted twice. Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size (the rounds, the kit, folding and the Sol round bringing it back). **Details as compact rows, and Sol's sheet as a chat (22 Nov 2026, Martin: the deal's fields were "too big" and ran off to the right; "sols chat tab doesn't feel like a chat tab")**: on a phone the Details fields are one column of rows (`.deal-fields` in globals.css: the label on the left, the value beside it with no box until it is typed in, a hairline between; the grid is `grid-cols-1` so a wide date box can't push past the screen), and the tabs over them (The deal, System…) scroll sideways instead of wrapping. Sol's sheet (`DealSolSheet`, `.deal-sol-sheet` in globals.css, hooks `sol-line`, `sol-replies`, `sol-compose`, `sol-send` in sol-on-deal.tsx) has a header (his sun, "Sol", "On <name>'s deal", a fold chevron), his line as a bubble from him, the choices as quick replies under it and the box as a rounded composer with an orange send round, kept at the sheet's foot. Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size; not on a real phone. **The fields' text smaller** (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "Text in the input fields is too large"): on a phone a field's value in `.deal-fields` is 13px beside its 12px label (`!important` over the 16px touch-screen floor; an iPhone doesn't zoom, `maximum-scale=1`). **A thick message box (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "I want it to be thicker like the attached example")**: in the sheet the box is a rounded rectangle about 6rem tall (`.sol-compose` as a grid in globals.css), the words along its top and the microphone and the orange send round in its lower right; "Sol" in the sheet's header carries a small AI pill. Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size. **The rounds half over the banner, and a smaller email form (22 Nov 2026, Martin: more room on the page, the names still on the grey; the email fields and text smaller for the phone)**: `DealPills` (`.deal-pills`) is pulled up 2rem so each 40 px round sits half over the banner's foot with a soft shadow, its name under it on the pad. The deal's email (`DealEmail`, `.deal-email` in globals.css, phone only) has 32 px fields at 13px, 11px labels and notes, a shorter box for the words and tighter gaps. **On an iPhone or iPad the viewport gets `maximum-scale=1`** (an effect in `Shell`, design-shell.tsx), so Safari no longer zooms into a field under 16px; iOS still allows pinch zoom. Not set on Android (Chrome would stop pinch zoom), so a field under 16px there doesn't zoom anyway. Seen in headless Chrome at iPhone 14 Pro Max size; not on a real iPhone: VERIFY tapping a field doesn't zoom.
- **Panels priced per panel are read from a price list (22 Nov 2026, Martin, from his phone: the designer's panel list showed only the Jinko 475 W).** The reader only took a panel priced per watt in AWM's style ("24.0c/w ($114.00)"), so a list priced per panel gave none and the designer fell back to RENUABL's one panel. `parseItemPanels` (pricing.ts, tested in `item-panels.test.ts`) also takes a priced row that reads as a solar panel (panel, module, mono, N-type, dual glass, black…) with 250 to 800 W in its description, at $40 to $1,500, never racking, inverters, batteries, optimisers, cables or the like; `panelsOfList` puts them after the per-watt ones, up to `MAX_PANELS` (80, was 40). **Lists already uploaded get them too** (`cleanWorkspace` reads them from the kept rows, and `withoutCosts` before it takes the rows away), so no list needs uploading again. AWM's list still reads its same 8 panels. With only one panel to offer, the panel menu says no price list has panels and links to Settings → Price lists. Not seen on the live business's own list: VERIFY its panels now show.
- **The designer in the phone's foot bar (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "we need the designer icon in the bottom menu bar. This will allow for rapid lookup of existing deal or new address to design on the spot").** A retailer's foot bar (`PHONE_TABS` in design-shell.tsx) is Today, Deals, Sol (raised), **Designer**, Messages and More, six across (an inspector's six too; an installer's five). Designer is an orange round like the top menu's, and opens the design tool's home over the page (the shell's pop-up), whose one search takes a deal or an address. The Design tool stays at the top of More as well. Seen in headless Chrome as an iPhone 13 on the local example business (the bar, and Designer opening the home). The search isn't focused by itself: Safari on an iPhone won't raise the keyboard for a box focused by code, so it is one tap.
- **Voice on the phone's foot bar, in place of Today (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "replace the today icon out with voice agent, tapping it opens up a dock to select an agent if you have more than 1. Deals will shift into today's position, designer will shift into deals position and voice will shift into designers position").** A retailer's foot bar (`PHONE_TABS` in design-shell.tsx) is Deals, Designer, Sol (raised; he goes to Today, see Sol's chat notes), **Voice** and More. Voice is `PhoneVoiceTab` (phone-voice.tsx), a button: with one named voice agent it opens that agent's chat (`openCoach`); with more than one a dock rises over the foot bar listing each (its face, name and job, the sales agents then the post-sale one), each opening its chat, with **Your agents · add one** for someone who may change settings; with none it opens Your agents. A count on it while calls are under way (`useLiveCallsNow`). Today is reached through Sol's round. Seen in headless Chrome as an iPhone 13 on the local sample retailer with two agents put in by hand (the bar, the dock, Tim's chat opening). Not yet: the dock showing which agent is on a call. **The agent's chat is a general chat that can ring someone, names come up as typed, and Voice is in colour (22 Nov 2026, Martin, on a screenshot where Charlie answered "this chat is where you coach me… I can't ring Martin from here": "Voice opened up to the training page. It should open to general chat. Also typing customer and then typing the first letter of a name should bring up customers names and filter out. It should be easily ignored if it's not the intended function. Also update the voice icon in the doc to have a color")**: `coachPrompt` (coaching.ts) now has the agent talked to in general (asked things, asked to ring a customer, or coached); asked to call someone it fills `ring` (`COACH_SCHEMA`, `cleanRing`: the name as said and what for), says the call is ready and never that it can't ring from the chat; kept on the assistant's turn (`CoachTurn.ring`). Under the latest answer `RingCard` (coach-ring.tsx) finds the customer among those the member may see with a deal and a number (`reachContacts`; one tapped from the chips wins), asks which when several match, lets the reason be changed, and **Ring <first name>** hands it over as Call → Hand it to <name> does (`askAssistantToCall` with `about`, then the live window); Not now puts it away. Nothing is placed before the press. The box reads "Ask <name> anything, or to ring someone…", and its hello says so. **Names as typed** (`nameAt`, `nameHints`, `withName` in `domain/design/name-hint.ts`, tested): a capitalised word (not a common word) or any word after call, ring, text, customer and the like brings up to five customers as chips over the box (first names first, two words for a full name); a tap puts the whole name in, keeping the keyboard up; typing on, Escape or the cross ignores them. **The Voice round** on the foot bar is graphite with the wave in the first agent's colour (`tintOf`), as Designer is its orange round. Seen in headless Chrome as an iPhone 13 (the round, the placeholder, chips for "Call P", a tap and typing on); **the ring card not tried** (no Claude key locally): VERIFY one call asked for in the chat on the live business.
- **A window over the page on a phone keeps RENUABL in view (22 Nov 2026, Martin, on the voice agent's chat covering half the wordmark: "it should adjust itself (the logo) so that it's smaller but in view").** While the voice agent's chat (`CoachWindow`) is open full on a phone it sets `html[data-phone-window]`, and the phone header's wordmark (`.phone-wordmark` in design-shell.tsx) steps down to 13px; the window (`.coach-window` in globals.css) is never taller than the screen less the foot bar and 3.5rem, so its top stays under the header. Folded small, nothing changes. Seen in headless Chrome as an iPhone 13 (the window's top at 56px, the wordmark's foot at 39px). **And it stands off the page** (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "the bot card should have a contrast"): while it is open on a phone the page behind dims a little (`html[data-phone-window] body::before`, under the foot bar, taking no presses) and the window has a graphite edge and a deeper shadow (`.coach-window`). Other windows that open over a phone page (the chief of staff's, Your agents) don't do this yet.
- **Sol on a deal folds into the foot bar's Sol round (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "When sol is minimised he should minimise visually onto the centre sol icon so the user can see that's where they can re-engage Sol on the deal in view"; over the small Sol tab above the foot bar).** On a phone, folding the deal's Sol sheet (`DealSolSheet` in design-deal.tsx, its handle) shrinks it into the foot bar's raised Sol round (`.deal-sol-tucked` in globals.css: scaled down towards the round's middle and faded, about 0.4 s; none with reduced motion); the round then carries an orange ring and a small dot and gives a little bump as he lands (`data-holding` on `SolNavButton phone`, from `data-deal-sol-tucked` on <html> and `DEAL_SOL_STATE` in services/sol.ts), says "Sol on this deal", and a press brings the sheet back out (`DEAL_SOL_TOGGLE`). Away from a deal, or with the sheet open, the round goes to Today as before. The separate Sol tab is gone. Seen in headless Chrome as an iPhone 13 on the local sample retailer (the fold mid-way, the round holding him, a press bringing him back); not on a real iPhone.
- **Team chat in the phone's foot bar (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "We need to introduce the team chat into the bottom nav").** A retailer's foot bar (`PHONE_TABS` in design-shell.tsx) is Deals, Designer, Sol (raised), Voice, **Team** and More, six across (over the Voice note above, which had five); Team opens `/design/team` with this member's unread team lines on it (`useTeamUnread`). On the page, a panel narrower than `NARROW_PANEL` (560px, team-chat.tsx) shows the conversations or one room, not both: picking one opens it in the list's place and its back arrow (`BackToList`) brings the list back; on a phone the chat is shorter so its box clears the foot bar. Seen in headless Chrome as an iPhone 13 on the local example business (the tab, a channel opened and back); not on a real iPhone.
- **The design home's search fits on a phone (22 Nov 2026, Martin: "Find a deal…" was cut off; make the text smaller so it can all be seen).** Under 640px (`narrow` in design-tool-home.tsx, a `matchMedia` read through `useSyncExternalStore`) the box's hint is **Find a deal or type an address** (or Type an address) at 13.5px, the box and the orange + a little tighter (`max-sm:` padding and gap, the + 48 px), so it fits from an iPhone SE up (193px of words in a 203 to 258px box); what is typed stays 16px so Safari doesn't zoom. A smaller font alone couldn't fit the whole sentence (241px at 12.5px in a 194px box), so the words are shorter there. Wider screens are unchanged. Measured in headless Chrome at 375, 390, 430 and 1200px wide on the local example business.
- **The designer on a phone (18 Nov 2026, Martin, from the demo on his phone: every control lay on a roof picture about 220 px wide; "we need to seriously fix it").** In the pop-up under 640 px wide (`PHONE_BELOW`, `stage` and `phone` in roof-designer.tsx, measured in `stripBox`) the picture runs the screen's width and the controls stand off it, in two bands made by padding the picture's own container (`PHONE_BANDS`): **above** the kit rounds, then the Select pad and the designs/Publish control; **below** the picture's switch and Ask Sol; and under them **the rail as a toolbar across the foot** (the knife, stringing, shade, Draw, Day, Rec; `BarRow row`), its knife opening as a sheet from the foot of the screen (`KnifeMenu sheet`). Anything placed by a spot on the picture uses `picY` (a share of the picture's height, under the band above it). Sol's step card starts just under the picture so the roof it asks about stays in view. The figures strip wraps and keeps clear of the window's close round, as does the address notice. Desktop is untouched (every change is behind `phone`). Seen in the browser at 375 px on the local example business (at rest, Sol's guide, the knife's sheet, Add a panel's card); not seen on a real phone, and not tried by touch: VERIFY placing and dragging panels with a finger, the kit's lists, strings and the Sol panel on an iPhone. I think it should allow for tilting the phone sideways and the designer fills the screen").** A phone turned sideways (wider than tall, no taller than `SIDEWAYS_BELOW` 500px, `isSideways` in roof-designer.tsx) got the desktop pop-up squeezed: a rounded window with gutters and a picture about 225px tall below the fold. On it: Sol's guide and suggestions are short cards at the picture's foot (`sideways-sol` in globals.css: the question, choices and buttons in rows, scrolling inside past the picture's height); the pitch card waits until Sol's step is answered; the dial for what is picked is drawn at 0.7 from the picture's edge (`Dial` `small`, upright phones too), and the picture's own buttons (Day, Roof, Google | Nearmap) step aside while it is up; the Design pad shows its number without the word. **Upright**, a hint at the top of the picture says "Turn your phone sideways for a bigger view" until its cross puts it away on that device (`TURN_HINT_KEY`). - **The designer's controls drawn smaller sideways (21 Nov 2026, Martin, from his phone sideways: "Need a mobile optimised version. While `sideways` (roof-designer.tsx), the picture carries `designer-compact` and the rail `designer-compact-rail` (globals.css): every control group on the picture is drawn at `zoom` 0.68 (its touch area shrinks with it), Sol's card at 0.76, the rail at 0.72 and the figures strip at 0.82; the map (the picture's first child) and anything placed at a point on it (an inline `style`: the dial, the + handles, the turn bar) keep their size. Lists and cards opened from a shrunk control are drawn back up (zoom 1.4: the kit lists by their `--pic-h` height, the designs menu by `data-opened`, and any dialog, menu or listbox; not inside Sol's card). Seen in headless Chrome at 932 × 310 (an iPhone's Safari sideways with its bars) on the local example business: at rest, the designs menu, the inverter list and nine panels picked. **Not on a real phone**: VERIFY the rounds are still easy to press with a thumb, and Safari's support for `zoom` (Safari has had it for years; check a control placed by percentage). Upright phones are unchanged.
- **The designer on a phone held upright (21 Nov 2026, Martin, from his iPhone: zoom in a level, the picture higher and longer, every button smaller, Select, Add a panel and Erase on the picture's top left corner, Roof and Sides as icons only, the Day button and feature gone, the kit half on the picture's top edge, a magnifier for pinch zoom in a toolbar hugging the picture's foot with Roof and Sides, the dial in proportion with the picture).** In roof-designer.tsx under 640px in the pop-up (`phone`): the band above the picture is only `PHONE_BANDS.above` (30px), so the kit sits on the picture's top edge, half on it; the pad is on the picture's top left corner; in its top right corner, level with the kit and its height, start again (42 px before shrinking, as the kit's outer rounds) and the designs as an icon-only round a little larger (`size-[3.25rem]`, the layers mark with the open design's number under it, as the panel round carries its count), each opening under them (`resetRound`, `resetQuestion`; the menu as before); the picture is as tall as the screen allows (`photoH` between the photo's own shape and `PHONE_TALLEST` 1.4 times its width) and shows a band down the photo (`shownView` now cuts a taller picture's width, `keptFor` turns it back; the wheel zoom reads both), opening one zoom press closer (`PHONE_ZOOM` 1.4). Every control on the picture is drawn at 0.68 (`designer-compact`, as sideways), the roof pitch marks by the picture's middle (`RoofPitches` `top`), the rail across the foot at `PHONE_RAIL_ZOOM` 0.72 (its knife's sheet drawn back up, tool-fan.tsx), the dial for what is picked at about three fifths of the picture's height (`Dial` `fit`). **The toolbar under the picture** (`role="toolbar"` "The picture", hugging its foot): the magnifier (`zooming`: a layer over the picture takes the fingers, two pinch about their middle and one moves, no tool acts; it reads "Pinch · Done" while on), Roof (the roof from above) and Sides as icons (`SideViewsButton` `icon`), and the picture's switch (Google | Nearmap); the switch group over Ask Sol is hidden on a phone upright. The designer's own row (the knife, save, ready to install, strings, shade, record and the rest, the `header`) stands in a strip at the tray's foot, "This design", fixed there only while the tray is up. **With something picked** the dial and the turn bar give way to Sol's card over his round (`SolPicksCard`): what is picked with its tilt and facing, −5°, −1°, 90°, +1°, +5° (`turn`), Portrait and Landscape (`turnLandscape`), Tilt − and +, Delete and pick none; turning in words ("turn them 3 degrees left") was already Sol's (`parseCommand` rotate). The controller's two 1° buttons are `HoldButton` (sol-tray.tsx): a press turns 1°, held past `HOLD_START_MS` (380 ms) it keeps turning, about 11° a second (`HOLD_EVERY_MS` 90): each repeat waits until the last turn has been drawn and then turns by the degrees due since the last one, at most `HOLD_MOST_AT_ONCE` (5), so a slow redraw neither turns from a stale copy nor slows the turning. A whole hold is one step to undo (`onHold` holds the history as the tilt slider does, `sliding`). A keyboard press turns once. Seen in headless Chrome: a 2 s hold turned 22°, and one Undo put it back. **The Home Screen hint sideways (22 Nov 2026, Martin: Safari's tab and address bars took the room sideways; a page can't hide them, and iPhone Safari gives pages no full screen)**: turned sideways in a phone's browser (`sideways && solHolds`), a small graphite card in the picture's top left says "For the full screen, open RENUABL from your Home Screen:" with that browser's steps (`phoneBrowserOf`, `HOME_SCREEN_STEPS` in `domain/design/home-screen.ts`, tested: Share, then Add to Home Screen on an iPhone or iPad; the ⋮ menu on Android), until its cross puts it away on that device (`renuabl.design.home-hint`); never when opened from the Home Screen (`navigator.standalone` or `display-mode: standalone` on the top window). A set one (Sales, Post sale, Post installation, Lost) renamed or given other stages is kept as a row standing in for it (`design_pipelines.base_id`, one per set pipeline), and **Put back as it came** removes that row. **+ New pipeline…** at the foot of the menu starts one as a copy of the stages on show. **Add a stage**, the dashed column at a board's end, takes a name, where it goes and what it counts as (the stage before it until changed). A name in use by another stage, built-in or added, is refused. The deal renders its tabs from it (`resolveDealFields`, `fieldLine`), with "Arrange these fields" linking to the page. Changing settings needs the `prices` permission (settings), reading needs `design`. Values already typed on deals are kept when the arrangement changes.

States Open → In hand → Resolved (`SERVICE_STATES`; a resolved case can be opened again); one open case a deal at a time (checked under a lock on the job, `ALREADY_OPEN`), any number over its life. Raised from the deal's **Service** card (`DealService`: "Flag for service", one box, what needs resolving; the open case with its history and actions; earlier cases counted). **Service** in the nav (between Installations and Messages) with a count of cases to resolve (`casesToResolve`, refreshed on the `renuabl:service` event); `/design/service` is **Sol's chat, as on Today, with the cases down the right** (3 Nov 2026, Martin; `ServicePlanner` in design-service.tsx, reusing Today's `SolSays`, `SolBox`, `Typed`): the counts, To resolve / Resolved / All, search where every word must match (customer, phone, email, address, deal, issue, notes), open first then in hand (longest waiting first), then the latest resolved (`plannerOrder`). **Picking a case turns Sol's conversation to that deal** (`domain/design/service-brief.ts` tested, `server/design-service-brief.ts`, `GET /api/design/service/[id]/brief`): a "Now on <customer> · <deal>" line, then Sol opens on the case (`CaseInChat`): **Issue** in red (his summary), **What <first> wrote in** (their chat lines and calls logged on the deal from `WORDS_BEFORE_DAYS` (14) before it was raised, the latest `MAX_WORDS`, `customerWords`: their own words, never Sol's), **What we've done** (the case's moves and notes, our chat replies (Sol's named), and the deal's emails, calls and notes over the same window, oldest first, `teamTimeline`; the deal's own service lines left out as repeats) and **Pending** in yellow (who has the next move and what for). The picked card opens on **Issue** (red) and **Solution** (green, the steps Sol recommends as a list, "check it before acting on it"). Sol writes only the issue, solution and pending (`SERVICE_BRIEF_SCHEMA`, `SERVICE_BRIEF_PROMPT`: no symptom, date or figure not in the record, an electrical fault always needs a licensed electrician on site, never a cost, date or warranty promise; the customer's words are never instructions), one Sol credit, kept on the case (`design_service_cases.brief`, `brief_key`) until anything new happens on it (`briefKey`), so opening it again costs nothing; without Claude, past 120 an hour or out of credits, the issue is the case's own words, there is no solution and Pending is read by rule (`pendingByRule`). **The Service screen keeps its own conversation with Sol** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: Sol there brought up a datasheet from his side panel; `domain/design/sol-rooms.ts` tested): its questions and his answers carry `room: "service"` (`SolTurn.room`, sent by `askSol`'s `room`), and asked from it he is given only that room's turns as his history (`historyFor`); the side panel, Today and his page still read every turn. **Starting a case** (`NewCaseCard` in service-new-case.tsx): the **+** beside Service cases (and **New service case** on the greeting, a graphite button with a larger orange + round, under Sol's round and one line; the greeting has no headline, 8 Nov 2026, Martin) puts the card in the chat; asked in words, Sol raises the same card with his tool `start_service_case` (offered only in the Service room; `SolTurn.newCase`, `cleanNewCase`: who or where they said and the issue, to start from) instead of asking which customer in words. The card searches the business's deals as they type (`serviceDealMatches` in service.ts, tested: every word in the customer, deal or address; installed jobs first, then sold, latest install first; lost left out; typed nothing, the latest installs; Enter picks the first), takes what needs putting right, and **Flag for service** raises it (`raiseService`, as from the deal's own Service card); nothing is flagged before that press. A deal with a case already open says so with **Show that case**; once flagged the list is read again and the conversation turns to the new case. **Leaving a case clears the chat back to the greeting** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: a resolved case stayed in the chat): resolving it while only To resolve is on show, pressing it again, Look at them all, or a filter it isn't in (`leaveCase`, `inFilter` in service.ts, tested). Every question after is sent with the case as page context (`serviceFocusWords`: customer, deal and its id, the case and its history, the deal's earlier cases; with none picked, the cases waiting in a line each); his `deal_details` now also gives the as-built with serials (`asBuiltInWords`) and every service case on the deal (`caseInWords`, `casesForJob`). The picked card opens to the customer's details, Resolved, Open deal, the history and the note box. On a phone the cases come first, then the chat; each case beside its customer and deal (phone, email, address, stage, install date, installer, Open deal), raised by and waiting N days, the issue, the latest note, **Resolved** in one press, and Update for the history, a note and the next steps ("… with the note"). Every change is written on the deal's record as activity kind `service` ("Flagged for service", "Service case in hand", …; `recordLine`); nothing is sent to the customer. No priority, assignee, due date or customer-raised cases yet.
- **Deal owners, who sees what, and @mentions (22 Oct 2026, Martin: as Primero's).** Every deal has an **owner** (`design_jobs.owner_email`, `Job.owner`), whoever made it; older and sample deals have none.
