Deals has **Everyone's deals / My deals / No owner yet** and the owner's initials on each card (`Owner` in design-sales.tsx). A new permission, **Sees every deal** (`allDeals` in `MemberPermissions`, on unless the owner switches it off; the owner always sees all): without it a member sees only their own deals, no one's, and any they are @mentioned on (`seesDeal`, `viewerOf` in design-context.ts; the server's `seen` condition in design-crm-repo.ts says the same) on Deals, the deal page and its chat, email and proposal, a customer's page, the Overview and the Proposals lists; another's deal answers as if it weren't there. The Install Scheduler, Ordering Station, pickup slips, invoices and Service stay the whole team's. **@mentions** (`domain/design/mentions.ts` tested, `server/design-notifications-repo.ts`, table `design_notifications`): a note, call or email logged on a deal with `@sam` (a member's whole email handle, or its first part when only they have it; never the writer) gives that member a notification and, with email set up, an email from RENUABL with the note and a link (`mentionEmail`, reply-to the writer); the deal's note box has a **Mention…** picker, and the writer sees "Told sam". **Notifications** in the nav (with the unread count, `useCount`) opens `/design/notifications`: the member's own mentions, newest first, unread marked, each opening its deal, and Mark all read; opening a deal reads its mentions. `design-team-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`.
- **Product datasheets (22 Oct 2026, Martin: as Primero's).** Settings → Pricing → **Product datasheets** (`settings-datasheets.tsx`, `domain/design/datasheets.ts` tested, `/api/design/datasheets` and `/read`, `server/datasheet-reader.ts`, `server/design-datasheets-repo.ts`, table `design_datasheets`; `design-datasheets-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`): upload a panel's or inverter's datasheet (PDF or photo, up to 10 MB; since 1 Nov 2026 the sheet is kept with its saved figures when under `KEEP_SHEET_BYTES` (4 MB, Vercel's request limit), checked by its own bytes, `design_datasheets.file_bytes`, `/api/design/datasheets/file`, "Open the sheet" for any member) and Claude reads its electrical figures (panel: Voc, Vmp, Isc, Imp and the three temperature coefficients as printed; inverter: max input voltage, MPPT range, start voltage, and each MPPT's current, short-circuit current and strings; null where unclear, never guessed), or type them in; a person checks them against the sheet (blanks marked), names the product code as on their price list (suggested from the lists), and saves. The server checks every figure again (`checkPanel`, `checkInverter`: present, in range, in order; one wrong stops the save and says which) and keeps them by product code with who checked them and when (`sheetBasis`, named by the string check). The designer puts them ahead of RENUABL's own (`withDatasheets` on its catalogue in design-studio.tsx), so the string check, String automatically and Design it for me (which prefers inverters with figures) work for any product with a datasheet. The page lists products on the price lists that still can't be strung. Changing them needs the `prices` permission; every member designs with them. Reading needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; without it the page says so and the figures are typed in.
- **Standards and regulations, and Sol on the electrical rules (1 Nov 2026, Martin: "we need to be able to download AS3000, AS3008 and the battery standards"; "Sol should know all electrical regulations to assist in customer support and installer support").** Settings → **Library** → **Standards and regulations** (`settings-standards.tsx`, `domain/design/standards.ts` tested, `server/design-standards-repo.ts`, `/api/design/standards` and `/[id]`, `services/standards.ts`; tables `design_standards`, `design_standard_parts`; `design-standards-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`). **Standards Australia sells the standards under licence, so RENUABL never holds or hands out a copy of its own**: each business uploads the PDFs it has bought, ticking that its licence covers everyone in the business who can open the copy (`LICENCE_LINE`, required, who ticked kept as `licence_by`), and every member downloads them; changing the library needs the `prices` permission. `STANDARDS` lists AS/NZS 3000, 3008.1.1, 5139, 4777.1, 4777.2, 5033 and 1170.2 by group, without a year (the copy carries its edition), each with what it covers in our words (no standard's text is reproduced), plus "Something else"; `OFFICIAL_SOURCES` links Standards Australia's store, Energy Safe Victoria, Victorian legislation, Solar Accreditation Australia and the CER (VERIFY yearly). **A copy goes up in parts** (`STANDARDS_LIMITS.partBytes` 3 MB, under Vercel's 4.5 MB request limit; up to 60 MB, 50 copies): POST starts it, PUT `?part=n` each part, POST `{do: "finish"}` checks every part is in, the sizes add up and the whole is a PDF by its own bytes (`isPdf`), and only then lists it; unfinished uploads go after a day; downloads stream a part at a time. **Sol** knows the rules (`SOL_REGULATIONS` in his system prompt): explains what a rule asks and why, names the standard and the clause only when sure, says which edition and "check it in your copy", never quotes a standard at length, never says a design, site or installation complies (the licensed electrician signs off), and says so when a safety figure isn't certain for their edition; tools `standards_on_file` (`libraryForSol`: copies on file with editions, the usual ones not on file, the official sources; never a standard's text) and `datasheet_lookup` (`datasheetFigures`: the business's checked figures, then RENUABL's marked to check). **Sol in a customer's proposal chat** may explain the electrical rules in general terms (`solCustomerRegulations`), never whether their own home or site meets one or what will be done there: that hands over to the team. Sol is never given an uploaded standard's text (licences can forbid putting it into AI tools).
- **The sun keeps time: RENUABL's progress visuals (24 Oct 2026, Martin).** Not Apple's rings (borrowed) and not solar panels ("too much"): the RENUABL way is that of leaders who think different, as Apple and Tesla do, so it is restraint: one signal colour, fine lines, big figures, nothing decorative. **The sun keeps time instead of a progress bar** (Martin: novel): it travels its arc across the period, from the first day to the last, and a fine dashed line drops from it through what is below (`SunArc`, `dayShare` in `domain/design/gauges.ts`, tested). **A goal is one hairline** (`GoalLine`): a faint track, a line in the signal orange grown to its share, a small glowing point where it has got to (to the end once reached), the sun's dashed mark across it; past the mark is ahead (`paceOf`: ahead / behind / reached, "+43%"). **A count is a row of fine ticks** (`Tally`, like a charge meter's segments: lit done, half-lit partly, dim; `tallyOrder`). Components in `components/design/gauges.tsx`; they draw in on a short timer, not an animation frame (a page behind another tab gets no frames), and don't move with reduced motion. **Where**: the overview's Sales opens on the month card (`MonthCard`: the sun over three hairlines, sales $, deals sold, kW sold, against the business's **Sales targets**, Settings → CRM → Sales targets, `settings-targets.tsx`, `/api/design/targets`, `design_prefs.targets`, else its own 3-month average, `monthRings` in `sales-rings.ts`, else "No goal yet"; with the pace, `monthPace`); beside it the financial year with six months as fine bars (`MonthBars`); tiles: the month's new deals as ticks (`newDealPanels`: lit sold, half with a proposal out), the open pipeline as one fine line split by stage (`PipelineBar`), proposals out as ticks. Upcoming installs tally each install (`installPanel`: lit when booked and ordered, half when one); In the works and Completed tally paperwork (`paperworkPanel`); Service has an "Every case" tally; the targets page previews the lines.
- **Job map, from the installer's side (24 Oct 2026, Martin: Apple Maps style; where the job is, where the base is, where the stock is collected).** Procurement → **Job map** (`/design/procurement/map`, `design-job-map.tsx`, rules in `domain/design/job-map.ts` tested, `/api/design/job-map` and `/image`). The map is Google's static map in a soft style of our own (`MAP_STYLE`: warm land, white roads, highways pale gold, water soft blue, parks soft green, places and transit off, grey labels; fetched by the server, `staticMapUrl`, so the key never reaches the browser; 640 × 480 at 2x, `MAP_SIZE`) with our own pins over it, placed by Web Mercator (`project`, `fitView` fits every point with room, the closest zoom no nearer than 15): the **base** (graphite, a house), each **collection point** (white with an orange ring, a box) and each **job** (orange, labelled with its install day), the whole team's sold jobs with a place (This week / Upcoming / All sold). Picking a job draws its **run** (`runFor`: base, then the wholesalers on its order nearest first, then the job; `runKm` as the crow flies) as a dashed line and a card: the stops with each leg's km, the total, wholesalers with no point marked, **Directions** (Google Maps through every stop, `directionsUrl`), its **Event card** and **Open deal**. The base and collection points are kept with the business's prefs (`places`, `cleanJobPlaces`: inside Australia, both figures), found with the address search in "Base and collection points" (collection points keyed by the wholesaler's name as on orders, `supplierLabel`); changing them needs the `prices` permission. No customer email anywhere on it; nothing reaches the customer.
- **The calculator** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: on the phone and asked a question, Sol is a wait and a long answer; the answer must be instant): a calculator round for any business that designs, on the rail under team chat (8 Nov 2026, Martin; it opens to the rail's left; in the top bar only on the design tool and for an installer, where there is no rail) (`QuickCalc` in quick-calc.tsx, rules in `domain/design/quick-size.ts`, tested): **A sum** (`sumOf`: numbers, + − × ÷, %, brackets, read by hand, never `eval`) and **A system from** the bill (dollars a month, 2 months or a quarter, at an editable c/kWh and supply charge, `dailyFromBill`) or kWh a day: the day's and year's use, **Solar only** (the year's use at Melbourne's average sunshine, never under 5 kW, panels at 475 W, the inverter at 133%) and **With a battery** (the evening's share of the day, 60%, at 90% usable; solar to fill it from a winter day's spare; a hybrid inverter at 150%), capped at `MAX_QUICK_KW` with a note, from the homeowner plan's `ASSUMPTIONS` (`quickSize`). A rule of thumb, said so; worked out in the page, nothing sent. Escape or a press outside closes it.
- **Now widget** (`NowWidget` in `design-shell.tsx`): the time and date (the market's time zone) and the temperature, top right of every main screen, never the design tool. The temperature is Melbourne's for now (`LAUNCH_MARKET.capitalPlace`; a business has no place of its own yet), from Google's Weather API current conditions (`currentTemperature`, `/api/design/weather`), and is simply left out when it can't be had (the Weather API must be enabled on the Google key).
- **The next panel shows itself at an edge** (8 Nov 2026, Martin: adjusting panels, he had to go back to Add a panel for one more): with Select, the pointer just past a panel's side (within half a panel, never at a corner or where one stands) shows the outline of the panel beside it, laid the same way (`edgeGhostAt` in roof-designer.tsx); a press places it and leaves it picked, a drag pulls out a row as Add a panel does. **Adding panels hands over to the arrow** (11 Oct 2026, Martin): with Add a panel on, the pointer over a panel that is there is the arrow (no outline; a press picks and drags it); dragging out an array and letting go switches to Select with the whole array picked (a single panel placed leaves the tool on). Over any panel of an array (panels touching, `ARRAY_GAP`), a small orange round appears just off the array's top right corner and picks every panel in it (`arrayOf`, `arrayHint`). The turn handles are large and sit just off the edge of what is picked.
- **Drawing mode and shade in the roof designer (13 Oct 2026, Martin: a different experience when designing, "wow", a sales tool).** Two ways of looking, on the right of the dock (`roof-designer.tsx`). **Drawing** (`drawn`, kept on the device): the photo put away and the ground drawn plainly, with the roof being designed lighter than everything else and edged in white (the traced faces, or Google's faces from the roof model until some are traced: `facesFromModel` in `domain/design/drawing.ts`). The ground comes from `server/ground-map.ts` (`/api/design/roof/ground`): one Google Solar Data Layers lookup a roof (charged, then kept in `ground_cache`) gives the drawing (`groundAt`: heights tell a tree from a lawn and a roof from a drive, the building outline, and colours read from the designer's own satellite photo) and the heights as a grid (`&part=terrain`, `Terrain` in `domain/design/shade.ts`); until it arrives, or with no data, the ground read from the photo's colours in the browser stands in (`groundOf`). **Shade analysis** (`shade.ts`, pure and tested: `sunPosition`, `sunSamples`, `shadedAt`, `sunAccess`, `shadeReport`): for each panel, the share of the year's direct sun that nothing on the height grid blocks, shown on each panel and as a summary with months. It is an estimate from Google's heights, which can be older than the roof and its trees; it says so, it is not a site shade assessment, and **it does not change the quote's or the proposal's output figures**. (A 3D view of the roof was tried on 13–14 Oct 2026 and removed at Martin's word: "its not good". Don't bring it back without asking.) Google's Solar data for an address can predate a rebuild (2 Hanwell Court shows the old house): the traced faces are the truth for the roof. Needs the Solar API on the Google key.
- **The Drawn view in the designer (10 Nov 2026, Martin: a truer way to show panels on a roof and a battery on a system than laying them on the Nearmap photo, with almost no latency).** `domain/design/illustrated-roof.ts` (tested), `components/design/illustrated-roof.tsx`. **Photo | Drawn** at the bottom right of the roof picture, above Google | Nearmap (and **Draw the roof** in the pop-up's tools); the choice is kept on the device (`renuabl.design.drawn-roof`). Drawn puts the roof in place of the photo as an illustration (`RoofArt`): the faces traced on the design, else Google's faces for the home (`facesFromModel`), each in the roof's own colour read from the photo already on the page (`colourInside`, shadows and existing panels left out; `faceColour` mixes it with the whole roof's, keeps it in a middle range and lights it from the north-west by the way the face looks), with tile courses across the slope, light ridge and hip lines and a darker gutter (`gutterOf`); the surroundings are the same photo made small, grey and dim (`ghosted`, `useRoofGhost`), so neighbours are a rough presence. Panels are drawn as panels (`PanelArt`: dark glass, a silver frame, cells up to `CELLS_UP_TO` panels, a shadow, settling as they land), strings carry moving dashes towards their first panel (`FlowLine`), and the inverter, battery (a stack of its modules, one in blue when held for blackouts), switchboard and EV charger stand on **the wall** (`WallStrip`, `wallLayout`), a small card at the picture's bottom left; the kit's marks stay on the picture. **Nothing is asked of a server**: it is SVG made in the browser from what is loaded (Google's roof model is asked for only when no face is traced); with no faces at all the photo stays and a line says to trace them. Still with reduced motion. This is not the older Drawing mode (`drawn`, no button) and not 3D. Seen on one traced face and on Google's faces in a deal's pop-up; VERIFY a whole hip roof from Nearmap's heights, strings flowing, a backup battery and Safari. Not yet: a sun slider through the day, the same drawing on the proposal, live co-design and the job card.
- **The home's day: time as the design's second dimension (10 Nov 2026, Martin: the roof layout answers where the panels go; what a customer is buying is what happens in their home through a day).** `domain/design/home-day.ts` (tested in `home-day.test.ts` and `proposal-day.test.ts`), `components/design/home-day.tsx`, `proposal-day.tsx`. `homeDay` plays a design through one day in 48 half hours for a summer, autumn or winter day (`DAY_SEASONS`: mid January, April, June): where the sun is (`sunPosition`, the clock set ahead of the sun by `clockAheadOfSun`, Victoria's: VERIFY elsewhere), what each panel makes by how squarely the sun meets it, what the home uses (the bill's daily use laid on `USE_SHAPE`, a typical household's hours, PLACEHOLDER), what goes into the battery and comes out (run twice so the day starts as the day before left it), and what the grid supplies or takes. **The day's output is the designer's own figure for that month**, only shaped through the day, so it agrees with the output shown elsewhere. No dollars, no inverter clipping, no charge-rate limit. **In the designer**: **Day** beside Photo | Drawn at the bottom right of the picture (and The home's day in the pop-up's tools) opens `HomeDayCard`, a player's bar along the foot of the picture (the same day, Martin: as a card it sat over the roof and the buttons beside it; the bar is low and runs nearly the picture's width, clear of the round that moves the picture, and Ask Sol, Roof | Sides, the picture's switch and the Day button step aside while it plays, back with its cross; the sun on the picture is larger; play, the clock, the season, a chart to drag through or step with the arrow keys: solar over the home's use, the battery's level, red where the grid is drawn on; the moment and the day in words, `momentWords`, `dayWords`) and `DayOverlay` on the picture: night falling, each panel dimmed or lit by the sun on it (`glow`), and the sun on its arc (the picture's middle is overhead, its edge the horizon, north up). There the heights round the roof take shade off each panel (`shadedAt`), and the card says how much of the day's sun is lost. It opens playing from before sunrise; still at midday with reduced motion. **On the customer's proposal**: inside the output block, under the months, **A day with this system** (`ProposalDay`, shown when `hasDay`: an output estimate and panels on the roof): their panels from above on a dark tile with the same overlay, and the card worded for them (`yours`: "your home", `DAY_NOTE_YOURS`: a modelled estimate, nothing guaranteed, typical hours, no shade, real days vary), in the business's colour, playing once when scrolled to. It is worked out in the page from the customer's copy alone, so it follows the system chosen (`viewForChoice`). For it the copy now carries each panel's tilt and facing (`roof.aspects`, an option's `roofAspects`) and what of the battery carries the evenings (`system.eveningKwh`); a proposal published before has neither and is read as a north roof at `TILT.roof` with the whole battery. **Never said**: that a home needs nothing from the grid, a saving, or a dollar figure (tested). Seen in the browser on the sample proposal and on one test roof in the designer; VERIFY on a real published proposal, on a light brand colour and on Safari. Not yet: the day in the proposal's PDF and on live co-design, the customer's own half-hourly smart-meter data in place of the typical hours, and the designer's card kept clear of a roof low in the picture.
- **A recorded walkthrough of the design, with Sol's subtitles (14 Nov 2026, Martin: "a Loom record style option" in the designer, narrating as he designs while Sol adds the subtitles in real time, so the customer gets a live presentation, not just a proposal link).** `domain/design/walkthrough.ts` (tested), `server/design-walkthroughs.ts` (table `design_walkthroughs`, business id first; the video in **private Vercel Blob storage**), `/api/design/jobs/[id]/walkthrough` (GET the deal's recordings and where a new one goes; POST `tidy`, `keep`, `show`; DELETE), `/upload` (Vercel Blob's client upload: a short-lived token that writes only under `walkthroughPrefix(business, job)`, a video of at most `MAX_BYTES`, so the file never passes through our functions), `/[wid]/video` (members who may see the deal), `/api/proposal/[token]/walkthrough` (the one shown, and `?vtt=1` its subtitles) and `/video` (Range passed on, so it seeks), `services/walkthrough.ts`, `components/design/walkthrough-recorder.tsx`, `walkthrough-player.tsx`. **A clapperboard round beside Share live** in the designer (a deal's design, `publish`) opens **Record a walkthrough**: `RECORDING_NOTE`, then the browser asks for this tab and the microphone (`getDisplayMedia` with `preferCurrentTab`, `MediaRecorder` at `VIDEO_BITS_PER_SECOND`, up to `MAX_SECONDS` 15 minutes; stopping the share stops it). **While it records the quote's costs and margin are put away** (`setPresenting` → `PRESENTING_EVENT`: the designer's Quote view shows as the Designer view), and a small bar at the foot shows the clock, Stop and **Sol's subtitles as they are spoken** (the browser's own recognition in `en-AU`, a fresh one started whenever it stops itself; each finished phrase cut into timed cues, `cuesFor`). Stopped, Sol tidies the subtitles (the quick model, no Sol credit, 60 an hour; `tidyPrompt`/`applyTidy`: punctuation, solar words misheard, figures with units, the same lines on the same times, nothing added; on any failure they stay as heard); the recording plays back with them, each line can be changed, and **Send to <customer>'s proposal** uploads it (with progress) and keeps it (`keepWalkthrough`: only a path under that deal, only a video really there; written on the deal's record). **The customer** sees the newest shown one at the top of their proposal (`WalkthroughPlayer` in the greeting slot of `CustomerProposal`): "Watch <business> walk you through your design" with its length, then the video with the subtitles as its captions track (WebVTT, `toVtt`) and `WALKTHROUGH_NOTE`; nothing once the proposal is withdrawn. Earlier recordings list under the recorder's start: watch, hide from or show on the proposal, delete (with its video). A demo must give its details first (`demoNeedsDetails`). **Needs `BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN`** (client uploads need the token, not the connected store alone); without it the recorder says recordings can't be kept. Tried through the API on the local database (the list, the refusal of another deal's path, Sol's tidy against Claude: "heres your six point six kilowatt system" → "Here's your 6.6 kW system.", the customer's route and its WebVTT); **not yet seen in a browser or recorded for real**: VERIFY a recording in Chrome and Edge (the tab prompt, the microphone, the subtitles keeping up), an upload of a few minutes to Blob, playback and seeking on the customer's page on an iPhone (Safari can't play WebM: Chrome records WebM, so the customer on an iPhone may need MP4; VERIFY and convert if so), and inside the designer's pop-up. Not yet: the business's own picture-in-picture camera, recording a part of the screen, emailing the customer a link to the walkthrough itself, and the walkthrough in the proposal's PDF.
- **The address under the map, Create deal, and Tab for Sol's example (16 Nov 2026, Martin: a new address searched from the design home had no deal, so no Record; "the address… at the bottom of the map, outside the map in the grey pad… a button to create deal… a popup… customer name, address will auto populate, email / phone not mandatory"; "press tab to actually use that text prompt, which should trigger sol's step by step question").** `AddressStrip` in address-strip.tsx, under the picture in the pop-up only (`RoofDesigner footRoom` keeps it in the first fold): the address, pressed (or its magnifier) to search another (`AddressEntry above`, its list opening upward), in place of the map pin that was in the pop-up's rail menu. With no deal on the roof, **Create deal** (signed-in retailers who see costs) opens `NewDealWindow`: first name (needed), last name, the roof's address shown, mobile and email optional; `createDealHere` in design-studio.tsx makes the customer and their first deal there (`createCustomer`, source "Design tool"), saves the design so the deal finds its roof, and tells the pop-up the deal (`tellDeal({ job })`, `adopted` in design-drawer.tsx), so Record and the proposal are there without going anywhere. With a deal, the customer's name and Open deal. **Sol's box**: its example is `examplePrompt` (design-commands.ts, tested): "Design this with me, step by step" on an empty design, else "Fit 8 kW on the north face"; Tab in the empty box takes it, and words asking to be led (`asksForGuide`) start Sol's guide from the home question instead of going to Sol. The guide's card stays over the open box in the pop-up (it was hidden while the box was open). Seen in the browser on the local example business (two deals made, each joined to its roof; Record beside the window at once; Tab and the guide).
- **The proposal builder, and custom proposals as a paid add-on (15 Nov 2026, Martin: the blocks you could rearrange and the pictures you could drop in "wasn't obvious at all"; "the proposal builder should feel like a builder… but you should have to toggle to builder"; charged per business).** `components/design/proposal-builder.tsx` (`useProposalBuilder`, `BuilderPanel`, `BlockTools`, `PictureSpot`, `BlockWords`), `domain/design/custom-proposals.ts` (tested), `server/design-addons.ts` (table `design_addons`, business id first). **Preview | Builder** sits over the preview on a deal's proposal screen (off to start, never on by itself); with Builder on, the left panel is the builder and the page is what is built (`ProposalPage` `build`, `BuildHooks`: each block, the cover and the logo framed). **Standard or Custom** (`BrandKit.design`, `PROPOSAL_DESIGNS`; `blocksForView`): standard is every block in its usual place under the template (`defaultBlocks`), custom the business's own `blocks`; a kit saved before with blocks arranged in the old customiser reads as custom (`blocksAreUsual`), and saving blocks from Settings → Proposal customiser makes it custom too. **Everyone**: the logo and cover photo dropped onto the page or picked by pressing them, the two colours and the type. **Custom**: hover a block for its tools (drag to move, up and down, heading and words, a picture, the picture left, across or right (`BLOCK_LAYOUTS`, `layout`), a dark ground for the blocks of the business's own words only (`mayGoDark`, `tone`, `darkGroundVars`), hide), drop a picture from the computer on any block, and drag **A block of your own** in from the tray (now six, `custom-1`…`custom-6`, `nextOwnBlock`, `placeBlock`). **Never allowed**: writing over a figure, taking the cover, price, going ahead or terms off, rewording the standard terms, free placement or code of their own. Every change saves to the brand kit at once (the page changes first, the server's preview catches up), so it holds for later proposals; one already sent stays as it was until published again. **The add-on**: publishing a custom design is refused (402, `CUSTOM_NEEDS_ADD_ON`) only once `CUSTOM_PROPOSALS_MONTHLY_CENTS` is set and billing is enforced, without a `design_addons` row (`mayPublishCustom`, `customProposalsAllowed`); since 16 Nov 2026 the add-on is **Growth** (`CUSTOM_PROPOSALS` is its `marketing` key, $19 a month, see Voice agents and marketing email are paid add-ons), so once billing is enforced a custom design needs Growth; `customPriceWords` says so. The PDF keeps every block stacked on the light ground (layout and tone are the page's only). Not seen in the browser (the local server's example deal can't preview: its quote has lines with no cost): VERIFY a drag, a dropped picture, a dark block and the logo drop. Not yet: buying the add-on through Stripe, AI-made pictures, layouts and dark blocks in the PDF, galleries, video, team and accreditation blocks, a dark cover, an icon beside the logo. **A block's picture is resized and moved in place** (15 Nov 2026, Martin: "i need to be able to position it and resize it"; `BlockPicture` in block-picture.tsx; `PICTURE_SIZE`, `cleanPictureSize`, `cleanPicturePlace`, `pictureAsUsual` in blocks.ts, tested in `block-picture.test.ts`): each block keeps `size` (its width as a share of its room, 20 to 100) and `x`, `y` (where it sits there, 0 to 100, 50 the middle), carried on the published copy only when not the usual full width in the middle. The picture sits by springs either side in the ratio of its place, so nothing is measured to draw it; down only tells beside the words (the room is the column's height). In the builder on a custom design (`BuildHooks.placePicture`): drag it to move, drag a lower corner to resize (the far edge stays put), arrow keys move it and + and − resize it, a double click or 0 puts it back; one save on letting go. The PDF keeps its width and place across. **More type** (15 Nov 2026, Martin: "provide more font style options"): `FONTS` in brand.ts now carries each choice's faces for headings and words (`--proposal-heading`, `--proposal-body` from `themeVars`; `fontOf`) and the PDF's (`pdfHeading`, `pdfBody`: a serif is Times there, the rest Helvetica): Modern, Editorial, **Classic** (Lora throughout), **Elegant** (Playfair Display headings), **Geometric** (Manrope), **Friendly** (Nunito) and **Technical** (Space Grotesk headings), the new faces kept in `src/app/fonts` (Latin, Google Fonts, SIL Open Font Licence) and declared in the root layout without preloading; the builder picks them from tiles each set in its own face. Seen: the font tiles in the builder with every face loaded, and the picture's resize, move, keys and double click on a test page (the local example deal can't preview); VERIFY on a real proposal preview and its PDF.
- **Needs you cards across the feed (19 Nov 2026, Martin: "i dont like that i have to scroll down to see all the information on the card in the feed on the left. i think we can occupy more width - and i think we can also reduce the font size of sols comment").** Each Needs you card on Today runs the feed's whole width, one under another (deal-feed.tsx; it was 30rem, two side by side where they fitted). - **Outlook mailboxes in the inbox (20 Nov 2026, Martin: "we should have an outlook email connector").** `domain/design/outlook-mail.ts` (tested), `server/outlook-mail.ts` (the Microsoft Graph calls), `/api/auth/outlook` and `/callback`, and `provider` on `design_gmail` (google for every row before). A member connects an Outlook mailbox as they do a Gmail (a small window over the page; `connectGmailHere(add, "outlook")`, outcomes `outlook-connected`, `outlook-failed`, `outlook-off`), up to `MAX_MAILBOXES` of either kind together. **gmail.ts is still the one mailbox layer**: each of its functions asks the mailbox's provider (`tokenFor`, `viaOutlook`) and an Outlook email is handed back in the shape of a Gmail one (`asMailShape`: headers, words, files, its own pictures fetched whole, UNREAD), so the inbox, an opened email, files, notes, sharing, Sol's summary and reply, Compose and **Send from** on a deal's email all work for either with no change. Read, unread (PATCH `isRead`) and archive (moved to the Archive folder, never deleted); sending goes through Graph's `sendMail` as the same MIME email (`rawEmail`), kept in the mailbox's Sent; the unread count adds both kinds. Every Graph call asks for immutable ids (`Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"`), so an archived email keeps its id and its notes; `isMessageId` takes Outlook's long ids, and `providerOfId` keeps a Gmail id from being asked of Outlook or the other way round. Microsoft has no revoke call: disconnecting only forgets the tokens here (the refresh token, renewed on each refresh, is kept sealed as Gmail's). Buttons: **Connect Outlook** on the inbox's connect card and **Add an Outlook** beside the mailboxes (only when Microsoft's sign-in is set up, `outlookReady`), and **Outlook** beside Gmail on the welcome's Email row. **Needs the same Microsoft Entra app as the Outlook calendar** (`MICROSOFT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET`) with the delegated Graph permissions **Mail.ReadWrite** and **Mail.Send** added and **`<site>/api/auth/outlook/callback`** as a second redirect. **The lead inbox is still Gmail only** (`businessMailboxes` lists Google mailboxes; its search is Gmail's). Tests pass and the sign-in route was tried locally (it answers that Outlook isn't set up there); **not run against Microsoft**: VERIFY with a work and a personal account the sign-in, the list, an email with a picture and a PDF, mark read, archive, and one email sent. Not yet: Outlook as the lead inbox, Outlook's Focused inbox or other folders, shared mailboxes and send-as, and a privacy policy line naming Microsoft.
- **The roof's pitch read from Nearmap's heights (10 Nov 2026, Martin: "can Nearmap tell us the roof pitch angle, rather than guessing?").** `domain/design/roof-pitch.ts` (tested): `pitchOfRoof` gathers the faces found in the heights (`facesFromHeights` inside the home's Nearmap outline) by pitch, within `ROOF_PITCH.alike` (3°), and takes the pitch holding the most roof (a garage or verandah at another pitch doesn't move it; under 3° is flat; steeper than 50° is left out; under 25 m² read says nothing), with how much of the roof agreed; `sure` from 55% of it. In roof-designer.tsx `readRoofPitch` reads it and puts nothing on the design (no faces laid, no roof check asked, so no Claude call). **By itself**: a roof on a Nearmap picture with no pitch set (`pitchSet` false) is read once a roof; when sure it sets the home's pitch (or flat) and says so beside the mark (`pitchReadWords`: the pitch, Nearmap's heights and their date, how many faces, "check it"); when not sure the pitch card (`PitchFirst`) opens on the figure with the reading as its hint and nothing is set. **Never over a pitch a person set.** **On the pitch mark's pad**: **Read it from Nearmap** reads it again and sets it whatever stood there. Only roof 1 (the home's own); only on a Nearmap picture; the heights can be from an older capture than the picture (the date is said). It changes the pitch new panels and faces start at, not panels already laid. Seen in the browser on two Rowville roofs (23° from 4 of 4 faces, and from 6 of 11); VERIFY against roofs of known pitch, and where only the coarse heights exist. Not yet: a pitch for the other roofs (a garage), and the same on Google's heights.
- **An array pivots as it is dragged out, and squares to an edge before it is let go (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "it would be great if you could pivot it as you move your mouse in a direction… the panels continue to lay in one direction"; and "releasing the mouse clicker can force a slight pivot leaving the array crooked").** `domain/design/array-pivot.ts` (`pivotDrag`, tested). With Add a panel, a panel put down **in the open** (not beside a panel, not on a traced face: `free` on the press) starts a row that runs from it towards the pointer, turning as the pointer swings; pulling sideways by more than half a row holds the direction where the row last grew and adds rows, so a block is drawn along, then down (a diagonal drag in the open now makes a turned row, not a block). The direction clicks square to standing panels, traced faces' rows and sides and guide lines within `PIVOT.snap` (4°); Alt turns freely. Beside a panel or on a traced face the drag is as before. **Squaring is shown while held**: panels being drawn or moved are turned square and pulled to the edge they are near during the drag (`settleToEdges` in the pointer move, each move worked from the angle they were picked up at), so letting go changes nothing. **A traced side within `ROWS_WITHIN` (5°) of its face's rows squares panels to the rows** (`RoofEdge.rows`), not to the side as drawn, so they match the face's other panels. Seen in the browser on the local example business (a row dragged at 20° in the open); the along-then-down block and the live squaring against a traced face not seen by hand.
- **Routines (20 Nov 2026, Martin: "an icon that is representative of routines… below the agent icons and below the + add agent icon on the floating bar… independently floating… joined by a line like a bridge"; "the routines page itself, shows all agents Voice and Text (sol) and what they have programmed into them… a really basic zapier / shopify flow type view… adjusting here should adjust actions").** **The round**: `RoutinesRound` (routines-round.tsx), a retailer's, under the floating bar in the aside (`SolRail` `routines`), held to it by a short bridge (two rails and a rung) and above team chat's round; it opens `/design/routines`. **The page** (`RoutinesPage` in routines.tsx, `GET /api/design/routines`): down the left every agent's routine (Sol · writes, Voice agents · ring, Coming soon) with Running, Drafts only or Not running; beside it the one picked as a flow on a dotted canvas: When, Checks (with "If not:" under it), Waits, Does, Hands over, Ends, joined by a line, and steps that repeat in a dashed orange box headed by how often. **Read from what really drives each agent, never a second copy** (`routinesFor` in `domain/design/routines.ts`, tested in routines.test.ts): Sol · Leads (the hello), Sol · Proposals (the follow-ups), each named voice agent (the first sales agent rings new leads up to the business's tries; others ring calls handed to them, twice; the post-sale one books installs), Sol · Designs and Won as Coming soon. **A step with a pencil changes the routine itself**: the routine settings now carry `followUpDays` (one to three, 1 to 30, `cleanFollowUpDays`), `reviewMinutes` (10, 30, 60, 120), `helloMinutes` (2, 5, 15, 30) and `callAttempts` (1 to 3) beside `on` and `hours` (`AgentSettings`, `ROUTINE_CHOICES`, `cleanAgentSettings` in sol-agents.ts; kept in `design_sol_agents` through `server/design-routine-settings.ts`, saved by PUT `/api/design/sol-agents`, switching an agent on or off still the owner's). They are used: `firesAtFor` waits `reviewMinutes`/`helloMinutes`; `dueFollowUp` takes `days` (Sol's drafts in design-prepared.ts, Proposals sent, Today's counts; `/api/design/proposals` answers `followUpDays`; a follow-up marked on any day 1 to 60); a new lead is rung `callAttempts` times (voice-calls.ts). A voice agent's trigger changes who it rings and its Does step the script it uses (through its coach route). **Locked steps say why**: calling hours (the rules for calling people at home) and the two tries of a handed-over call. Seen in the browser on the local example business (the page, the round and its bridge, Proposals' loop, a day taken away and added back, Charlie's flow); not seen with a real call or follow-up. **Live on each step (20 Nov 2026, Martin: "build the live counts on each step")**: `routineLive` (routines.ts, tested) reads by rule what each step is doing now and puts a pill on it (`LiveLine` in routines.tsx): Sol's emails counting down on the wait step ("2 waiting · next goes in 12m", `countdownWords`), waiting for a press or sent this week on the send step; a voice agent's call on now or calls lined up on its ring step (the next one's time), wanting the team to call back on the answer step, couldn't be reached on the loop, spoken to on the end; the post-sale agent's on its offer, pencil and team steps. Orange for now, amber when it waits on a person, grey for done; pressed, the deals behind it, each opening its deal. The route reads the outbox as this member may see it, read only (nothing started or sent by looking), and each voice agent's own deals (`assistantDeals`); Sol's sent emails now carry their kind (`SentBySol.kind`). Seen in the browser on the local example business (Charlie on a call with a deal); the email counts not seen there (nothing in its outbox). Not yet: a running count on the round, routines of Sol's other kinds (deposit reminders, design ready), the missed-call text and calling hours per business, adding a step that isn't there.
- **Routines you build yourself (20 Nov 2026, Martin: "go ahead with 1 and 2, keep the live pills": a routine built from scratch, picking an agent, what starts it and its steps; and steps added, taken away and put in order; routines are for setting up what the voice and text agents do, not notifications).** `domain/design/custom-routines.ts` (tested), `server/design-custom-routines.ts` (tables `design_routines`, `design_routine_runs`, business id first; `design-custom-routines-db.test.ts` runs with `npm run test:db`), POST `/api/design/routines` (`do` save or delete; who may publish builds and changes, switching one on is the owner's, never while viewing as someone), `saveOwnRoutine`/`deleteOwnRoutine` in services/routines.ts, `RoutineBuilder` in routines-builder.tsx. On the Routines page, **New routine** heads the list and **Your routines** is its first group; one opens as a builder on the same dotted canvas: its name, **Who runs it** (Sol, who writes, or a named voice agent, who rings; `stepsFor`: Sol waits, checks, emails and texts, a voice agent waits, checks and rings; changing the agent turns an email or text into a call and back, keeping its words), **What starts it** (`CustomTrigger`: a new lead comes in, or a deal reaches Designed, Proposal sent, Won, Installation scheduled or Installed), then its steps as cards, each changed in place (a wait in hours or days; a check: they haven't replied, not signed yet, still at the stage it started; an email, text or call with what it's about, up to `CUSTOM_LIMITS.about`), moved up or down, taken away, with a **plus** between any two to add one (up to `CUSTOM_LIMITS.steps` 12, `CUSTOM_LIMITS.routines` 20 a business); nothing changes until **Save**, and it can be switched on only with something to do and words for it (`routineProblem`). **Running it** (`runCustomRoutines`, from the ten-minute `/api/cron/sol-outbox`): a switched-on routine catches only deals that start it after it was switched on (`startsOn`: made after, or moved to the stage after, read from the deal's "Moved to" line), never a sample customer, once a deal; each run goes through checks and actions at once and stops at a wait not yet over. **An email** is written by Sol from what it's about (`routineWritePrompt`: the business's words fenced as data, no price, figure, date, product or promise not in them, deep tier, a Sol credit as a follow-up) and lined up in Sol's outbox (`design_prepared` kind `routine`) with the business's review time as its `notBefore`, so it counts down and goes inside the hours like his own; one Sol couldn't write waits held for a person. **A text** goes only inside the business's hours, where texts are on, to the mobile on the record, never after STOP, written by Sol (quick tier) and signed with the business's name, kept with the deal's texts, in its chat and on its record; none written, none sent. **A call** is `queueCall` with what it's about as `teamAbout` and the agent (the post-sale agent books installs), so calling hours and every call rule hold. A deal that's lost, or a check that doesn't hold, stops the run; each step's outcome is kept on the run (`log`). **Live lines** (`customLive`): each step shows which deals are at it now (a wait with when the next moves on). Seen in the browser on the local example business (a routine saved, a step added, moved up and saved in order, switched on, a voice agent's routine with its email turned into a call, a run's live line); the runner tried in the database test (Sol not there, so the emails waited held). **Not tried for real**: VERIFY one routine's email written by Sol and sent from the outbox, one text and one call on the live business. Not yet: the run's log shown anywhere, triggers beyond a new lead and a stage (a proposal gone quiet, a customer's reply), branches (a check that goes another way rather than stopping), one routine using two agents, and a copy of a built-in routine to change.
- **Sol's price check on the quote (18 Nov 2026, Martin: "is there opportunity for Sol to provide any intelligence guidance advice on pricing?", then "build all 4").** `domain/design/price-check.ts` (tested), `server/design-price-check.ts` (`pricePoints`: each proposal's signed version, else its newest, from the last `HISTORY_DAYS`; indicative ones left out), `GET /api/design/price-check` (`costs` permission), `services/price-check.ts` (`usePricePoints`, read once in five minutes), `PriceCheck` in design-quote.tsx under the totals. By rule, no Sol credit, **only from the business's own proposals, never market prices**; the deal the designer is open on is left out. (1) **The price against signed jobs this size** (`priceAgainstSigned`, `alike`: both with a battery or both without, sizes within about a third): $/W after STCs for solar alone, the price itself with a battery, against the middle and middle half of the last 20 signed; under `MIN_LIKE` (5) it says how many there are. (2) **Typed costs** (`typedCostFlags`): a cost typed over a product's line more than `TYPED_GAP` (10%) from the price list, or with none listed from what the business costed it at on its latest proposal, says so under the line with Sol's mark. (3) **Margin** (`marginCheck`): under the business's floor (Settings → Margin → **Margin floor**, `margin.floor`, a share, 0 none), the lowest sent this quarter (3 or more), and against the signed jobs' middle margin; the floor is also said over Proceed to proposal, which still goes ahead (**never enforced**). (4) **Signed against quiet** (`signedAgainstQuiet`: unsigned and out `QUIET_DAYS` (21), or the deal lost): with 5 of each this size, the price above which none signed, or the two middle halves. **Ask Sol** under it sends the check's lines to his panel to advise on (one credit as any answer). Not seen in the browser (another session's dev server held the folder): VERIFY on the live business with a few signed proposals. **On the proposal screen too** (18 Nov 2026, Martin): `ProposalStudio` shows the same card above What it is, from the preview, whose answer carries `pricing` (the margin share and the floor) only for a member who sees costs; with options it checks the recommended system. Not yet: the check in the quote's short view, and by postcode or season.
- **The quote builder prices by the unit (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "the quote builder is missing the total price… the current price field should be for the single unit qty. then the total should be the qty x by the unit price").** In `DesignQuote` (design-quote.tsx) the columns are Item, Qty, **Each ex GST** and **Total**: a counted line's box is the cost of one and its total is that times how many; a line with no count (priced by the watt, a meter, a fee) keeps one cost, shown in both. Typed, the cost each is kept as `LineEdit.each` (quote.ts; `editedLines` prices the line at each × the count as it stands, so it follows a count typed over and the design's own count; kept by `cleanExtras`; tested in `quote-each.test.ts`), and `typedCostFlags` checks it against the price list. **A cost typed before this was the whole line's** (`LineEdit.amount`) and still wins: it shows as that divided by the count each, until the unit price is typed again. Lines added by hand were already priced each and now show their total in the column. Seen in the browser on the local example business in the wide quote (125 each × 42 = $5,250; a count of 40 = $5,000). Not seen: the quote beside the picture at its narrow width, and the short view (it shows group totals only).

GST in the quote builder's totals (design-quote.tsx), **STC Solar Incentive** and **BSTC Battery Incentive** each show their certificates and dollars in green (only the ones there are), above After STCs as before. `stcSplit` in quote.ts (tested in `stc-split.test.ts`): the panels' and the battery's certificates, the battery's dollars taken as the total less the panels' so the two always add up to `stcValue`. The short view still shows After STCs alone. Not seen in the browser.

I keep running into a no prices on design 2… when I proceed to proposal on design 1").** `withHomeOf` (quote.ts, so `sharedHome` on every quote change, as the roof opens and when a proposal is priced) now also hands each line's typed cost to the same line (by `lineKey`) on the roof's other designs (`sharedCost`, tested in `shared-prices.test.ts`): the cost each (`each`), a rate a watt (`rate`), a whole cost on a line with no count (`amount` with `whole`, set by the quote builder when typed on such a line) and a line left at $0. It replaces that design's own cost for the line; its own count, a line taken out there and a cost decided for that design alone (`own`) stay; the margin, lines added by hand and a whole-line cost typed on a counted line before costs were typed each (no `whole`) are not shared. The design worked on wins: it is the one changes are shared from. Not seen in the browser: VERIFY a price typed on design 1 showing on design 2 and its proposal publishing. - **An inverter's phase from its model code, and inverter lines of one family kept apart in the quote (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "Yes, fix both", two gaps found trying the three-phase warning).** (1) `inverterPhase` (supply-match.ts, tested) reads the list's phase, then **Sungrow's own model code** in the product code or name (`phaseFromCode`: SG or SH, the size, then RT, T or CX three phase, RS single; VERIFY against Sungrow's range), then the name; the code wins over the name, so SGWSG20RT ("Multi MPPT String Solar Inverter") and SGWSH20RT (listed as "Single Phase") are marked not for a single-phase home. Other makers' codes aren't read. (2) `lineKey` (quote.ts) keeps an Inverters line's figures (they are its model, not a count; only a count in front is dropped), so a 15 kW and a 10 kW of one family are two lines: taking one out of the quote had taken both, as their names differ only by numbers. A change kept under the older key is still read (`editOf`, `legacyLineKey`: quote.ts, deal.ts, price-check.ts and design-quote.tsx read edits through it) and moves to each line's own key the first time one is changed (`withOwnKey` in the quote builder's `edit`). Tested in `quote-line-keys.test.ts`. Seen in the browser on the local example business (both 20 kW Sungrows marked; the 10 kW line taken out, the 15 kW left). Not seen: an older hand-typed inverter cost in the browser, and a proposal or stock order from a design with two same-family inverters.

The per-panel figures show only while it is open, and while stringing neither the figures nor the tints are drawn, so the strings show. The home's day left the rail (the Day button by Roof and Sides opens it). Not seen in the browser.

Asking (`withPreApprovalRequest`) sets the deal's Network pre-approval to **Requested** (a new `PRE_APPROVAL` choice, `PRE_APPROVAL_REQUESTED`), fills the Distributor when none was typed, notes it on the deal's record and tells the badges (`renuabl:approvals`); **nothing is sent to the network**. Once asked the card says where it stands, and goes once approved. **Pre-approvals now holds deals still being sold whose pre-approval is asked for** (`gateOf`: an open stage with a pre-approval Requested, Submitted or Declined; Solar Victoria and booking still wait for the sale), each row with "not sold yet" and **Network: <name>** (from the postcode: check the NMI). Not seen in the browser (another session's dev server held the folder): VERIFY on the live business. Not yet: submitting to the distributor's portal, the request from a deal page or Sol's chat.
- **A drag does one thing: add or pivot; rows come from the array's + handles (21 Nov 2026, Martin, on the note above: "i dont understand how to pivot… if i pull sideways - whichever way the panel is facing, this is when it should add panels… if i… pull in another direction, this is when it should pivot… then to add rows, this is the tricky part"; over the note above where they differ).** `pivotDrag` (array-pivot.ts, tested) settles the drag as it begins (`PIVOT.start`): started within `PIVOT.cone` (20°) of the way the panel's row runs it is **row**, adding panels with the direction fixed; started any other way it is **pivot**, the row pointing at the pointer and growing along it, clicking square to nearby panels, traced faces' rows and sides, guide lines and the way it was put down (`PIVOT.snap`, Alt frees). It holds until let go. **It pivots on a traced face too** (starting out facing the way the face runs); beside an existing panel it only adds. **A drag lays one line, never a block** (`arrayFrom` `only`: along the row when free, else whichever of across or along the pointer has gone further); pulling sideways mid-drag no longer adds rows. **Rows and columns come from handles** (`domain/design/array-grow.ts`, tested: `arrayFrame`, `growHandles`, `growCount`, `growArray`): loose panels picked that lie on one grid (the same way round and tilt, whole steps apart) show a **+** on each side (`growSpots` in roof-designer.tsx, slid along its side when it would sit on a turn handle); dragged out it copies the row or column on that side, one a step, never onto a panel standing outside the group; dragged in it takes them away, never the last (press mode `grow`; panels taken away come off their strings; one step to undo). Driven in the browser on the local example business with made pointer events: a sideways pull stayed straight (12 panels), a pull at 38° pivoted (13), and the bottom handle pulled two steps added two rows (26); **the handles not seen by eye** (the pane's picture was covered by cards): VERIFY their look, that the turn buttons under a picked array don't cover the bottom handle, and a drag by hand. Not yet: handles on panels in a traced face's own rows, and on a phone.
- **The pivot kicks in sooner, turns the way the panel faces, and a press on + adds one (21 Nov 2026, Martin: "at first it wasnt pivoting… took some aggresive movement"; "if the panel starts in the north pointing position, and i pivot 180 degrees, i would expect the pointer to face south"; "the + icons… dont do anything"; over the note above where they differ).** `pivotDrag` no longer settles row or pivot once as the drag begins: the drag is a row while the pointer stays within `PIVOT.cone` (now 12°) of the row's line and **pivots the moment it leaves it**, then goes on pivoting until let go (`PivotState.angle`). **The panel turns with the pointer from where it last was** (`nearer` against the last angle, not the angle it was put down at), so a row swung half way round faces the other way and its arrow with it; a row pulled straight out the other end still faces as it did. **A plain press on a + adds one row or column there** (`held.dragged` in the grow press; before, only a drag did anything, so a click looked dead); dragging still adds one a step or takes them away. Seen in the browser on the local example business with real clicks: the four handles on a pivoted row, a press on one adding a row (11 to 18 panels) and a drag on the end handle adding two columns. The half-turn is tested, not seen by hand.
- **The designer's dock** is centred on the picture with the comment bubble as a larger round in its middle: undo and redo to its left, the whole picture and the label to its right.
- **Tilt frames from over 5°** (18 Oct 2026, Martin; it was 10° or more): on a flat roof, panels tilted more than `TILT_FRAME_ABOVE` (5°) in `roof-design.ts` add tilt kits and the tilt frame installation in the designer's quote.
- **The designer designs itself** (18 Oct 2026, Martin: "the system to basically design itself"). **A traced face reads its own slope** (`domain/design/face-aspect.ts`, tested): when a face is closed, a plane is fitted through Google's heights inside it (`Terrain`, kept `ASPECT.edge` in from the edges, a tree or chimney set aside and fitted again), giving the way down the slope and the pitch; a face whose heights aren't one plane (a hip traced as one face, a rebuilt roof) falls back to Google's roof model face with the most spots inside (`readAspect`); then `faceFromAspect` lays the rows along the gutter, looking down the slope, at the pitch (a flat roof under 3° keeps rows along its longest edge, looking north). Only while nobody has changed the face's rows, direction or pitch; "Read it again" in the face's panel (`SlopeNote`) always applies it. Always "check it against the photo". **"Design it for me"** (the wand at the right end of the dock; `domain/design/self-design.ts`, tested): three designs for three positive outcomes (Martin), each a `RoofDesign` with `goal` (`DESIGN_GOALS` in `roof-design.ts`, kept through `cleanDesign`, so a renamed design keeps it) and its label (`SELF_DESIGN_LABELS`): **Fastest payback** (panels only, the count with the lowest payback; return as a share of outlay is the same ranking, so it isn't a design of its own), **Best return** (panels and a battery, the most dollars ahead over `RETURN_YEARS` (15, VERIFY, internal: never shown to customers, no 25-year figures) once paid for) and **Most independence** (Martin: blackout backup, so it always differs from the best return: the whole roof (every traced face; of Google's model, the well-sunned spots) as far as an inverter takes it, a battery holding the whole of the evenings plus one module more held back for blackouts, `DesignBattery.backup`). Panels go on the traced faces (sunniest face first, places switched off stay off), else Google's roof model (`suggestPanels`); every count is priced as the quote does (`systemSpecOf` → `costSystem` − `stcValue`) and set against the bill (`designFinance`); inverter by `suggestInverters` on the quote's phase (hybrid with a battery), battery by `suggestBatteries`, strings by `autoStringDesign` (or a "Not strung" note when limits aren't on file). Two outcomes that come out as the same system are made once, with a note. Without a bill it sizes to `NO_BILL_KW` and says so; a home with small evenings gets a battery only in the most independence. **Blackout backup** on any battery (the battery menu's "Blackout backup" tick, or the most independence): the quote adds "Backup circuits for blackouts" (`InstallRates.backup`, a placeholder $1,000 a business sets in its rates) and the held module isn't counted in the savings (`shiftingKwhOf` in `system.ts`, used by the bill panel and the self-design; the circuits themselves are agreed with the customer). The designs replace the roof's (asked first; traced faces kept), the best return is the primary, and a card lists each with what the customer pays after STCs, the yearly saving, payback and why (the "why" is for the business, never the customer's proposal). Off for a member who can't see costs. Every figure is an estimate. **Backup holds nothing back; a module kept for blackouts is its own option (19 Nov 2026, Martin: a proposal's Option 3, 5.22 kW with an 8 kWh battery and blackout backup, said "Saves about $600 a year" and 19.9 years beside the same system without backup at $1,150 and 9.4; then "reserving one whole module for backup is crazy. however this should be built in as an option. add an extra module, and keep that for backup - but only offered on backup systems"; over what these notes say elsewhere about backup holding a module back)**: `DesignBattery.backup` is the wiring alone (the backup circuits on the quote); the whole battery still runs the evenings. `DesignBattery.reserve` (kept by `cleanDesign` only with backup and two or more modules) says one module more was added and is kept for blackouts: `heldModulesOf` and `shiftingKwhOf` (system.ts, tested in self-design.test.ts) leave only that one out of the savings. **In the designer** the battery menu's Blackout backup tick has a second tick under it, shown only while backup is on, **Add a module kept for blackouts** (adds a module and marks it; unticking it, or backup, takes the module off again). Design it for me's Most independence and the calculator's With backup set it, as they add the module. **In the configurator** (`withBackup` is the wiring only, `withReserve` the module more; `ConfigPick.reserve`, the key's last digit 2, `ProposalOption.reserve`) Blackout backup no longer adds a module, and **Extra module for blackouts** shows under it only once backup is on; each is priced at publish. The battery drawing and the Size cards say when a module is kept ("Backup + module"). **A proposal already published keeps the figures and mixes it was published with (its backup mixes still carry the old held module): publish it again.** Tests pass; the configurator seen in the browser on an older local proposal (the new wording, no kept-module switch as that proposal has no such mix); the designer's second tick and a republished proposal not seen.
- **The quote's postcode is the address's.** There is no postcode field in the quote builder: the designer passes the postcode found in the roof's address (`postcodeIn`) over whatever a saved quote holds.
- The roof designer has its own look (`.theme-studio` in `globals.css`, 10 Oct 2026, Martin: "elite software", like Tesla or NASA, without looking like either): white and cool greys, near-black ink, hairlines, small corner radii, figures in JetBrains Mono (every `tabular-nums` and number box inside the theme), and one signal colour, a solar orange (`--sun-line` #FF5A1F), kept for what is live or picked: the drawing on the roof, the figure that matters, focus. It swaps the same semantic tokens (forest and sage become graphite and pale grey there), so design components use tokens only, never raw colours. The homeowner site keeps the brand sheet above.
- Logo: the plain RENUABL wordmark (`Wordmark`), no tagline underneath (tried "HOME & ENERGY" and removed it: too busy).
- Type: Inter for UI (headlines large, regular weight, tight tracking), Source Serif for editorial lines ("Cleaner homes. Lower bills."), Caveat script for handwritten accents (`<Script>`).
- Voice: clear, reassuring, human, optimistic, modern, built for everyday people.
- Mascot and photography: `src/components/ui/brand-art.tsx`. "About your home" uses the battery mascot (`pose="battery"`, image B), still: a bounce moved the image past its page-coloured backing and showed a white edge. Files in `public/brand/` are crops from the mockups (placeholders); replace them with the original renders and licensed photos under the same names, then clear `.next/cache/images` locally.
- Light artwork uses `.art-blend` (multiply + feathered edges) so it sits on the page colour without a box Multiply needs a backdrop in the same layer: if its container is `sticky`, `fixed` or otherwise isolated, give the container `bg-canvas` or a white box appears.

## Real businesses and claims

- "Setting up your plan" (`/start/analysing`, `src/lib/domain/sources.ts`) is headed "How we're working out your energy plan." and ticks off our trusted sources: Google Maps (only for Google addresses), NASA POWER (only once its sunshine arrives; otherwise "Local sunshine averages"), the Clean Energy Regulator and Solar Victoria (VIC only). A tick means that data has arrived. Never "exact", "precise" or "guaranteed" (tested). Text only, no third-party logos.

- Primero Electric & Solar is the installer of choice in Victoria. Only show figures that are real and attributed (its Google rating: 4.7 from 141 reviews, as supplied). Never invent ratings, review counts, install numbers or testimonials for a real business: `verifiedStats`/`reviewSource` on `Installer` gate what customers see.
- RENUABL gives no workmanship guarantee and doesn't guarantee installation work: the installation partner warrants their workmanship, and products carry their manufacturers' warranties. Never say otherwise (support FAQ, Revo's facts, guides).
- RENUABL has no customer reviews yet: don't show ratings, review counts, "homes powered" or testimonials for RENUABL until there are real, verifiable figures (Australian Consumer Law).

## Launch market

- Victoria first. Address search covers all of Australia; picking an address outside the launch market stops there with a friendly card (`outsideMarketMessage` in `domain/address.ts`, tested: "Sorry, we're not servicing <suburb> at this time.", then that we install in Victoria for now), in the home page and the guided brief alike. Use `LAUNCH_MARKET`, `todayInMarket()` and `marketDateTime()` from `src/lib/domain/market.ts` instead of hard-coding a state, postcode, time zone or the server's local date (Vercel runs in UTC).

## Architecture rules

- Business logic lives in `src/lib/domain` (pure TS, unit tested). Don't put it in components.
- UI reads and writes only through `src/lib/services/*`; mock data stays in `src/lib/mock`.
- Use the semantic color tokens (`bg-canvas`, `bg-surface`, `text-ink`, `text-muted`, `border-line`, `text-positive`, …) rather than raw colors so both themes work.
- `Card` drops its white `bg-surface` when its className has its own `bg-…` (otherwise the white wins and e.g. white text on `bg-forest` disappears).
- `cn` only joins classes (no tailwind-merge): a `className` can't override a component's own display or colour classes, so wrap it (e.g. a `hidden lg:block` div) instead. Disabled buttons use solid muted colours, never transparency.
- Small text links and controls get a ~44px touch area with the `tap-area` utility (in `globals.css`). The mobile menu (`MobileMenu`) offers "Start with your address" and "My RENUABL", plus a quieter "Become a partner" (for installers and retailers). The home page header also has "For solar businesses" (to `/business`, RENUABL Business), home page only, never mid-purchase. In preview, partner sign-up offers a sample certificate of currency (`public/samples/`) for testing; the live server rejects it.
- Typing boxes are at least 16px on touch screens (`@media (pointer: coarse)` in `globals.css`): smaller text makes iPhone zoom in on tap and stay zoomed, so the page slides sideways. Keep it that way for any new field.
- Pages never scroll sideways on phones: `html, body` have `overflow-x: clip` (clip, not hidden, so sticky headers keep working), and decorations that hang past the edge (the home hero's `.sun-glow`) sit in an `overflow-x-clip` container. Check new pages at 390 px: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` must equal the screen width.
- Mobile-first, but build deliberate desktop layouts (see `FlowStep`, `InstallerShell`) rather than stretching mobile.
- Don't put `position: fixed` elements inside an animated (`transform`) ancestor, because it breaks fixed positioning. `FlowStep` renders its sticky CTA outside the animated section for this reason.
- Server pages that depend on "today" call `await connection()` so they render per request.

## Checks before committing

```bash
npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test && npm run format:check && npm run build
```
